The backlog phrase describes the expected *use case*, not a technical gate this app enforces anywhere else for a comparable relationship -- and a real gate would need to walk shared (including past `'left'`) campaign membership history for a privacy benefit that doesn't actually exist here, since every event this feed can show is already fully public on the followed user's own `/players/[id]` page regardless of who follows them. **The load-bearing design decision, and the reason the feed's scope reads narrower than a literal reading of "session-log posts":** session log entries are private to a campaign's active party (`hasPrivateAccess` in `lib/access.ts`, with existing unit + HTTP-integration regression tests proving strangers/pending requesters/left members/signed-out visitors can't see them) -- a feed literally surfacing "session-log posts" for a non-member follower would be a real regression against that already-tested boundary, not a feature, so this feed deliberately never touches `session_log_entries`, `party_notes`, `campaign_messages`, or the DM-only `initiative_entries`/`npc_notes` tables at all. Instead, a new `feed_events` table (backed by `lib/feed.ts`) is written to at the exact moment one of four already-public events happens: a character is created, a character's status transitions into `retired`/`fallen` (not on other edits, and not on a transition back to `active`), a DM's campaign transitions from accepting requests to full, or a campaign receives its first-ever public rating (not every subsequent rating, to avoid noise) -- hooked directly into `lib/characters.ts`'s `createCharacter`/`updateCharacter`, `lib/memberships.ts`'s `approveRequest`, and `lib/campaignRatings.ts`'s `rateCampaign`. Every one of these four facts is already visible to anyone on the actor's `/players/[id]` page or the campaign's own public detail page today -- `feed_events` is a browsing *view* over already-public facts, not a new kind of content, exactly matching the backlog line's own "no new content types, just a new way to surface existing public ones" framing. An explicit event log (written at the transition) was chosen over deriving events by querying mutable rows after the fact, since e.g. a character's `updated_at` bumps on every edit (not just a status change), so it can't double as a reliable "this is when the milestone happened" timestamp the way an immutable per-row table like `session_log_entries` can be read directly. One data-integrity wrinkle handled explicitly: `feed_events.character_id` references `characters(id)`, but characters can be genuinely deleted (unlike campaigns, which are only ever soft-cancelled) -- `deleteCharacter` now nulls out any referencing `feed_events.character_id` before deleting the row, preserving the event (its message already has the character's name baked in as plain text) rather than hitting a foreign-key error or silently cascading the event away. 345 -> 361 tests, all green (`__tests__/unit/feed.test.ts`, 16 new tests: following/unfollowing, self-follow and duplicate-follow rejection, a nonexistent-user follow rejection, unfollow's idempotency, per-user follower/following counts and id listing, a followed user's new character surfacing in the feed, a character's retired/fallen transition surfacing but a plain edit and an active-transition-back not, a campaign's open-to-full transition surfacing exactly once, a campaign's first rating surfacing but a second rating not producing a duplicate event, strict follow-only + newest-first + pagination correctness, an empty feed for a user following nobody, unfollowing removing past events from the feed, character deletion nulling the event's `character_id` without breaking the FK or losing the event, and -- the explicit privacy-boundary regression test -- a DM posting a real session log entry to a campaign the viewer/follower has no membership in producing *zero* feed events and an empty feed, proving session log content can never leak through this feature). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean. Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db: self-follow rejected (400), following a user succeeds (201) and following them again is rejected (400) with a clear message, a signed-out follow attempt 401s, a followed user's new character immediately appeared in the follower's `/feed` while an unfollowed third user's new character never did, a signed-out visit to `/feed` showed a sign-in prompt instead of erroring, unfollowing removed the previously-visible event from the feed, and the target's `/players/[id]` page correctly reverted its button from "Following" back to "Follow" -- server and temp db/cookie files cleaned up afterward.

**2026-09-07 update (live session, continued): backlog item #39 (attendance-based reliability signal) is DONE, scoped as a real "mark attendance" affordance (option (a) from the two paths this item's own framing raised), not a proxy derived from RSVP data.** Before building anything, this item needed the same kind of data-honesty gut-check backlog #18 already ran into: #39's own backlog line says it "depends on #35 producing real attendance data," but #35's RSVP (confirmed/declined) is *intent* to attend the next session, not a record that anyone actually showed up -- and it isn't even durable history, since `lib/schedule.ts`'s `updateSchedule()` wipes every `session_rsvps` row for a campaign the instant `next_session_at` changes (see that table's comment in `lib/db.ts`), so there's no persisted RSVP trail left to mine after the fact once a session has come and gone. Deriving a "reliability" stat from RSVP data alone would have meant either fabricating a number from intent-not-attendance (the exact mistake #18 avoided by dropping "hours at the table" outright rather than inventing a fake duration), or silently reading `null` forever once a session passed and its RSVPs got cleared -- neither is honest. So this pass took the other path instead: a new `session_log_attendance` table (`entry_id`, `user_id`, `attended`) lets the DM optionally record, per session log entry, which of the campaign's currently-active non-DM party members actually showed up -- session log entries are the only durable, immutable per-session record this app has, so honest attendance has to hang off *that*, not off RSVPs. No row for a (entry, user) pair means "not recorded," never "recorded absent," so an entry the DM never marks doesn't silently count against anyone. `lib/attendance.ts` is the new business logic: `attendanceCandidates()` (the active party minus the DM -- a DM is presumed present for every entry they themselves post, so this signal is deliberately player-only), `listEntryAttendance()`/`setEntryAttendance()` (DM-only read/write, full-replace on save mirroring `lib/availability.ts`'s weekly-grid convention), and `getAttendanceStats()` (the aggregate `{ recorded, attended, rate }` read, `rate: null` -- not `0%` -- when `recorded` is 0, matching this app's established unrated-not-zero convention from `lib/ratings.ts`). Wired into `SessionLogPanel`: posting a new entry has an optional "Record who attended this session" checklist (defaults everyone checked, DM unchecks absentees) submitted in the same request, and each existing entry gets a DM-only "Attendance" edit affordance to fix or fill in later. Per-entry detail ("who missed this specific session") stays DM-only to view -- more pointed than this app wants to surface to the whole table -- but the aggregate rate is shown publicly on `/players/[id]` right next to the existing DM/player reputation lines, satisfying the original prototype's own "sub reliability rating requested: 4+*" use case without exposing which specific sessions someone skipped. **Known limitation, documented rather than glossed over:** attendance-marking candidates are computed from the *current* active party, not from who was actually approved back when an entry was first logged (this app has no point-in-time membership history to reconstruct that, the same category of gap as backlog #19's chronicle only counting currently-linked characters) -- in practice this only matters when editing attendance on an old entry well after the roster has changed, since marking it at the moment the entry is first posted (the common path) reflects the real party at the time. 361 -> 375 tests, all green (`__tests__/unit/attendance.test.ts`, 14 new tests: active-party-minus-DM candidate computation, a brand-new user reading as no-data rather than 0%, every candidate defaulting to `null` (not recorded) before the DM marks anything, recording attendance reflected both per-entry and in the aggregate stat, a second save fully replacing rather than merging the previous record, averaging across multiple entries, an entry the DM never marks not counting against anyone, rejecting a non-DM active member and a stranger from recording attendance, rejecting a userId that isn't a current candidate (including the DM themselves), rejecting an unknown entry id for both listing and setting, a departed member dropping out of the candidate list while keeping their historical recorded attendance, per-user isolation, and de-duplicating a repeated userId in one submission). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean (one pre-existing, unrelated `LayoutProps` TypeScript error in `app/layout.tsx` was confirmed present on `main` before this change too, via `git stash`, and is not something this pass introduced or is responsible for fixing). Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db: DM posted a session log entry with attendance attached (Alice attended, Bob didn't) in one request, confirmed the DM's own `GET .../attendance` shows the correct per-entry breakdown, confirmed a non-DM party member gets a 403 on that same endpoint, confirmed Alice's public `/players/[id]` page renders "Attendance (as player) 100% (1/1 logged session)" and Bob's renders "0% (0/1 logged session)", used the DM-only PUT to flip Bob's attendance to attended and confirmed his rate updated to 100%, confirmed a second entry posted with no attendance attached left Alice's rate unchanged (didn't silently count as a miss), confirmed submitting attendance for a userId with no relationship to the campaign 400s, and confirmed a brand-new signed-up user with no attendance history at all renders "No attendance data yet" -- server and temp db/cookie files cleaned up afterward.

**2026-09-02 update: reviewed the original product-design chat and cloud HTML prototype.** The owner shared the Claude.ai chat that produced the original concept (a swipe-to-match, social-network-styled D&D/tabletop app called "Session Zero," system-agnostic, freemium) plus the resulting clickable HTML prototype (`session-zero (4).html`). That prototype is a static mockup with no backend — it never persisted data, authenticated anyone, or talked to a server. It exists to establish the target feature set and visual/product direction; this repo is the real, functioning implementation, built from scratch and currently ahead of the prototype on the campaign-lifecycle/private-side/notification features (none of which the prototype had — it only mocked a swipe deck, a ratings widget, a logbook feed, character legacies, and a sub-request flow, all with fake, hardcoded data and no real accounts). See backlog items 15-24 below for the gap analysis and feasibility read on each prototype feature, now folded into this repo's plan of record.

What the app does today
Session Zero is a Next.js + TypeScript + SQLite app for finding a D&D (or other tabletop) group and tracking a campaign once you're in one.
Real authentication (backlog #2 — done 2026-09-02): a tabbed sign-in/create-account page (`/signin`) backed by `app/api/auth/signup/route.ts` and `app/api/auth/signin/route.ts`. Accounts are stored with a `bcryptjs`-hashed password (`bcrypt.hashSync(password, 10)`); `lib/auth.ts`'s `signUp()` validates a non-blank display name, a password of at least 8 characters, and a not-already-taken (case/whitespace-normalized) email before creating the account, while `signIn()` looks the account up by normalized email and rejects with `AuthError` on an unknown email or a `bcrypt.compareSync` mismatch — the same generic rejection either way, so sign-in doesn't reveal whether an email is registered. Session cookies/tokens are unchanged from the placeholder scheme (`createSession`/`getUserBySessionToken`/`destroySession`); every user-lookup query now explicitly selects columns and excludes `password_hash`, so a hash can never leak into an API response or a rendered page.
Once signed in, a DM can create a campaign (title, description, system, capacity) and players browse/filter (exact `system` match)/sort/paginate the list and request to join. The DM approves or declines requests; the party auto-fills (closes to new requests) once capacity is hit and auto-reopens if someone leaves, or the DM can manually reopen a full campaign to accept more requests without anyone leaving first. The DM can also cancel/un-cancel a campaign (notifying the active party on cancel) and edit its details after posting (capacity can't be dropped below the current approved headcount — validated).
An approved, non-DM member can leave a campaign (with a confirm step in the UI). Leaving marks their membership `left`, re-runs the auto-reopen-if-under-capacity logic, and immediately revokes their access to the campaign's private side.
Once approved, players and the DM get access to the private side of a campaign: a session log the DM can post to, edit, or delete; a next-session date (stored as a UTC instant, with a computed unscheduled/upcoming/past-due status, compared in UTC so it's timezone-safe); and shared party notes (DM + active members can edit). All of this is hidden from strangers, pending requesters, left members, and signed-out visitors — this boundary has explicit regression tests at both the unit layer (`hasPrivateAccess`) and the HTTP-integration layer (driving a real running server with independent per-role cookie jars, checking both API status codes and that the rendered HTML doesn't leak private content to unauthorized viewers).
Everyone gets in-app notifications for the things that affect them, visible in an inbox page (`/notifications`) with an unread badge in the nav bar (polled every 15s — see backlog #6e for the live-push follow-up). The notification types the app defines (`lib/types.ts`): `join_requested`, `join_approved`, `join_declined`, `member_left_dm`, `member_left_party`, `party_notes_updated`, `session_log_posted`, `schedule_updated`, `campaign_cancelled`.
Notification completeness (6a/6b): leaving a campaign notifies the rest of the active party (DM + other approved/active members), not just the DM — `leaveCampaign()` fans a `member_left_party` notification out to every other approved/active member (DM and the leaver excluded; the DM gets their own separate `member_left_dm` notification). Editing the shared party notes notifies the rest of the active party symmetrically (notes are editable by DM + active members — a deliberate judgment call carried over from earlier sessions — so whoever edits notifies everyone else in the active party except themselves).
Per-notification-type preferences (6d): a signed-in user can mute any individual notification type from a settings page (`/settings/notifications`) so that type is never created for them again.
* A `notification_preferences` table keyed by `(user_id, type)` with an `enabled` boolean, defaulting to enabled for every type when no row exists.
* Enforcement lives in `lib/notifications.ts`'s `notify()`/`notifyMany()` — the single choke point every fan-out path goes through. A muted type is checked before the row is ever inserted, so nothing is written for that user (as opposed to writing it and filtering at read time) — the unread badge count and the inbox list are correct for a muted user with zero extra filtering logic anywhere else, at the cost of not being able to later "unmute retroactively" and see history for the period it was muted.
* `app/api/notifications/preferences/route.ts` (GET the full map + human labels, PUT to toggle one type) is the server-side surface; `app/settings/notifications/page.tsx` is the toggle UI.
Live notification push (6e — done 2026-09-02 nightly build): the unread-badge count in `NavBar` no longer relies solely on a 15s poll. `lib/notificationEvents.ts` is a single in-process `EventEmitter`-based pub/sub (one channel per user id); `notify()`, `markRead()`, and `markAllRead()` in `lib/notifications.ts` all publish the user's fresh unread count to it after writing. `app/api/notifications/stream/route.ts` is a `GET` SSE endpoint (auth required, same `requireUser()` as the other notification routes) that sends an immediate unread-count snapshot on connect, then forwards every subsequent publish for that user as an `event: unread` SSE message, plus a comment-line heartbeat every 20s to keep proxies from timing the connection out. `NavBar.tsx` opens an `EventSource` to that endpoint and updates the badge from each `unread` event; if `EventSource` is unavailable (SSR, or a browser/proxy that doesn't support it) or the connection errors out, it falls back to the original 15s-interval polling for that mount (no reconnect/backoff logic on the SSE side — a deliberate scope cut, see session log).
Character profiles (backlog #15 — done 2026-09-06, nightly build): a signed-in user can create, edit, and delete lightweight characters at `/profile` — a name, a free-text class/ancestry/role field (kept system-agnostic, matching this app's existing "preferred systems" approach), a bio, a longer backstory, and a portrait chosen from a curated set of emoji (there's no image-upload infrastructure, so this is a deliberately lightweight placeholder-art system rather than real avatar uploads). Every character a user has created shows up read-only on their public `/players/[id]` page, alongside their profile bio/systems/availability.

Direct messaging (backlog #31 — done 2026-09-07, live session): any signed-in user can send another signed-in user a private message via `/messages` (the inbox: one row per conversation partner, most-recently-active first, with an unread badge per conversation) and `/messages/[userId]` (the full thread plus a composer). No prerequisite relationship is required to message someone — matching the openness already established for campaign browsing, the sub-request volunteer pool, and public player profiles. A "Message" link appears on every other user's `/players/[id]` page. A new message fires a `message_received` notification (through the existing `NOTIFICATION_TYPES`/`notify()` machinery) carrying a new `related_user_id` field on the notification row — a second, non-campaign link target for notification types like this one that point at a conversation rather than a campaign.

Table group chat (backlog #32 — done 2026-09-07, live session): a campaign's DM and approved active members get a group chat thread on the campaign detail page, gated behind the same `hasPrivateAccess` boundary as party notes and the session log — distinct from both 1:1 direct messages (backlog #31, a different table scoped to a user pair) and party notes (a persistent shared document rather than a conversation history). A new `campaign_chat_message` notification type fires, but only for the first unread message since a recipient's last visit to that campaign's chat, not on every message.

DM dashboard basics (backlog #33 — done 2026-09-07, live session): a campaign's DM gets an initiative tracker (a per-campaign ordered list of combatant entries — name, initiative value, free-text HP, free-text notes — add/edit/move-up/move-down/remove/clear-all) and NPC quick-notes (a per-campaign list of name + free-text notes entries, add/edit/delete) on the campaign detail page, right below `DmControls`. Both are DM-only — not shown to players, unlike party notes/the session log/schedule — since they're prep/running tools for the DM's own use, and both persist as "current state" between requests until the DM explicitly clears/deletes entries, matching how party notes already persist as a living document.

Resource vault, phase 1 (backlog #34 — done 2026-09-07, live session): a campaign's active party (DM + approved members) can upload files to a per-campaign resource vault on the campaign detail page — maps, handouts, homebrew notes — as images (PNG/JPEG/WEBP/GIF), PDFs, or plain text, capped at roughly 4MB each, stored as base64 `data:` URLs directly in SQLite (the same no-new-infrastructure pattern already used for character portraits, #26). Visible to the same `hasPrivateAccess` audience as party notes/session log/table chat; editing/deleting a resource is further restricted to its uploader or the DM. The cross-community library browsable by system (the backlog line's own "larger second phase") is explicitly not built yet — it needs the system-hub browsing surface from backlog #36 to make sense as a destination.

Session RSVP + reminders (backlog #35 — done 2026-09-07, live session): once a campaign has a `next_session_at` set, the DM and approved active members can confirm or decline on the campaign detail page (`session_rsvps` table, one row per member, no row = no answer yet), visible to the whole active party as a "who's in" summary. Rescheduling to a different date clears every existing RSVP (an answer for a date that's no longer scheduled is meaningless); RSVPing is blocked while nothing is scheduled yet. A `session_reminder` notification fires once per (campaign, user, exact `next_session_at` value) when that date is within 24h and still upcoming, checked lazily off real user activity (`GET /api/notifications` and the campaign detail page load) since this app has no background worker/cron — known limitation: a reminder only fires if someone actually triggers that check by using the app before the session happens, documented in `lib/sessionReminders.ts` and the dated session log entry below.

Curated system hubs (backlog #36 — done 2026-09-07, live session): `/systems` shows a curated `as const` list of six well-known tabletop systems (D&D 5e, Pathfinder 2e, Call of Cthulhu, Vampire: The Masquerade, Blades in the Dark, Powered by the Apocalypse) with descriptions, each linking to its own `/systems/[slug]` hub page listing campaigns whose free-text `system` field case-insensitively matches that system's name or curated aliases. `campaigns.system` stays free text — the hub is a browsing layer over it, not a reclassification — and an explicit "Other/homebrew" link on `/systems` (and every hub page) sends anyone whose system isn't curated to the ordinary `/campaigns` browse+search page rather than a dead end.

Community discussion boards (backlog #37 — done 2026-09-07, live session): `/boards` shows a curated `as const` list of four topic boards (New Player Questions, Homebrew & House Rules, Looking for Group Advice, Local Meetups & In-Person Games), each with flat threads and flat replies at `/boards/[slug]` and `/boards/[slug]/[threadId]`. Browsing is public; starting a thread or replying requires sign-in. "Light moderation" means self-moderation only for this pass — a thread or reply's own author can delete it (deleting a thread cascades to its replies) — with no edit affordance and, notably, no admin/reporting system of any kind yet; that gap is explicit, not an oversight. The concrete first slice of backlog #25, which is now marked substantially addressed rather than fully closed (see the backlog list below).

Follow + activity feed (backlog #38 — done 2026-09-07, live session): any signed-in user can follow another (a Follow/Unfollow toggle plus follower/following counts on `/players/[id]`) and see a `/feed` page of recent public events from people they follow — a character being created, a character reaching retired/fallen status, a DM's campaign filling up, or a campaign receiving its first public rating — newest first, paginated. Following has no shared-campaign-membership gate (matching backlog #31's messaging precedent); the feed itself deliberately never touches session log entries, party notes, table chat, or DM-only tools (all still gated by `hasPrivateAccess`/`isDm`) — every event type it can show is already fully public on the actor's own `/players/[id]` or the campaign's own detail page today, so this is a browsing layer over existing public facts, not new exposure.

Attendance-based reliability signal (backlog #39 — done 2026-09-07, live session): the DM can optionally record, per session log entry, which of the campaign's currently-active non-DM party members actually showed up (`session_log_attendance` table, full-replace on save, DM-only to view or edit per entry). Deliberately NOT derived from backlog #35's RSVP data — an RSVP is intent to attend, not a durable record that anyone actually did, and RSVP rows don't even survive a reschedule. The resulting aggregate "shows up" rate (`recorded`/`attended`/`rate`, `rate: null` — not 0% — with no recorded history) is shown publicly on `/players/[id]` next to the existing DM/player reputation lines; per-entry detail (who missed which specific session) stays DM-only.

New-player onboarding path (backlog #40 — done 2026-09-07, live session): a signed-in user can flag their own profile "new to tabletop" on `/profile`, and a DM can flag their own campaign "new-player friendly" on `/campaigns/new` or via the edit form on the campaign detail page — both plain booleans following the exact `danger_level`/`location` convention (shown on the campaign detail header and browse/discover cards, filterable on `/campaigns` via a checkbox threaded through the same query-param/pagination-preservation pattern as the existing filters). A signed-in viewer whose own profile is flagged "new to tabletop" sees a small opt-in nudge on `/campaigns` suggesting the new-player-friendly filter, rather than it being silently pre-applied. A public "New to tabletop" badge renders on `/players/[id]`.

In-person-first discovery, phase 1 (backlog #41 — phase 1 done 2026-09-07, live session): campaigns gained a structural `session_format` (`in_person`/`remote`/`hybrid`/unset), settable on `/campaigns/new` and `DmControls`'s edit form and shown as a badge on the campaign detail header, browse cards, and the Discover deck; profiles gained the symmetric, purely-informational `session_format_preference` (`in_person`/`remote`/`either`/unset) shown on `/players/[id]`. `listCampaigns()` now ranks in-person campaigns ahead of hybrid ahead of remote/unset by default (a `CASE`-based re-sort ahead of whatever newest/oldest/title order was already requested), applying automatically everywhere it's called — `/campaigns`' list and Discover views, and the curated system hubs — without excluding remote games, which stay findable both in the (lower-ranked) default view and via a new opt-in `sessionFormat` exact-match filter. Real geocoded "near me" distance search remains phase 2, explicitly blocked on a maps/geocoding provider API key this environment doesn't have — the same category of blocker as #6c (email/SMTP).

Sub requests carry a specific date/time and location (backlog #29 — done 2026-09-07, live session): posting a "looking for a sub" request (backlog #20) can now include an optional specific date/time it's needed for (`sub_requests.needed_at`, a nullable ISO-8601 timestamp mirroring `campaigns.next_session_at`, judged upcoming/past-due by the same `computeScheduleStatus()` used for a campaign's own schedule) and an optional location override (`sub_requests.location`, nullable — null means "use the campaign's own location"). Both the campaign-page panel and the app-wide `/subs` pool show the specific date/time and the effective location (the override if one was given, otherwise the campaign's own location, labeled "campaign default") distinctly from the free-text note.

Campaign starting level + tone tags (backlog #30 — done 2026-09-07, live session): a DM can optionally set a free-text starting level/rank on their campaign (`campaigns.starting_level`, e.g. "Level 3" or "Tier 2" or a narrative milestone -- deliberately not a rigid integer, since systems vary too much in how they express this) and a curated multi-select set of tone/style tags (`campaigns.tone_tags`, from a closed `CAMPAIGN_TONE_TAGS` vocabulary -- horror, heavy-combat, roleplay-focused, political-intrigue, comedic, mystery-investigation, exploration, one-shot-friendly -- since a table can genuinely be more than one of these at once). Both are settable on `/campaigns/new` and `DmControls`'s edit form, shown on the campaign detail header, browse/discover cards, and the curated system hub pages, and `tone_tags` is filterable on `/campaigns` via an any-of match (matching a campaign that carries at least one of the selected tags).

Board reply notifications (backlog #43, self-identified — done 2026-09-07, live session): a discussion board thread's own author is now notified (`board_reply`, via the existing `NOTIFICATION_TYPES`/`notify()` machinery) whenever someone else replies to it — closing the gap backlog #37's own session log entry flagged (posting a thread gave no way to know anyone had replied). Notifications gained a third link target, `related_thread_id` (nullable, alongside `campaign_id`/`related_user_id`), and `GET /api/notifications` looks up the thread's board slug at read time so `/notifications` can render a "View thread" link. No self-notification on replying to your own thread; a since-deleted thread's notification keeps its message text (which already names the thread by title) even after `related_thread_id` is nulled out.

Dedicated messages unread badge (backlog #44 — done 2026-09-07, live session): the NavBar's "Messages" link now shows its own live unread-count badge, entirely separate from the "Notifications" badge above it — previously a new direct message only ever bumped the general notifications count, with `/messages` itself carrying no unread indicator of its own in the nav. Backed by a new `getTotalUnreadMessageCount()` (`lib/messages.ts`, summing unread rows straight off the `messages` table) and a dedicated SSE stream (`GET /api/messages/stream`, `lib/messageEvents.ts` — a small parallel pub/sub mirroring `lib/notificationEvents.ts`), with the identical EventSource-with-15s-polling-fallback pattern the notifications badge already used.

Table chat unread badge (backlog #45 — done 2026-09-07, live session): a party member can now tell there are unread messages in a campaign's table chat, closing the gap #44's own session log entry flagged. A new `getTotalUnreadCampaignMessageCountForUser()` (`lib/campaignMessages.ts`) is pushed live over `GET /api/campaigns/chat/stream` (`lib/campaignChatEvents.ts` — a third small parallel pub/sub, mirroring `lib/messageEvents.ts`/`lib/notificationEvents.ts`) and surfaces as a badge on the NavBar's profile link (no dedicated "my tables" nav link exists to hang it on instead), plus a per-campaign badge next to each campaign in `/profile`'s "My campaigns" list (from a new `unreadCampaignChatCounts` map on `GET /api/profile`), plus a one-time server-computed "N new" snapshot next to the "Table chat" heading on the campaign detail page itself — the panel's own client-side fetch immediately marks the thread read, so a live badge there couldn't stay accurate the way a page-load snapshot can.

Calendar export (backlog #46, self-identified -- done 2026-09-07, live session): a campaign's DM and approved active members can now download a real `.ics` calendar file for the campaign's currently scheduled next session, via an "Add to calendar (.ics)" link next to the schedule status on the campaign detail page (visible whenever `next_session_at` is set). The file is a hand-built RFC 5545 document (`lib/calendarExport.ts`, no `ics` npm package needed) served from a new `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/calendar` route with a real `text/calendar` `Content-Disposition: attachment` response -- gated on the same `hasPrivateAccess` boundary as the RSVP panel, since `next_session_at` is part of this app's private-side trio even though the raw campaign GET route leaks the field itself. The event's DESCRIPTION includes the downloading viewer's own current RSVP status, and its DTEND assumes a fixed 3-hour session length since this app has no real per-campaign duration data to derive one from.

Sub-request pool sort/filter (backlog #51, self-identified -- done 2026-09-08, live session): the app-wide "looking for a sub" pool at `/subs` now defaults to soonest-needed-first ordering with past-due requests hidden (both explicit opt-outs via a sort-select and a "Show past-due requests" checkbox), rather than the original undifferentiated newest-posted-first list -- `needed_at`/`neededAtStatus` (backlog #29) existed on every request already but were never used for ordering or filtering. The campaign-page panel is unchanged; a single campaign's own request list stays small enough not to need this.

Cross-board search (backlog #52, self-identified -- done 2026-09-08, live session): a new `/boards/search` page (linked from `/boards`) and `GET /api/boards/search` route let anyone search thread titles, thread bodies, and reply bodies across all four boards at once, closing the gap the numbered backlog's own seed list named directly -- previously the only way to find a relevant past thread was to already know which board it lived on and page through it by hand. `searchBoards()` (`lib/boards.ts`) mirrors `listCampaigns()`'s `q` keyword-search convention (case-insensitive substring, `escapeLikePattern`-escaped, same paginated shape), plus a correlated `EXISTS` subquery against `board_replies` so a real answer buried in a reply still surfaces its parent thread -- each result carries a `matchedInThread` flag so the UI can explain a reply-only match. An empty query returns no results rather than every thread on every board -- this is a search, not a second browse view.

Explicitly not yet done (remaining larger backlog items): email notifications (backlog #6c — blocked, no SMTP/provider credentials); real geocoded distance search (backlog #41 phase 2 — blocked, no maps/geocoding provider API key).
Duplicate campaign (backlog #47, self-identified -- done 2026-09-08, live session): a campaign's DM can click "Duplicate campaign" in `DmControls` to instantly spin up a brand-new campaign pre-filled with the original's re-usable setup fields (title suffixed "(Copy)", description, system, capacity, location, new-player-friendly flag, session format, starting level, tone tags, and danger level) -- built for a DM who runs recurring one-shots or west-marches-style tables and would otherwise have to re-type everything from scratch each time. The roster, schedule, session log, party notes, table chat, resources, initiative tracker, NPC notes, sub requests, and ratings are deliberately not copied -- the duplicate starts exactly like any other freshly created campaign, open for requests with an empty roster and nothing scheduled yet.
Site-admin role + board moderation (backlog #48, self-identified -- done 2026-09-08, live session): `users.is_admin` is a plain boolean, granted automatically at signup to whoever's email matches a new `ADMIN_EMAIL` environment variable (unset in this sandbox -- see the dated session log entry for the "nobody is actually admin here" caveat). A site admin can delete any thread or reply on `/boards`, not just their own (the existing self-moderation delete routes now accept either the post's own author or an admin), and reviews a simple reported-content queue at `/admin/boards` (sorted by report count) fed by a new `board_reports` table -- any signed-in user can report a thread or reply once each via a "Report" button on the thread page. The NavBar shows an "Admin" link only to a signed-in admin. Scoped to boards only, per the task's own framing; chat/resources/other content have no moderation layer yet.

Character sheet export/print view (backlog #49, self-identified -- done 2026-09-08, live session): every character now has its own read-only, print-friendly page at `/characters/[id]` (public, no sign-in required -- a character's fields are already fully public via `/players/[id]`), reachable from a "View / print character sheet" link shown everywhere a character is displayed (profile, public player page, campaign page, sub-request panel). The page reuses the existing `CharacterSummary` component and adds a `window.print()` button plus a "Download as text" link to `GET /api/characters/[id]/export`, which serves a plain-text character sheet (name, class/ancestry/role, status, bio, backstory, epilogue) as a real file download, built by the new pure/DB-free `lib/characterExport.ts`. The app's nav chrome is hidden via `print:hidden` when printing.

Self-service account data export (backlog #50, self-identified -- done 2026-09-08, live session): a signed-in user can download everything this app has recorded about their account as one `.json` file, via a "Download my data" link on a new `/settings/account` page (cross-linked with `/settings/notifications`, and linked from `/profile`). `GET /api/account/export` (`lib/accountExport.ts`'s `getAccountExport()`) aggregates the user's profile, characters, campaigns they DM, every membership across every status, sub requests/volunteering/placements, direct and table-chat messages, ratings given and received, session log entries and kudos, notifications, follows, board posts and reports, RSVPs, DM-recorded attendance about them, uploaded resources, DM-only prep tools for campaigns they run, and derived stats -- gated only on being signed in, since it always exports the caller's own data and nobody else's. Account **deletion** is explicitly not built alongside this -- a DM's owned campaign has an active party depending on it, and deciding what happens to it on deletion is a real product decision this pass didn't make; the settings page says so directly rather than silently omitting the option.


Stack
Next.js 16.3.4 (App Router, Turbopack) + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS v4 + better-sqlite3 (file-based, not in-memory) + zod v4 + vitest v4. React 19.2.8.

Backlog (priority order)
1. ~~Persist git history off the ephemeral workspace~~ — DONE 2026-09-02. Repo lives at `https://github.com/Billbiller/Session-zero`, checked out at `C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo` on the owner's own laptop, authenticated with the owner's own PAT stored in that machine's local git config (not the cloud sandbox's broken `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` placeholder). Going forward: work in that checkout (via the desktop bridge) and `git push` for real, instead of rebuilding from scratch in a fresh cloud sandbox every session.
2. ~~Real authentication~~ — DONE 2026-09-02. Password-based sign-up/sign-in with `bcryptjs` hashing, validation (name, 8+ char password, unique email), and generic-rejection sign-in error handling. See "What the app does today" above.
3. Deployment decision (SQLite needs persistent-disk hosting, not serverless) — a decision, not a coding task.
4. ~~Campaign tracking UI polish (scheduling/timezone handling)~~ — done.
5. ~~Leave campaign flow~~ — done.
6. Notification polish, split into sub-items:
   * 6a. ~~Leaving a campaign notifies the rest of the party, not just the DM~~ — done.
   * 6b. ~~Party-notes edits notify the rest of the party~~ — done.
   * 6c. Email notifications — not started. No SMTP/email-provider credentials exist yet.
   * 6d. ~~Per-notification-type preferences~~ — done.
   * 6e. ~~Replace unread-badge polling (currently every 15s) with live push (websocket/SSE)~~ — done 2026-09-02 (nightly build). Implemented as Server-Sent Events (not a full websocket) — see "What the app does today" above and the dated session log entry. Polling is kept as an automatic fallback, not removed.
7. ~~Free-text/keyword campaign search~~ — done 2026-09-03 (nightly build). A `q` param on `listCampaigns`/`GET /api/campaigns`/the `/campaigns` UI does a case-insensitive substring match across title, description, and system, ANDed with the existing exact `system` filter rather than replacing it.
8. ~~Player profiles~~ — done 2026-09-06 (live session). `/profile` (self, editable: bio, preferred systems, availability, plus a "My campaigns" DMing/playing view) and `/players/[id]` (anyone, read-only), linked from DM/roster names throughout.
~~9. The campaign detail page (`app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx`) already composes seven feature components (DmControls, JoinLeaveControls, RequestsPanel, RosterPanel, ScheduleForm, PartyNotesPanel, SessionLogPanel) — worth revisiting for further splitting/a shared layout primitive before more is added to it.~~ **Done 2026-09-06** — extracted a shared `components/Section.tsx` outer-card wrapper, used by all nine feature panels on the page. Pure style extraction, no behavior change.
10. If a general account-settings surface is ever needed (display name changes outside of re-signing-in, etc.), consider whether `/settings/notifications` should become a tab within a broader `/settings` section rather than its own top-level route.
11. `notification_preferences` has no admin/DM-side equivalent — e.g. a DM currently can't see whether their players have muted `join_requested`. Not a bug — muting is a personal choice — but worth a UX pass later (maybe a non-muteable "critical" tier) if this ever surfaces as real user confusion.
12. Tooling notes for future sessions — see below.
13. New tonight: `next/font/google` (Geist/Geist Mono, in the default `create-next-app` template) fails `next build` in network-restricted sandboxes (`Failed to fetch Geist from Google Fonts`) — removed in favor of system font stacks in `app/globals.css`'s `@theme inline` block. If custom webfonts are wanted later, self-host them with `next/font/local` instead of `next/font/google`.
14. New tonight: same-millisecond `ORDER BY created_at DESC` ties (notifications, session log entries, and campaign listings created back-to-back in the same test/request) don't reliably sort newest-first on `created_at` alone since ISO-string timestamps only have millisecond resolution — added `rowid` as a secondary sort key (`ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC`, and the mirrored `ASC` forms) everywhere "newest/oldest first" matters.
15. ~~Character profiles (bio, art/avatar placeholder, backstory, per-system essentials — class/ancestry for 5e-likes, archetype for others)~~ — done 2026-09-06, merged from two parallel implementations (nightly build + a live session; see the "Current status" note and session log above/below). `characters` table + CRUD at `/api/characters`, editable on `/profile`, read-only on `/players/[id]`. Portrait is a curated emoji set, not an uploaded image (no image-upload infrastructure exists). A character can also optionally be linked to one campaign (access-checked the same way as party notes/session log), surfacing it on that campaign's page next to the roster too. Prerequisite for items 17 and 19 below, which can now build on this.
16. ~~Two-way, post-session ratings~~ — done 2026-09-06 (live session). Players rate the DM, the DM rates players, via a 1-5 star + toggleable descriptive tags (different tag sets per rated-role) on the campaign detail page, upserted per (campaign, rater, ratee) pair so a rating can be revised. Aggregated into an "As DM" / "As player" average + top tags on `/players/[id]` — a user with no ratings shows as **unrated**, not a zero score, matching the source chat's design intent. Judgment call: scoped to per-campaign rather than true per-session (this app has no discrete "session" entity to hang a rating on, only a DM-authored session log and a single next-session date), and strictly DM<->player (no player-to-player ratings, matching the backlog's own wording). Leaving a campaign now sends a `rating_prompt` notification to both sides.
17. ~~Session logbook with reactions ("kudos")~~ — done 2026-09-06 (live session). Every session log entry has a tap-to-react "👏" button; the DM and any approved/left party member can toggle their own kudos on an entry, and the entry's author is notified (`session_log_kudos`) the first time someone else reacts. Judgment call: kept visibility exactly as it already was (the entry's existing private party) — broadening logbook visibility beyond the current party (e.g. to a player's followers) is an explicit future product decision, not folded into this item.
18. ~~Personal stats dashboard~~ — done 2026-09-06 (live session). Campaigns DMed/played, sessions run/played, characters created, distinct systems tried, most-played system, and longest-running campaign (by sessions logged) on both `/profile` (self) and the public `/players/[id]`. Judgment call: dropped "hours at the table" from the shipped scope — this app has no session-duration data anywhere to derive it from honestly, and fabricating an estimate felt worse than omitting it; the other five stats are all directly derivable from existing tables. "Sessions played/run" is a proxy (session log entry counts), not a literal session count, documented as such in the code.
19. ~~Character legacies & campaign chronicle~~ — done 2026-09-06 (live session). Characters gained a status (active/retired/fallen) and a free-text epilogue; campaigns gained a DM-set, player-visible danger level tag and an aggregate "Chronicle" line counting currently-linked characters by status. Judgment call: "characters passed through" in the chronicle means characters *currently* linked to the campaign, not a full historical roster — this app's character-campaign link is a single current pointer (see item 15), with no retained history of past links once a character is unlinked or relinked elsewhere. A fuller "every character who ever passed through" history would need link-history tracking as its own follow-up, not folded into this item.
~~20. Substitute player workflow: a player can post "looking for a sub" for a session; a volunteer pool other users can browse and volunteer into; three-way approval required (character owner sets guardrails, DM approves, active table confirms) before a sub is seated; guardrails default to blocking permanent/irreversible actions (character death, spending rare resources, major story choices) unless the owner explicitly allows them. Rationale: ranked *higher* than the legacy/chronicle features in the source chat's own discussion, because it targets the actual reason campaigns die (attendance/scheduling erosion) rather than engagement polish. Feasible, but the heaviest single item here — a real multi-party approval workflow plus a temporary-custody state on a character, not just a display feature. Should be scoped as its own multi-night effort (e.g., first the request + volunteer pool, then the three-way approval state machine, then guardrail enforcement) rather than attempted in one pass.~~ **Done 2026-09-06.** Phase 1 (the request + browsable volunteer pool, at `/subs` and on each campaign's own page). Phase 2: owner + DM approval (an explicit product decision changed "three-way" to two approvers -- the rest of the active party is notified but isn't a blocking vote, to keep the state machine bounded) plus a temporary-custody marker on the character once both approve. Guardrails exist as a free-text note the owner attaches, not the structured default-blocking checkboxes the backlog line originally described, and there's no programmatic *enforcement* of them (this app has no gameplay to enforce them during) -- see the dated session log entry for the full reasoning.
21. ~~Discovery as a swipe/deck UI~~ — done 2026-09-06 (live session). `/campaigns` gained a "Discover" toggle: the same filtered campaign list, presented one card at a time with "Pass" (advance) and "View & join" (to the real detail page). Judgment call: scoped to campaign cards only, not also player/DM cards — campaigns are the entity this app's browse/filter already covers end-to-end, and a "connect" action on a player/DM card has no obvious real target yet (there's no direct messaging or player-to-player matching in this app to connect *into*), so extending the deck to people would need product scope this backlog line doesn't define. No join/leave logic is duplicated in the deck itself — it only decides what to show; the real membership actions stay on the campaign detail page, the single source of truth. Pass/skip decisions are session-local UI state, not persisted.
22. ~~Location/"near me" discovery~~ — coarse version done 2026-09-06 (live session). Free-text `location` field on profiles and campaigns (e.g. "Austin, TX" or "Online/Remote"), filterable on `/campaigns` via a case-insensitive substring match. Judgment call: shipped only the coarse, free-text level explicitly called out as feasible now — precise geolocation/distance-radius search remains blocked pending a geocoding/maps API key and the privacy-surface decision this backlog line itself flagged; that part is intentionally not attempted here.
23. Freemium tiers (Free vs. "Zero+" paid tier) and any payment processing. Rationale: explicitly part of the original product plan and pricing shown in the prototype ($0 vs $7/mo), but **not feasible to build right now**: it requires a payment processor integration (e.g., Stripe) or, if ever distributed as a native iOS app, Apple's mandatory In-App Purchase — either way that means real business/legal decisions (entity setup, subscription terms, tax handling) and payment-provider credentials this session doesn't have and can't obtain unattended. Blocked on the same kind of human decision as item 6c (email notifications), not a coding gap. Revisit once there's a decision-maker and credentials available; until then, ship features free rather than half-gating them.
24. Native iOS/App Store distribution. Rationale: the source chat's own stated long-term goal, but this repo is a web app (Next.js), and shipping to the App Store either means wrapping it (with real limits on what that can do well) or a separate native build — a large, distinct effort with its own cost/timeline (the source chat's own model estimated ~30 weeks and ~$191K for a full native build, or ~16 weeks/~$26K for a lean, no-code, web-first MVP validation path). Not something to attempt incrementally in nightly sessions; folds into existing backlog item #3 (deployment decision) as a bigger, explicit decision point rather than a task to start coding toward without that decision being made first.
25. Clubs/regional groups and curated community lists (e.g., "best one-shots for new players"). Rationale: mentioned as a lower-tier idea in the source chat's own competitive research, and the same research explicitly warned against adding engagement-bait features that work against getting people to an actual table. Worth doing eventually as light community/discovery features, but correctly the lowest priority of everything above — not scheduled ahead of items 15-22. **Substantially addressed 2026-09-07 (live session)** by backlog #37 (lightweight community discussion boards), the concrete first slice this item's own text was gesturing at -- see #37 below and the dated session log entry. Left open rather than marked fully DONE: #37 built topic-based *discussion* (boards, threads, replies), not persistent regional *groups* (e.g. a standing "Austin tabletop meetup" membership list distinct from a thread about it) -- the "Local Meetups & In-Person Games" board gives that idea a place to be talked about, not a place to organize as an ongoing group with its own roster. If that distinction ever matters enough to build, it would be a genuinely separate feature on top of #37, not a revision of it.
26. ~~Character portrait image upload~~ (owner-requested, added 2026-09-06) — done 2026-09-06 (live session), using the simplest of the three storage options this line itself documented: a real uploaded picture (PNG/JPEG/WEBP/GIF, capped at roughly 200KB) stored as a base64 `data:` URL directly in a new `characters.portrait_data_url` column, shown instead of the curated emoji avatar wherever a character appears. Judgment call: deliberately shipped now rather than waiting on item #3 (deployment decision) as this line originally suggested — a small, size-capped base64 column has no dependency on where the app eventually deploys, unlike local-disk or object-storage options, so there was no real blocker left once "simplest option, capped size" was accepted as the tradeoff. No server-side resizing/compression: an oversized upload is rejected with a clear error rather than transformed, keeping scope to validation only. Revisit with real object storage (S3/R2/etc.) once a deployment target is chosen, if image quality/size becomes a real constraint.
~~27. Calendar-based availability + schedule-matching search (owner-requested, added 2026-09-06). Replace the free-text `availability` field on profiles (e.g. "Weeknights after 7pm ET") with structured, queryable availability — a recurring weekly calendar grid (day + time-of-day blocks) a player fills in once — so it becomes a real filter: a DM could search for players free at their table's actual session time, and a player could search campaigns/DMs whose sessions land in their own free blocks, instead of everyone just reading each other's free-text and eyeballing the overlap. Rationale: this is the actual matchmaking payoff the app's name promises, and free-text availability (shipped as part of item #8) was always a placeholder for this. Feasibility: a real structural change, not a quick follow-up — needs (a) a new data model for recurring weekly time-slots per user (not just one text field), (b) a calendar/grid picker UI component (nothing like this exists in the app yet), (c) timezone handling for comparing a player's availability against a campaign's `next_session_at` (the existing UTC-safe schedule-status logic in `lib/schedule.ts` is relevant prior art but only handles a single one-off datetime, not a recurring weekly pattern), and (d) the actual search/filter query once availability is structured. Scope as its own multi-part effort (data model + picker UI first, then the search/matching layer) rather than one pass, similar to item #20.~~ **Phase 1 done 2026-09-06** (data model + weekly day/block picker grid, shown read-only on public profiles). The search/matching layer (comparing two users' grids, timezone handling) explicitly remains for a future phase — see the dated session log entry.
~~28. Rate the campaign itself, not just the people at the table (owner-requested, added 2026-09-06).~~ **Done 2026-09-06 (nightly scheduled build).** Any approved-or-left member (not the DM) can give the campaign a 1-5 star rating plus tags from a new `CAMPAIGN_RATING_TAGS` set, aggregated into a public average/count/top-tags shown on the campaign detail page for everyone, including signed-out visitors. Judgment call: shipped as a new `campaign_ratings` table (its own `UNIQUE (campaign_id, rater_id)`) rather than relaxing the existing `ratings` table's `ratee_id` to also accept a `campaigns.id` — a separate table keeps referential integrity simple and follows this app's existing per-concept-table convention. Not yet surfaced on `/campaigns` browse cards (detail page only) — a natural follow-up, not attempted tonight to keep this a clean one-night scope. See the dated session log entry for the full design writeup.
~~29. "Looking for a sub" should carry a specific date, time, and location for the session it's covering (owner-requested, added 2026-09-06). Today's sub request (item #20, shipped) has only a free-text `note` field — a requester might mention "can't make it Sept 20th" in prose, but there's no structured `needed_at`/location data a volunteer could filter or sort by, and no way for the app to show "this sub is needed this Saturday at 7pm" distinctly from the note text. Feasibility: straightforward, following an existing convention — a nullable `needed_at TEXT` column on `sub_requests` (ISO-8601 timestamp, mirroring how `campaigns.next_session_at` already works, including reusing the UTC-safe formatting/display logic in `lib/schedule.ts`) plus a location field (free-text, matching the coarse `location` pattern already used on profiles/campaigns rather than inventing precise geocoding). Worth deciding whether location is even meaningful per-request (most campaigns already have their own `location`/online-vs-in-person setting — a per-request location might mostly matter for in-person games where a sub needs to know exactly where to show up) or whether it should just default from the campaign's own location field with an optional override.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session) — an older, still-open item picked up after being skipped over earlier in today's batch (#31-42); see the dated session log entry for that note.** Resolved the backlog line's own open question explicitly: location defaults to the campaign's own `location` field (already shown on the campaign) and is only stored as a per-request override (`sub_requests.location`, nullable — null means "use the campaign's location") when the requester explicitly types a different one, rather than treating location as always meaningful per-request. `sub_requests.needed_at` (nullable ISO-8601, same `ALTER TABLE`-migration-guard convention as `danger_level`/`new_player_friendly`/`session_format`) reuses `lib/schedule.ts`'s `computeScheduleStatus()` as-is to classify a request's own date as unscheduled/upcoming/past-due, the same UTC-safe way `campaigns.next_session_at` already is. Both the campaign-page panel and the app-wide `/subs` pool show the specific date/time and the effective location distinctly from the free-text note.
~~30. Campaigns should carry additional structured info: starting level, genre (within the system), and role-playing style/tone (e.g. horror, heavy combat, roleplay-focused) (owner-requested, added 2026-09-06). Today a campaign only has `system` (free text, e.g. "D&D 5e") plus the recently-added `danger_level` (a closed enum: low-lethality/moderate/high-lethality/deadly-OSR) and `location`. There's no starting level, no genre tag, and no tone/style descriptor — all real filtering/matching signals a prospective player would want before joining (a level 1 horror one-shot reads very differently from a level 10 combat-heavy dungeon crawl in the same system). Feasibility: straightforward, following the `danger_level` convention already established — `starting_level` as a simple text or small-integer field (systems vary enough in how they express level/rank that free text may be more honest than a rigid number, e.g. "Level 3" vs. "Tier 2" vs. a narrative milestone system), and `genre`/`style` as either a curated `as const` array with a label map (matching `DANGER_LEVELS`) if the set of options is worth constraining for filterability, or free text/tags if the space is too open-ended to enumerate honestly. Worth deciding genre/style as a single closed enum vs. a multi-select tag list (a campaign can plausibly be both "heavy battle" and "horror") before implementing, since that shapes both the schema and the campaign browse filter UI.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session) — an older, still-open item picked up after being skipped over earlier in today's batch (#31-42); see the dated session log entry for the full design.** Resolved both open questions explicitly: `starting_level` is free text (not a rigid integer), following the exact nullable-text-column convention `sub_requests.location` established under #29. Genre/tone/style is a curated multi-select tag list, not a single closed enum -- `CAMPAIGN_TONE_TAGS` in `lib/types.ts` -- since a campaign can genuinely be more than one of these at once, stored as a JSON-encoded array following the exact `tags` pattern already established by `lib/ratings.ts`/`lib/campaignRatings.ts`, validated against the closed vocabulary and capped at 5 tags. Both fields are shown everywhere `danger_level`/`session_format` already render, and `tone_tags` is filterable on `/campaigns` via an any-of match.
~~31. Direct messaging, 1:1 (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a prototype-gap audit). The prototype's own tagline is "find your party, not just a group chat," and explicitly promises "swipe, connect, and message once you're both in" -- but this app has no messaging of any kind today, between anyone. Rationale: probably the single highest-leverage item in this whole batch, since it's foundational -- a prospective player messaging a DM before requesting to join, or two matched people coordinating without exchanging personal contact info, makes several other items below (resource sharing, RSVP coordination) more useful once it exists. Feasibility: a real but bounded new subsystem -- needs a conversation/message data model, an inbox UI, and a decision on who can message whom (anyone, or only after some existing relationship like a join request or shared campaign membership -- worth deciding deliberately given this app's existing private-access-boundary conventions).~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** A new `messages` table (sender/recipient/body/read), with a conversation modeled as the unique pair of users rather than its own entity. Judgment call: any signed-in user can message any other signed-in user, no prerequisite relationship -- matching this app's existing openness (campaign browsing, the sub-request pool, and public player profiles are all ungated the same way). Unread tracking rides the existing notification system (a new `message_received` type) rather than a second, parallel live-badge system. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~32. Table group chat (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a prototype-gap audit). The prototype's other explicit promise: "a group thread per table, separate from your DMs, so campaign chatter doesn't get lost." Distinct from the existing shared party notes, which is a persistent document (more like a wiki page) rather than a conversation history. Feasibility: depends on #31's messaging infrastructure existing first (or at least the same underlying message data model), scoped to a campaign's active party (DM + approved/active members) using the same `hasPrivateAccess` boundary already gating party notes/session log.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** A new `campaign_messages` table (same sender/body/created_at shape as #31's `messages` table, scoped to a campaign instead of a user pair) plus a `campaign_message_reads` table tracking each user's own last-read timestamp per campaign. Access is exactly `hasPrivateAccess` (DM + approved active members) -- a departed member's own past messages stay visible in the thread's history, but they lose read/write access the moment they leave, same as the rest of the private side. Judgment call: notifies a party member only on the first unread message since their last visit (not every message, and not zero notifications either) -- see the dated session log entry for the full reasoning.
~~33. DM dashboard basics: initiative tracker + NPC quick-notes (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a prototype-gap audit). Prototype promised an "initiative tracker, NPC quick-notes, and a campaign timeline so the story stays straight session to session." Not built -- the session log is a recap diary, not a prep/running tool. Rationale: arguably the highest-leverage unbuilt prototype feature, since DMs are the scarce resource in this ecosystem (see StartPlaying.games' entire business model, built around GM scarcity). Feasibility: a real but scopeable new feature -- an initiative tracker could start as a simple ordered list scoped to a campaign (not a full combat simulator), NPC notes as a lightweight per-campaign text/card list similar in shape to existing party notes.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** An initiative tracker (`initiative_entries` table -- name, initiative value, free-text HP, free-text notes, an app-maintained `order_index` moved via explicit move-up/move-down, not drag-and-drop or auto-sort) and NPC quick-notes (`npc_notes` table -- name + free-text notes, add/edit/delete) on the campaign detail page, both DM-only via a new narrower `isDm()` predicate in `lib/access.ts` (distinct from the wider `hasPrivateAccess()` already used for party notes/session log/chat, which includes approved players too). Judgment call: DM-only, not party-visible -- these are prep/running tools for the DM's own use (a monster's real HP, an NPC's secret motive), not something the backlog line itself resolved but a deliberate boundary choice documented in the dated session log entry. Both persist as "current state" between requests/sessions (like party notes) until the DM explicitly clears/removes entries, rather than auto-resetting -- this app has no discrete "session" entity to reset against. No combat automation (damage tracking, conditions, etc.) and no campaign timeline -- out of scope, matching the backlog line's own "basics" framing.
~~34. Resource vault: per-campaign uploads + community resource library (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a prototype-gap audit). Prototype: upload maps/handouts/homebrew for your table, or browse what the community's shared for your system. Feasibility: follows the same size-capped, no-deployment-dependency pattern already established for character portrait uploads (#26 -- base64-in-SQLite, capped size, no image processing) -- a per-campaign vault could ship the same way; a cross-community library browsable by system is a larger second phase (needs the system-hub browsing surface in #36 below to make sense as a destination).~~ **Phase 1 done 2026-09-07 (live session).** Any active party member (DM + approved members, the `hasPrivateAccess` boundary -- not the narrower DM-only boundary used for #33's initiative tracker/NPC notes) can upload a file to a campaign's resource vault -- images (PNG/JPEG/WEBP/GIF), PDF, or plain text, capped at roughly 4MB, stored as base64 `data:` URLs in a new `campaign_resources` table (the same storage pattern as #26's character portraits, broadened to more file types and a larger cap). Editing/deleting is further restricted to the uploader or the DM. Judgment call, explicitly scoped, matching the #20/#27 phased-delivery convention: the cross-community library browsable by system remains phase 2, deferred until backlog #36 (system hubs) exists to give it a browsing destination. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~35. Session RSVP + reminders (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a prototype-gap audit). Prototype: "auto-reminders" and "track who's confirmed for next session." Today a campaign has one DM-set `next_session_at` and a computed unscheduled/upcoming/past-due status, with no per-member confirm/decline and no reminder notification as the date approaches. Feasibility: doesn't require the still-blocked email/SMTP (#6c) -- an in-app notification fired some interval before `next_session_at` works with existing notification infrastructure; per-member RSVP is a new small table (campaign_id, user_id, response) similar in shape to memberships. Also produces the attendance data #39 below needs.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** A `session_rsvps` table (campaign_id, user_id, response) scoped to the campaign's *current* `next_session_at` -- rescheduling clears every row (see `lib/schedule.ts`'s `updateSchedule()`), since an RSVP for a date that's no longer scheduled is meaningless. A `session_reminder` notification fires once per (campaign, user, exact session date) when that date is within 24h and still upcoming, via a lazy check-and-fire (`lib/sessionReminders.ts`) triggered by real user activity (`GET /api/notifications`, and a campaign's own detail page) rather than any background worker/cron -- this app has neither, and building one is out of scope (same category as #3). Judgment call, documented as a known limitation rather than a bug: a reminder only fires if someone actually triggers that check before the session happens -- if nobody in a campaign opens the app in that window, nobody gets reminded for that session. Produces the attendance data #39 could build on next (RSVP confirmed-vs-actually-showed-up is still a separate, unbuilt signal).
~~36. Curated system-specific hubs (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a prototype-gap audit). Prototype: a hand-picked set of systems (5e, Pathfinder 2e, Call of Cthulhu, Vampire: TM, PbtA/Blades) each with a short description and a "most matched" style meta tag, plus explicit "system-agnostic, add your own" framing. Today `system` is pure free text with an exact-match filter and a keyword search -- there's no system-level landing page, description, or browsing surface. Feasibility: a curated `as const` list of well-known systems with descriptions (matching the `DANGER_LEVELS`-style convention), each with its own page showing open campaigns/activity for that system, while still allowing free-text "other/homebrew" the way the prototype's own "+ your system" card does.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** `CURATED_SYSTEMS`/`CURATED_SYSTEM_INFO` in `lib/types.ts` (six systems, PbtA and Blades in the Dark split into two distinct entries rather than the prototype's combined one since they're genuinely different systems) plus `lib/systems.ts` (slug -> curated info/match-pattern resolution and the live `campaignsForSystem()` query) and `/systems` + `/systems/[slug]` pages. Judgment call, stated explicitly as this item's own core design question: `campaigns.system` stays free text rather than becoming a closed enum -- a curated hub is a case-insensitive substring-match *view* over that free text (reusing `lib/campaigns.ts`'s existing `escapeLikePattern` convention via a new `systemAliases` option on `listCampaigns()`), never an authoritative reclassification, so nothing about existing campaigns or the existing keyword search changes. A non-matching system is exactly the prototype's own homebrew case, not an error -- surfaced via an explicit "Other/homebrew" link to `/campaigns` rather than dropped.
~~37. Lightweight community discussion boards~~ -- done 2026-09-07 (live session). A fixed, curated `as const` set of four topic boards (New Player Questions, Homebrew & House Rules, Looking for Group Advice, Local Meetups & In-Person Games), not campaign-scoped -- `lib/boards.ts`, `board_threads`/`board_replies` tables, `/boards` + `/boards/[slug]` + `/boards/[slug]/[threadId]` pages. Browsing is public; posting a thread or reply requires sign-in. "Light moderation" shipped as self-moderation only for this pass (a post's own author can delete it, no backstop, no admin/reporting system yet -- an explicit, documented scope cut, not an oversight). Judgment call: no new notification type for replies, since notifications' link-target columns (`campaign_id`/`related_user_id`) have no natural slot for "a reply landed on your thread" without new schema this narrow a pass didn't justify. This is the concrete first slice of backlog #25, which is now marked substantially addressed above.
~~38. Follow + activity feed (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a community-platform review). Opt-in following of players/DMs you've actually played with, surfacing their public session-log posts and character milestones in a lightweight feed -- Strava's core retention mechanic ("worth sharing," per this app's own logbook copy), adapted to respect the existing private-side boundary (only content the author has already made visible, nothing new exposed). Feasibility: a follows table plus a feed query aggregating existing session-log/character-legacy events from followed users -- no new content types, just a new way to surface existing public ones.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** Following is open (no shared-campaign gate), matching backlog #31's own precedent. The feed is deliberately narrower than a literal reading of "session-log posts": session log entries are private to a campaign's active party, so surfacing them for a non-member follower would be a real regression against this app's own tested `hasPrivateAccess` boundary -- instead, a new `feed_events` table (written to at the moment one of four already-public events happens: character created, character reaches retired/fallen, a DM's campaign fills up, or a campaign gets its first public rating) surfaces only what's already visible on the actor's own `/players/[id]` or the campaign's own detail page. See the dated session log entry for the full privacy-boundary reasoning.
~~39. Attendance-based reliability signal (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a prototype-gap audit + community-platform review). The prototype's sub-pool mocks "sub reliability rating requested: 4+" as if a reliability score already exists -- it doesn't. Meetup built dedicated check-in tooling for the same reason (distinguishing "no-show" from "didn't RSVP"). Feasibility: depends on #35 (RSVP + reminders) existing first to produce real attendance data; once it does, a simple "shows up X% of the time" derived stat, shown alongside the existing rating/reputation info.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** Re-examined the "depends on #35" framing before building anything: #35's `session_rsvps` is intent to attend the *next* session, not a record that anyone actually did, and its rows don't even survive a reschedule (`updateSchedule()` clears them), so there is no durable RSVP history to derive a stat from after the fact. Built the real thing instead of a proxy: a new `session_log_attendance` table lets the DM optionally mark, per session log entry (the one durable per-session record this app has), which of the campaign's currently-active non-DM party members actually showed up -- no row means "not recorded," never "recorded absent." `lib/attendance.ts`'s `getAttendanceStats()` derives a `{ recorded, attended, rate }` signal (`rate: null`, not 0%, with no history -- matching the unrated-not-zero convention `lib/ratings.ts` already established) shown publicly on `/players/[id]` next to the DM/player reputation lines; per-entry detail (who missed which specific session) stays DM-only to view. Known, documented limitation: attendance-marking candidates are the *current* active party, not a point-in-time snapshot of who was actually on the roster when an old entry was originally logged (this app has no membership history to reconstruct that). See the dated session log entry below for the full design.
~~40. New-player onboarding path (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a community-platform review). A "new to tabletop" profile flag plus a curated, filterable view of beginner-friendly open campaigns/DMs -- echoes the prototype's "New player friendly" tag and this app's own stated goal of lowering the barrier to a first table. Feasibility: a boolean or small enum on the profile, plus a filter on campaign browse/discovery (a DM could similarly flag a campaign as new-player-friendly, following the existing `danger_level`/`location` filter-field convention).~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** `profiles.new_to_tabletop` (self-flag, settable on `/profile`) and `campaigns.new_player_friendly` (DM-set on create/edit, following the exact `danger_level` convention) -- both plain booleans, not a curated multi-value enum, matching this backlog line's own "boolean or small enum" framing at the simpler end. A "New-player friendly only" checkbox filter on `/campaigns` (query-param + pagination-preserving, same pattern as System/Location), plus a subtle opt-in nudge toward that filter for a viewer whose own profile is flagged "new to tabletop" -- not a curated landing page or a pre-applied default filter, which felt like more surface than a first pass justified. See the dated session log entry for the full design.
~~41. Real proximity-based, in-person-first discovery (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07; scope refined 2026-09-07 per explicit product direction). The natural phase 2 of #22 -- today's `location` is a coarse free-text substring match, not actual distance, and doesn't structurally distinguish "in-person, N miles away" from "Online/Remote." Product direction, stated explicitly by the owner: StartPlaying.games is the closest competitive template for the matchmaking/reputation loop this app is building, but StartPlaying is all-online and explicitly has no local/in-person support -- this app should go the other way and actively drive players toward real, in-person tables, not just tolerate them as one possible `location` value equal to remote play. Feasibility: needs (a) geocoding/distance search (a real new dependency -- a maps/geocoding API key, same blocker this item already flagged before), (b) a structural in-person-vs-remote field on both profiles and campaigns (not just free text), and (c) discovery/ranking that surfaces and favors nearby in-person tables rather than treating in-person and remote as equivalent. The biggest single differentiation opportunity in this batch, but also the one most dependent on a real infrastructure decision (a geocoding provider) and privacy-surface care (precise location data is more sensitive than everything else this app stores).~~ **Phase 1 done 2026-09-07 (live session)** -- (b) and (c) above, split out and shipped following the exact #20/#27/#34 phased-delivery convention: `campaigns.session_format` (`in_person`/`remote`/`hybrid`, nullable, `danger_level`-style CHECK-constrained enum) and the symmetric, display-only `profiles.session_format_preference`, plus a `listCampaigns()` ranking clause that puts in-person ahead of hybrid ahead of remote/unset by default, applied everywhere campaigns are browsed (list view, Discover deck, curated system hubs) without excluding remote games (also filterable explicitly via a new `sessionFormat` option). Phase 2 -- (a), real geocoding/distance search -- remains explicitly blocked: this environment has no maps/geocoding provider API key, confirmed by checking for one before starting, and obtaining one is a credential/infrastructure decision outside this session's authority, the same category of blocker as #6c (email/SMTP). See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
42. Paid/professional GM marketplace -- flagged only, not scoped (owner-requested, added 2026-09-07, from a community-platform review). StartPlaying.games' entire business model: professional GMs bookable for a fee, with profile pages showing specialties and ratings. Not a recommendation to build -- flagging only as an alternative or complement to the already-blocked Zero+ subscription (#23) worth knowing about if/when the business-model conversation happens. Same category of blocker as #23: a real business/legal/payment-processor decision, not a coding gap.
~~43. Board reply notifications (self-identified, added 2026-09-07 -- the numbered backlog ran dry, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case).
