Rationale: backlog #37's own session log entry explicitly flagged this gap when discussion boards shipped -- a thread author has no way to know anyone replied, undercutting the whole feature's usefulness -- and explained it was scoped out only because notifications had no link-target column for a thread id, not because it wasn't worth doing.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** Added `notifications.related_thread_id` (third link target, nullable, alongside `campaign_id`/`related_user_id`) and a `board_reply` notification type; a thread's own author is now notified on every reply from someone else (not batched), with no self-notification and no notification-spam-avoidance batching (board threads are lower-volume than live table chat, so the `message_received` per-message precedent applies, not `campaign_chat_message`'s first-unread-since-visit one). A deleted thread nulls out the FK reference rather than breaking it, matching `lib/characters.ts`'s `feed_events.character_id` convention. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~44. Dedicated unread-message badge in the nav (self-identified, added 2026-09-07 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #43, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case, drawing on this same task's own candidate list rather than a session-log-flagged gap). Rationale: direct messages (#31), table chat (#32), and board replies (#43) all fire real notifications that bump the general `/notifications` inbox and its NavBar badge, but `/messages` itself has no unread indicator of its own the way `/notifications` does -- a new DM only ever shows up as an undifferentiated bump to "Notifications," even though `lib/messages.ts`'s `listConversations()` already computes a real per-conversation `unreadCount` the nav never surfaces.~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** The NavBar's "Messages" link now carries its own live unread badge, entirely separate from the "Notifications" badge: a new `getTotalUnreadMessageCount()` (`lib/messages.ts`) sums unread rows directly off the `messages` table, pushed live over a new `/api/messages/stream` SSE endpoint (`lib/messageEvents.ts`, a small parallel pub/sub mirroring `lib/notificationEvents.ts` one-for-one) with the exact same EventSource-with-polling-fallback shape the notifications badge already uses, published from `sendMessage()` (the recipient's count) and `markConversationRead()` (the reader's count). See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~45. Table group chat has no unread-count UI anywhere in the nav or elsewhere (self-identified, added 2026-09-07 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #44, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case; explicitly flagged as the next candidate by backlog #44's own session log entry). Rationale: direct messages (#31, via #44) and the general notification inbox both got a live unread-count badge this session, but table group chat (#32) -- which already fires a `campaign_chat_message` notification and already tracks a real per-campaign unread count via `lib/campaignMessages.ts`'s `getUnreadCampaignMessageCount()` -- has no badge or indicator of its own anywhere: not in the nav, not on `/profile`'s "My campaigns" list, not on the campaign detail page next to the chat panel itself. A party member currently has no way to tell "there are unread messages in this campaign's table chat" short of opening every campaign they're in and scrolling to the chat panel (which itself immediately marks the thread read on load, so even that doesn't show a stable count).~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** Mirrors the #43/#44 pattern one level down: a new `getTotalUnreadCampaignMessageCountForUser()` (`lib/campaignMessages.ts`, summing `getUnreadCampaignMessageCount()` across every campaign a user DMs or actively plays in, reusing `lib/profiles.ts`'s `myCampaigns()` the same way `lib/sessionReminders.ts` already does) is pushed live over a new `GET /api/campaigns/chat/stream` SSE endpoint backed by a third small parallel pub/sub, `lib/campaignChatEvents.ts`, mirroring `lib/messageEvents.ts`/`lib/notificationEvents.ts` one-for-one. The NavBar's existing signed-in-user link (display name -> `/profile`) now carries this as its own live badge, using the identical EventSource-with-15s-polling-fallback shape the other two badges already use (polling fallback sums a new `unreadCampaignChatCounts` map added to `GET /api/profile`'s response, mirroring how the messages badge's fallback sums `GET /api/messages`). Judgment call on exact placement, since unlike Messages/Notifications there's no existing "my tables" top-level nav link to hang a badge on: rather than adding a new nav link (more surface than this pass justified) or picking an unrelated existing link, the badge rides the profile link, since "My campaigns" -- the natural place to see *which* table has new chat -- already lives on `/profile`, which now also shows a small per-campaign badge next to each campaign in the "Running (as DM)"/"Playing in" lists (sourced from that same new `unreadCampaignChatCounts` map). A second, complementary indicator was added directly on the campaign detail page: since opening a campaign's chat panel immediately marks it read (`markCampaignChatRead` fires on the panel's own `GET`), a live badge there would always read zero by the time it could render -- instead, `app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx` (a server component) computes the viewer's unread count for *that* campaign server-side, before the client panel's own fetch runs, and passes it to `CampaignChatPanel` as a one-time `initialUnreadCount` snapshot shown next to the "Table chat" heading ("3 new") -- an honest "here's what you're about to catch up on" signal rather than a live counter that can't stay accurate on this particular page. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.


~~46. Calendar export (.ics) for a campaign's next session (self-identified, added 2026-09-07 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #45, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case, drawing on this task's own candidate list). Rationale: a campaign's `next_session_at` (backlog #35, plus RSVP status) exists only inside this app -- there's no way to get "next session for Curse of Strahd is Sept 12, 7pm" into a real calendar app (Google Calendar, Outlook, Apple Calendar) without a party member manually retyping it, and a missed session because nobody's calendar reminded them is exactly the kind of attendance/scheduling erosion backlog #20's own rationale named as the actual reason campaigns die. Small, well-contained, no external dependency (a hand-built RFC 5545 `.ics` text file, no `ics` npm package or calendar-provider API needed).~~ **Done 2026-09-07 (live session).** A pure `lib/calendarExport.ts` (`buildSessionIcs()` plus its `escapeIcsText`/`foldIcsLine`/`formatIcsUtc` RFC 5545 helpers, DB-free and directly unit tested) generates a single-VEVENT `.ics` document, served from a new `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/calendar` route gated on the same `hasPrivateAccess` boundary as `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/rsvp` (403 for a non-member, 404 when no session is scheduled yet) and returned as a real `text/calendar` download (`Content-Disposition: attachment`) rather than JSON -- the first non-SSE, non-JSON API response in this codebase. The event's DTEND uses a fixed 3-hour default duration since this app has no per-campaign session-length field to derive one from honestly (same category of gap #18's stats dashboard already documented for "hours at the table"), and its DESCRIPTION includes the viewer's own current RSVP status (confirmed/declined/not yet answered) read fresh via `getViewerRsvp()` on every export -- the concrete answer to this item's own "and now RSVP status" framing: RSVP data isn't a separate export, it's folded into the one event as context. A `GET`-triggered download (a plain anchor link) was chosen over a `POST` action since exporting has no side effect worth confirming. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~47. Duplicate campaign / campaign templates (self-identified, added 2026-09-08 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #46, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case, from the seed list of candidates the task itself suggested). Rationale: a DM who runs recurring one-shots or west-marches-style tables currently has to re-enter every field from scratch for each new table -- title, system, capacity, location, format, tone tags, etc. -- with nothing in the app to carry a known-good setup forward. Judged the cleanest, most self-contained win on offer against the other candidates (sub-request sort/hide, board search, character-sheet export, an admin/moderation role) since it needed no new schema, no new role concept, and reuses createCampaign/updateCampaign wholesale.~~ **Done 2026-09-08 (live session).** A "Duplicate campaign" button in DmControls (DM-only, same as Edit/Cancel/Reopen) calls a new POST /api/campaigns/[id]/duplicate, which copies the campaign's re-usable setup fields (title suffixed "(Copy)", description, system, capacity, location, new-player-friendly flag, session format, starting level, tone tags, and danger level) into a brand-new campaign owned by the same DM, then navigates straight to it. Deliberately does NOT copy the roster, schedule, session log, party notes, table chat, resources, initiative tracker, NPC notes, sub requests, or ratings -- those belong to one specific run of the table, not the template it was built from, so the duplicate starts exactly like any other freshly created campaign. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~48. Minimal site-admin role + basic content moderation for discussion boards (self-identified, added 2026-09-08 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #47, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case; explicitly flagged as a still-open, intentional scope cut by backlog #37's own session log entry, twice). Rationale: #37 (discussion boards) shipped with self-moderation only and explicitly no admin/reporting system, since boards aren't campaign-scoped and have no DM-equivalent backstop moderator the way campaign resources/session log entries do -- a bad-faith post was only removable by its own author. Scoped narrow: `users.is_admin` boolean (no roles table), granted via a new `ADMIN_EMAIL` env var at signup (no UI to grant it, no human present to configure one by hand), an admin can delete any board thread/reply, and a lightweight `board_reports` table + `/admin/boards` queue lets any signed-in user report a post once for an admin to review.~~ **Done 2026-09-08 (live session).** `lib/access.ts`'s `isSiteAdmin()` plus `lib/auth.ts`'s `ADMIN_EMAIL`-at-signup grant; `lib/boards.ts`'s `requireAuthorOrAdmin()` lets the existing thread/reply `DELETE` routes accept a site admin as well as the post's own author; `reportThread()`/`reportReply()`/`listReportedContent()` back a new `/admin/boards` moderation queue (sorted by report count) and a "Report" button on the thread page. Explicitly NOT solved end-to-end in this environment -- `ADMIN_EMAIL` is unset here, so nobody is actually an admin from running the test suite or a smoke test; the real owner would need it set in whatever hosting environment #3 eventually picks. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~49. Character sheet export/print view (self-identified, added 2026-09-08 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #48, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case, from this task's own seed candidate list). Rationale: a character (backlog #15/19/26) now carries a real amount of data -- name, class/ancestry/role, bio, backstory, status, epilogue, portrait -- entered once and shown only inline inside this app (on `/profile` and `/players/[id]`), with no way to get it out to print for the table or hand to a new DM. Feasibility: no new schema, no new access rules (a character's own fields are already fully public via `/players/[id]`) -- purely a new read-only view and a plain-text download over data that already exists.~~ **Done 2026-09-08 (live session).** A new `/characters/[id]` page (public, no auth check -- character fields are already fully public via `/players/[id]`) gives every character its own print-friendly detail view, reusing the existing `CharacterSummary` component so it can't drift from how a character already renders elsewhere, plus a `window.print()` button and a "Download as text" link to a new `GET /api/characters/[id]/export` route (`text/plain`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, built by the new pure/DB-free `lib/characterExport.ts`, mirroring backlog #46's `.ics` export pattern). `CharacterSummary` itself gained a "View / print character sheet" link (shown everywhere it renders: profile, public player page, campaign page, sub-request panel), `print:hidden` where appropriate, and the root layout's nav chrome is hidden when printing too. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.
~~50. Self-service account data export (self-identified, added 2026-09-08 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #49, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case, from this task's own seed candidate list). Rationale: this app has real per-user data spread across ~20 tables (profile, characters, campaigns, memberships, messages, ratings, notifications, board posts, and more) and has never offered any way for a user to get their own data out, or even see everything the app holds about them in one place -- a real gap for a production app with real user data, flagged explicitly by this task's own seed list. Feasibility: read-only and additive -- every table already has a `user_id`/`author_id`/`rater_id`-shaped column to query by, so this is a new aggregating read path, not new schema or new write logic.~~ **Done 2026-09-08 (live session).** `lib/accountExport.ts`'s `getAccountExport(userId)` gathers a signed-in user's complete data footprint (account, profile, availability, characters, campaigns they DM, every membership across every status, sub requests/volunteering/placements, direct + table-chat messages, ratings given and received, session log entries and kudos, notifications, follows, board posts and reports, RSVPs, DM-recorded attendance about them, uploaded resources, DM-only prep tools for campaigns they run, and derived stats) into one `AccountExportData` object, served as a real downloadable `.json` file from `GET /api/account/export` (`requireUser()`-gated, no id parameter -- it only ever exports the caller's own data, so there's no access check to get wrong). A new `/settings/account` page ("Your data") gives it a home, cross-linked with the existing `/settings/notifications` page, plus a "Download your data" link on `/profile`. Judgment call, stated explicitly on the settings page itself: account **deletion** is deliberately NOT built alongside the export, even though the same page might imply it should be -- a DM-owned campaign has an active party depending on it, and deciding what happens to it on account deletion (cancel it? transfer it? orphan it?) is a real product decision this pass isn't positioned to make unattended, the same category of judgment call as backlog #23/#24's payment/App-Store items. A "contact the site owner" note stands in for real self-service deletion until that design work happens. See the dated session log entry for the full design and testing.

~~51. Sub-request pool sort/filter (self-identified, added 2026-09-08 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #50, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case, from this task's own seed candidate list). Rationale: backlog #29 gave `sub_requests` a structured `needed_at` and `neededAtStatus`, but `/subs` (the app-wide pool) and the campaign-page panel still only ever list every open request newest-posted-first with no way to sort by how soon it's needed or to hide the past-due ones cluttering the top of a growing pool -- exactly the gap this task's own seed list called out by name. Feasibility: `needed_at`/`neededAtStatus` already exist on every row; this is a query/UI change over data that's already there, no new schema.~~ **Done 2026-09-08 (live session).** `listOpenSubRequests()` (`lib/subRequests.ts`) gained an options bag -- `sort: "soonest" | "newest"` (default `"soonest"`) and `includePastDue` (default `false`) -- so the app-wide pool now defaults to soonest-needed-first with past-due requests hidden, both explicit opt-outs rather than silent, unescapable filtering. `GET /api/subs` and `/subs` gained matching `?sort=`/`?includePastDue=` query params and a small filter form, following `/campaigns`' own query-param filter-form convention. The campaign-page panel (`listSubRequestsForCampaign`) is untouched -- a single campaign's own request list doesn't grow large enough to need this. See the dated session log entry for the full design and a mid-session recovery from the mounted-vs-scratch-clone edit-path pitfall.

Cross-board search (self-identified, added 2026-09-08 -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #51, so this item was picked per this task's own fallback instructions for that case, from this task's own seed candidate list -- specifically the one named directly: "as the four boards (#37) accumulate threads, is there a way to search across them?"). Rationale: `/boards` and each board's own `/boards/[slug]` page can only be browsed one board at a time, newest-thread-first -- there's no way to find a relevant past thread without already knowing (or guessing) which of the four curated boards it lives on, and that only gets worse as threads accumulate. Feasibility: no new schema -- `board_threads`/`board_replies` already exist; this is a new read-only cross-board query mirroring `lib/campaigns.ts`'s existing `q` keyword-search convention, plus a `/boards/search` results page and a matching `GET /api/boards/search` route.~~ **Done 2026-09-08 (live session).** `searchBoards()` (`lib/boards.ts`) case-insensitive-substring-matches a query against a thread's own title/body, `escapeLikePattern`-escaped exactly like `listCampaigns()`'s `q` filter, plus a correlated `EXISTS` subquery against `board_replies` so a match buried in a reply still surfaces its parent thread (flagged via a new `matchedInThread` field so the UI can explain why a thread with no visible match in its own text showed up). `GET /api/boards/search` and a new `/boards/search` page (linked from `/boards`) serve it, both public like the rest of board browsing. An empty query returns zero results rather than every thread on every board, since this is a search, not a second browse view.


53. Accessibility pass across the app (self-identified, added 2026-09-08 -- it has surfaced as a seed candidate in several prior increments' own prompts -- alongside board search, sub-request sort/filter, and campaign filter-UI cleanup -- but was never actually picked, making it the most overdue self-identified candidate on offer once the numbered backlog cleared at #52). Rationale: icon-only buttons (round-btn pass/connect on the Discover swipe deck, kudos, delete/report) have inconsistent `aria-label` coverage, focus-visible states aren't uniformly present on every interactive control, the Discover deck's keyboard operability was never verified, and form labels/heading hierarchy have never had a systematic audit -- a real accessibility gap for a production app, not a cosmetic one. **Done 2026-09-08 (live session).** A systematic audit, not a redesign -- every fix is a markup/attribute change, no visual restyling. Found and fixed: (1) zero `focus-visible` styling existed anywhere in the app (confirmed via a full-codebase grep) -- rather than hand-adding a Tailwind `focus-visible:` class to every button/link/input across ~50 files (this app has no shared Button/Input component), added one global `:focus-visible` rule in `app/globals.css` covering every `a`/`button`/`input`/`select`/`textarea`/`[tabindex]`, light- and dark-mode aware, so a mouse click never shows a ring but real keyboard/AT navigation always does -- a single surgical change that reaches every current and future interactive element. (2) The star-rating buttons in `RatingsPanel.tsx`/`CampaignRatingPanel.tsx` (the task's own named reference implementation) turned out to only have a group-level `aria-label="Stars"` -- each individual star button's accessible name was just "★", so a screen reader user couldn't tell which star was which; added a per-button `aria-label` ("1 star"/"2 stars"/etc). (3) The session-log kudos button (`SessionLogPanel.tsx`) had `aria-pressed` and a `title` but no `aria-label`, so its accessible name was just the raw "👏 3" text; added an `aria-label` stating the action and count ("Give kudos (3 given)" / "Remove kudos (3 given)"). (4) `InitiativeTrackerPanel.tsx`'s move-up/move-down buttons already had `aria-label`s but they were generic ("Move up"/"Move down") with no indication of which combatant a screen-reader user tabbing through a list of several would be moving; made them per-entry ("Move Goblin Archer up"). (5) The Discover swipe deck (`DiscoverDeck.tsx`) was audited specifically for keyboard operability per the task's own framing -- it turned out to already be fully keyboard-operable (Pass/Start over are real `<button>`s, View & join is a real `<Link>`, no custom drag-only gesture underneath the "swipe" framing), so no changes were needed there beyond the new global focus ring. (6) A systematic input/textarea/select audit across all 22 files with form controls found roughly two dozen genuinely unlabeled fields -- bare `placeholder`-only or no text at all, not a real `<label>`/`aria-label` -- across `CampaignChatPanel`, `ConversationView`, `NewThreadForm`, `ReplyForm`, `ReportPostButton`, `PartyNotesPanel` (no placeholder at all), `ResourceVaultPanel` (both its add-form and inline edit-form), `NpcNotesPanel` (same), `SubRequestPanel` (the note, guardrails, and volunteer-message fields), `SubVolunteerAction`, `InitiativeTrackerPanel`'s inline edit-mode fields, `SessionLogPanel`'s new-entry and edit-entry textareas, and `CharacterManager`'s portrait-upload file input (whose only association was adjacent, non-`<label>` text "Or upload a picture" -- exactly the "looks like a label but isn't" gap the task called out); every one gained an `aria-label` matching its visible placeholder/context. The many other forms audited (campaign create/edit, sign-in, profile, browse filters, character forms) were already correctly using real `<label>` wrapping and needed no changes. (7) `app/settings/notifications/page.tsx`'s mute checkboxes had an accessible name of just "On"/"Muted" (the actual notification-type label lived in a sibling `<span>`, not inside the `<label>`) -- a screen reader tabbing through the list couldn't tell which type each checkbox controlled; added `aria-label={row.label}`. (8) `NavBar.tsx`'s three live unread-count badges (messages/notifications/table-chat) were bare numbers inside a `<span>` with no accessible context beyond the parent link's own text; added a `${count} unread` `aria-label` to each. (9) Heading hierarchy and landmark structure were audited across `/campaigns`, a campaign detail page, `/profile`, `/players/[id]`, `/boards`, and a few others (grepped every `<h1>`-`<h4>` in the codebase) -- found a consistently sane nesting already in place (exactly one `<h1>` per page, `<h2>` siblings for each feature panel, `<h3>` only nested under a `<h2>` for genuine sub-groupings like `/profile`'s "Running (as DM)"/"Playing in" under "My campaigns") and no skipped levels anywhere, so no changes were needed there. Also confirmed there are no non-semantic clickable `<div>`/`<span>` elements anywhere in the codebase (a grep-based check) -- every click handler in this app is already on a real `<button>` or `<Link>`, so there's no keyboard-trap risk of that kind to begin with. 516 tests, unchanged -- this is a presentational/markup-only pass with no new library-level logic to unit-test, the same convention backlog #21's Discover deck (and other UI-only passes) followed. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean. Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with fresh SQLite dbs, run across several `mcp__workspace__bash` calls (each self-contained per this sandbox's own backgrounded-process constraint): confirmed via the actual rendered HTML that the new `aria-label`s render correctly and dynamically -- e.g. the DM's own view of a session-log entry showed `aria-label="Give kudos (1 given)"`/`aria-pressed="false"` while the player who'd actually given kudos saw `aria-label="Remove kudos (1 given)"`/`aria-pressed="true"` on the same entry -- along with the table-chat, NPC-notes, resource-vault, and portrait-upload labels all present in the served HTML. Some of the newer labels (initiative-tracker edit fields, notification-settings checkboxes, live NavBar badges, the rating-stars editing view) sit behind this app's existing client-side data-fetch-then-render pattern and can't be observed via a plain `curl` fetch of the SSR shell (a pre-existing architectural fact about this app's client components, not something this pass introduced) -- those were instead verified by confirming each edit was a unique, successfully-applied string replacement plus a clean `tsc`/build/lint pass. Also found and fixed an unrelated environment issue while getting to a clean baseline: ~4GB of leftover per-test SQLite databases (`data/test-*.db*`, gitignored, never cleaned up by prior sessions) had filled the sandbox's `/sessions` disk to 100% and were causing `ENOSPC` test failures unrelated to any code change -- deleted, unblocking the test run; the disk sat at 55% free afterward. Server processes and all temp db/cookie/response files were cleaned up after every smoke-test run.

54. Home page introduction (owner-requested, added 2026-09-08). The landing page has no copy describing what Session Zero is or why it exists -- add an introduction/description section (what the site does, the in-person-first community goal behind it) for a first-time or signed-out visitor. **Done 2026-09-08 (nightly scheduled build).** Added a "What is Session Zero?" section to `/` -- a short description of the matchmaking + campaign-tracking model, a three-item "Find a table / Run your own / Keep it going" summary, and an explicit in-person-first framing -- shown only to signed-out visitors (`{!user && (...)}`), matching this item's own "for a first-time or signed-out visitor" framing. Copy/markup-only, no new schema or library logic; 516 tests unchanged. `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, and `npm test` all pass clean.

55. Curated-system resource links (owner-requested, added 2026-09-08; scoped 2026-09-08 per explicit product direction). Each of the five curated system hub pages (#36 -- 5e, Pathfinder 2e, Call of Cthulhu, Vampire: TM, PbtA/Blades) should link out to a hand-picked set of external resources for that system (e.g. for 5e: Wizards of the Coast, a DNDBeyond-style reference, Wikipedia). Scoped to the curated hub systems only -- not the long tail of free-text `system` values elsewhere in the app, which have no reliable resource set to link to.

56. D&D 5e character sheet (owner-requested, added 2026-09-08; scoped 2026-09-08 per explicit product direction). Characters (#15/#19/#26) currently carry the same simple shape (name, class-or-archetype, bio, backstory, portrait, status) for every system. For characters linked to a 5e campaign specifically, expand to the real 5e stat block: six ability scores + modifiers, proficiency bonus, saving throws, skills, AC, HP/hit dice, and an equipment list, plus spell slots where relevant. Scoped to 5e-specific fields only, alongside the existing system-agnostic fields -- other systems keep today's simpler model unchanged; generalizing to a template-driven sheet for other systems (Pathfinder, CoC, etc.) is a natural future item once this proves out, not attempted here.

57. Explicit weekly availability (owner-requested, added 2026-09-08; scoped 2026-09-08 per explicit product direction). `profiles.availability` (and the calendar-matching search from #27) currently uses named blocks (e.g. "evenings") that read differently to different people. Replace with a day + hour time-block grid -- specific clock-time ranges per day (e.g. "Tue 6-9pm") a user sets directly -- so availability means the same thing to every viewer. Needs a timezone-handling decision (store relative to the profile owner's own local time vs. UTC-normalized, and how it's displayed to a viewer in a different timezone) as part of the implementation.

58. Seed content for community boards (owner-requested, added 2026-09-08). The four discussion boards (#37) are currently empty until real users start posting. Pre-populate each board with a handful of commonly-requested starter threads (e.g. an FAQ-style thread on "New Player Questions," a few example posts on "Homebrew & House Rules") so a new visitor finds something useful on day one. Author the seed content under a clearly-labeled site/staff account (not impersonating a real user) -- the existing admin account from #48 is the natural fit.

59. Friends (owner-requested, added 2026-09-08; scoped 2026-09-08 per explicit product direction). On top of the existing 1:1 direct messaging (#31, already open to anyone) and one-way follow (#38), add a mutual friend relationship: send/accept/decline friend requests, a friends list, and some profile/activity content gated to friends-only rather than fully public. Review Instagram's own friend/follow model for shape, but scope stays to requests + list + friends-only visibility -- not a second messaging system (direct messaging already exists) and not a friends-specific activity feed (the existing public follow feed from #38 stays as-is; a friends-only feed variant is a possible future item, not this one).

Tooling notes (read this before re-discovering the same issues)
* This is Next.js 16 — the `create-next-app` scaffold drops a `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` pointer at the repo root telling you to read `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` before writing code, because App Router conventions keep changing. Confirmed still true this session: dynamic route `params` (and `searchParams`) are `Promise`s that must be `await`ed (`ctx: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }`), and `cookies()` from `next/headers` is async (`await cookies()`). "Cache Components" (`cacheComponents: true` in `next.config.ts`) is opt-in in Next 16, not default — this app doesn't enable it, so the pre-16 caching model applies and `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` on every route handler that touches cookies/the DB is enough (no `"use cache"` directives needed).
* ESLint flat config for `eslint-config-next@16`: current `create-next-app` scaffolds `eslint.config.mjs` using `defineConfig`/`globalIgnores` from the `eslint/config` package plus `eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals` and `eslint-config-next/typescript` sub-path imports — this shape works out of the box with the `eslint@9` that `create-next-app` itself installs; no manual workaround was needed this session (earlier sessions' notes about `eslint-config-next`'s default export shape and pinning `eslint` below `10.x` may be stale for this scaffold version — re-verify if `npx eslint .` breaks on a fresh install).
* `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`: a `useEffect` whose body calls a `load()` function declared at component scope (needed when a button handler must also trigger the same reload) gets flagged even though `load()` only sets state after an internal `await`. Fix is a scoped `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` directly above the `load()` call — **the disable comment must be the line immediately before the flagged call**; multi-line explanatory comments have to go *above* the disable directive, not between it and the code, or the directive silently doesn't attach (ESLint then reports both "unused disable directive" and the original error). `NavBar`'s polling effect avoids the whole issue by declaring its loader function inside the effect body itself.
* `next build` type-checking test files: `tsconfig.json`'s default `include` picks up every `**/*.ts`/`**/*.tsx`, which pulls Vitest test files into the Next.js build's TypeScript pass and fails it. Fix: add `"__tests__/**"` and `"vitest.config.ts"` to tsconfig's `"exclude"` array.
* `next.config.ts` cannot carry an `eslint` key in this Next.js version — ESLint config now lives solely in `eslint.config.mjs`.
* `@types/react-dom` doesn't publish a version matching `react-dom`'s own version number — let npm resolve `@types/react`/`@types/react-dom` against their own `^19` ranges rather than pinning to the runtime package's exact version.
* `next/font/google` requires outbound access to `fonts.googleapis.com` at build time — fails hard in a network-restricted sandbox. Use system font stacks (or `next/font/local` with self-hosted font files) instead.
* HTTP integration test spawning a real server: spawn the **local** `next` binary directly (`node_modules/.bin/next start`), not via `npx next start` — `npx` forks a wrapper process that doesn't reliably forward a kill signal on to the actual `next-server` child, which left orphaned servers holding the spare port open between test runs (`EADDRINUSE` on the next run). Spawn with `detached: true` and kill the whole process group with `process.kill(-server.pid, "SIGTERM")` in `afterAll`.
* Same-millisecond `ORDER BY created_at DESC` ties need a secondary sort key — see backlog item #14 above.
* Per-test-file isolated SQLite databases: `lib/db.ts` reads `process.env.SQLITE_DB_PATH` at module-load time (falling back to `data/session-zero.db` for the real app). `__tests__/setup.ts` is wired in via `vitest.config.ts`'s `setupFiles` and assigns a fresh `data/test-<uuid>.db` path before that test file's own imports resolve — Vitest isolates each test file's module registry, so this gives every test *file* (not every individual test) its own DB. The HTTP integration test manages its own separate DB file explicitly (passed via `env` to the spawned `next build`/`next start` child processes).
* `package.json` needs `"type": "module"` for `vitest.config.ts`'s `export default` to load natively without a CJS-compat warning; harmless with `next build`/`next lint`/the test suite since every source file already uses ESM syntax.
* `vitest.config.ts` should use `path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))` instead of bare `__dirname`, which Vite's native config loader warns is unsupported and will eventually be removed.

Testing
516 tests total: 508 unit tests across `__tests__/unit/*.test.ts` for the business logic in `lib/` (real auth — signup validation, duplicate-email rejection, sign-in success/failure, session token lifecycle; campaign CRUD + capacity-floor validation + browse/filter/sort/paginate, membership join/approve/decline/leave + auto-fill/auto-reopen/manual reopen, the private-access boundary function + active-party-membership helper, notification create/fan-out/read tracking plus the notificationEvents pub/sub publishing an unread-count update on notify()/markRead()/markAllRead() and only to the matching user's channel, per-notification-type preference get/set/default behavior plus enforcement at notification-creation time, session log CRUD, party notes + its notify-on-edit fan-out including a muted-preference case, UTC-safe schedule-status computation including a UTC-midnight boundary case, free-text keyword campaign search across title/description/system (case-insensitivity, ANDing with the exact `system` filter, and `%`/`_` being treated as literal characters rather than SQL `LIKE` wildcards), and player profiles (default-empty profile for a new user, save/re-read with whitespace trimming, partial updates, length-limit rejection, per-user isolation, splitting the stored preferred-systems string for display, and the "my campaigns" DMing/playing lists including exclusion of pending/declined/left memberships)), and characters (deterministic default-avatar assignment from a curated set, trimming, blank-name rejection, per-field length-limit rejection, an unrecognized avatarEmoji value falling back to a valid default, per-user listing newest-first and isolated from other users' characters, partial updates, ownership enforcement rejecting another user's edit/delete attempts, and — merged in from the campaign-scoped design — creating a character pre-linked to a campaign the owner is DMing or an approved member of, rejecting a link to a campaign the owner has no access to or that doesn't exist, defaulting to unlinked, linking/relinking/unlinking via update, rejecting a relink to an inaccessible campaign while leaving the existing link intact, an omitted campaignId on update leaving the existing link untouched, and campaign-scoped listing returning only that campaign's characters oldest-first), and ratings (a player rating the DM and the DM rating a player, rejecting player-to-player/self/stranger ratings, rejecting an out-of-range star value and a tag that doesn't belong to the ratee's role, upserting a second rating instead of duplicating, still allowing a rating after the player has left, listing the right rating targets for a DM vs. a player, an empty target list for someone with no relationship to the campaign, a brand-new user reporting as unrated rather than zero, averaging multiple ratings with per-role tag tallies, and the rating_prompt notification firing to both sides on leave), and session log kudos (an active party member toggling kudos on/off an entry, kudos count and the viewer's own given/not-given state being correct per viewer, the session_log_kudos notification firing to the entry's author on a fresh kudos from someone else but not on self-kudos or on un-giving, and a stranger with no relationship to the campaign being rejected), and stats (all-zero/empty/null for a brand-new user, campaigns-DMed plus sessionsRun counted correctly, campaigns-played plus sessionsPlayed counted correctly and still counting entries posted after the player left, characters-created counted, distinct systems and the most-played system derived correctly across both DM and player roles, and the campaign with the most session log entries picked as the longest campaign), and character legacies/campaign chronicle (a new character defaulting to active status with an empty epilogue, creating directly as retired/fallen with an epilogue, updating status and epilogue independently of other fields, rejecting an over-length epilogue, an unrecognized status value falling back to the current/default status, chronicle counts aggregating correctly by status among currently-linked characters and excluding unlinked ones, a character dropping out of the chronicle once unlinked, a new campaign defaulting to a null danger level, the DM setting/changing/clearing the danger level, and an omitted danger level on update leaving it untouched), and coarse location (a profile's default empty location, saving/re-reading it, rejecting an over-length value, a campaign defaulting to an empty location and having it set on create/update, and browse-page filtering by a case-insensitive substring match on location including a no-match case), and character portrait upload (a new character defaulting to a null portrait, creating with a valid portrait data URL, rejecting a non-image or malformed data URL, rejecting an over-length data URL, the owner setting/replacing/removing a portrait via update, and an existing portrait staying untouched when omitted from an update), and weekly availability (an empty grid for a brand-new user, saving and reading back slots in day-then-block order, a second save fully replacing rather than merging the grid, clearing the grid with an empty array, de-duplicating repeated day/block pairs, rejecting an out-of-range day and an unrecognized block, and per-user grid isolation), and sub requests (the DM or an approved member posting a request defaulting to open, rejecting a post from someone with no relationship to the campaign, rejecting an over-length note, open counts, a stranger volunteering and viewerHasVolunteered reflecting correctly per-viewer, rejecting the requester volunteering for their own request, rejecting volunteering on a non-open request, a Session log
2026-08-29 through 2026-09-01: see prior entries in this file's git history for the full narrative (initial build-out through the 6a/6b/6d notification work). Summary: placeholder sign-in, campaign CRUD, browse/filter/sort/paginate, join-request + approve/decline flow with auto-fill/auto-reopen/manual-reopen, leave-campaign flow, UTC-safe scheduling, the private-side boundary (session log, party notes) with unit + HTTP-integration regression coverage, in-app notifications with 6a/6b fan-out completions, and 6d per-notification-type preferences — all established across several from-scratch rebuilds in the ephemeral cloud sandbox, each one starting by reading this file since nothing else survived between sessions.

2026-09-02 (tonight)
Rebuilt the app from scratch in a fresh cloud sandbox to parity with the 2026-09-01 spec (68 tests: 64 unit + 4 integration; build/lint/test all clean; manual smoke test of `/`, `/campaigns`, `/signin`, and a 404 case against a spare-port production server). Then solved backlog item #1 for real, at the owner's request, using this session's link to their desktop app:
* Confirmed the desktop bridge reaches "bill-25-laptop" and requested access to `C:\Users\billb\Session-zero` (a plain, non-OneDrive path — the OneDrive-redirected `Documents` folder couldn't be granted, likely because it's a symlink/junction the bridge won't follow).
* Found the bridge's shell (`device_bash`) runs inside an isolated Ubuntu 22.04 VM on the laptop, not the user's actual Windows environment — so no pre-existing git/GitHub credentials were available there either. It does have real outbound internet access (unlike the cloud sandbox) and no `sudo`.
* Installed the GitHub CLI from the official release tarball into `$HOME/bin` (no root needed).
* Had the owner generate a GitHub personal access token and drop it into a temporary `github_token.txt` file inside the connected folder (rather than pasting it into the chat transcript), read it once, configured `git` (`user.name`/`user.email`, `credential.helper store`, a `~/.git-credentials` entry using the token), verified `git ls-remote` against `https://github.com/Billbiller/Session-zero.git` succeeded, then deleted the token file (had to request one-time delete permission on the folder first — `device_bash` can't delete inside mounted folders by default).
* Cloned the repo (`C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo`) — found just the auto-generated README, confirming no app code had ever reached GitHub before.
* Copied tonight's freshly-built app from the cloud workspace into that checkout, wrote this progress file, and made the first real commit + push.
Net effect: backlog item #1 is resolved. Future sessions should work directly in the laptop checkout (via the desktop bridge) rather than rebuilding from scratch in the cloud sandbox each time — the whole reason this file existed as a rebuild-from-spec crutch no longer applies once history genuinely persists. This file is kept going forward as ordinary project documentation, not as a memory workaround.

Later the same night: attempted to set up a recurring nightly scheduled task (cloud Routine with `requires_local_device: true`, "every night at 10pm PST, do the next backlog item or, if the app is fully functional and tested, propose and build the next customer-facing feature") so this work would happen automatically instead of in ad hoc chat sessions. This surfaced a real gap in the tooling: creating the trigger only ever reports `no_signed_approval — this task will run in the cloud only`; the actual device-binding approval is a separate step in the desktop app's own Scheduled Tasks UI, invisible to (and uncontrollable from) the session that created the trigger. After one accidental delete-via-settings and a recreate, the owner completed that approval in the app UI and confirmed via screenshots it was bound correctly (Active, "Only on this computer", "Always allowed — Automatically approve", next run ~10:00 PM). A manual "Run now" test still failed with "the device bridge to the laptop isn't connected" even though this chat session's own bridge was live at that moment — so the bound task's own device-connection step is not yet reliable, for reasons not yet root-caused (possibly a timing/liveness check that's stricter than what this session's tools see). Per the owner's instruction, the fallback for now is: keep doing the "nightly" increment manually, in a live chat session like this one, rather than relying on the scheduled task, until its reliability is confirmed. Revisit the automated schedule later rather than continuing to debug it blind tonight.
Then did tonight's actual increment by hand: implemented backlog item #2, real authentication, end to end (see "What the app does today" and the backlog above) — added `bcryptjs`/`@types/bcryptjs`, rewrote `lib/auth.ts` and the two `/api/auth/*` routes, rewrote the `/signin` page with a sign-in/create-account UI, migrated `users` to add `password_hash`, and updated every test that used to call the old placeholder `findOrCreateUser` to go through real signup instead (68 → 75 tests, all green; build and lint both clean). Transferred the changes from the cloud workspace to the laptop checkout and pushed to `main` (see commit referenced in this same push).

2026-09-02 (nightly scheduled build)
This was the first run of the new automated nightly scheduled task (the "device bridge" approach mentioned above was replaced today with a mounted-folder + GitHub-token setup — see the SETUP note in the task definition). Ran fully unattended per that task's instructions: no clarifying questions, judgment calls documented here instead.

Picked backlog item #6e (replace unread-badge polling with live push) as the night's work: it's the next unblocked item in priority order — item #3 (deployment decision) is explicitly a human decision, not a coding task, and item #6c (email notifications) is explicitly blocked on SMTP credentials that don't exist. Chose Server-Sent Events over a websocket: this app already speaks plain HTTP/fetch everywhere, SSE needs no new dependency or protocol upgrade handling, and the actual requirement (server -> client one-way "your unread count changed" push) doesn't need websockets' bidirectionality. Documented as SSE, not "websocket/SSE", in the backlog going forward so this reads as a deliberate choice rather than an open question.

Implementation: `lib/notificationEvents.ts` (new) is a single in-process `EventEmitter` pub/sub keyed by user id. `lib/notifications.ts`'s `notify()`, `markRead()`, and `markAllRead()` each publish the caller's fresh unread count after writing. `app/api/notifications/stream/route.ts` (new) is an authenticated `GET` SSE endpoint (`export const runtime = "nodejs"` — it touches cookies and better-sqlite3, both of which need Node, not edge) that sends an immediate snapshot on connect, streams `event: unread` messages as they're published, and sends a heartbeat comment every 20s. `components/NavBar.tsx` now opens an `EventSource` to it and falls back to the original 15s polling if `EventSource` is unavailable or the connection errors — no reconnect/backoff logic was written for the SSE side itself (`EventSource`'s built-in auto-reconnect covers transient drops; a hard, repeated failure is treated as "fall back to polling for this page load" rather than retried indefinitely, a scope cut to keep this a one-night change). Added a `notificationEvents.test.ts` unit suite (publish-on-notify/markRead/markAllRead, per-user channel isolation, muted-type suppression) and one new HTTP integration test that opens a real SSE connection, reads the initial snapshot off the wire, triggers a join-approval, and asserts the push arrives without polling. 75 -> 80 tests, all green; build and lint both clean.

Judgment call / process note worth flagging: this session's tools mount the shared folder at two different paths — a Windows-style path for the Read/Write/Edit/Grep/Glob tools (which is the *live* `C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo` checkout, i.e. the one the owner browses on their laptop) and a separate `/sessions/.../mnt/Session-zero` path for the bash sandbox, where `~/build/session-zero` (the actual git scratch clone used for all git operations, per the task's own setup instructions) is a completely different directory. Early in this session the notification-stream code was written with Write/Edit directly against the Windows-style path — i.e. directly into the *live* mounted checkout — before noticing it, rather than into the scratch clone as instructed. Caught it when `next build` in the scratch clone didn't pick up the new route. Recovered by: copying the correct file contents from the mounted checkout into the scratch clone (so the scratch clone, where build/lint/test/commit actually happen, has the real changes); reverting the three pre-existing files that had been edited in place in the mounted checkout (`lib/notifications.ts`, `components/NavBar.tsx`, `__tests__/integration/http.test.ts`) back to their last-committed content via `git show HEAD:<path>` from the scratch clone; but two brand-new files created directly in the mounted checkout (`lib/notificationEvents.ts`, `app/api/notifications/stream/route.ts`) could not be deleted from there — file deletion in that mounted folder returned "Operation not permitted", and the interactive delete-permission tool isn't usable in this unattended run. Net effect: the mounted checkout now has 2 leftover untracked files (content-identical to what's in the real commit) sitting alongside its otherwise-unchanged tracked files. Step 8 (sync the mounted checkout after pushing) will see this as a dirty working tree and its own logic will skip the copy-over rather than risk clobbering something — see this session's final summary for what that means for the owner's local folder, and consider deleting those two stray files by hand (or just `git status`/`git clean` there, if that folder's own git can be trusted again) so future runs start from a clean mounted checkout.

2026-09-02 (backlog review, ad hoc session)
Owner shared two source materials for broader context: the original Claude.ai design chat that produced the "Session Zero" concept (swipe-to-match social-network framing, system-agnostic, freemium, plus a string of follow-on features added over that conversation: ratings, session logbook, character legacies/campaign chronicle, substitute players, market/competitive analysis, and cost/business modeling), and the resulting static HTML prototype (`session-zero (4).html`) with no backend. Read both in full and reconciled them against this repo's actual state: the real app (this repo) already covers the core matchmaking/campaign-lifecycle loop the prototype never implemented (real auth, campaign CRUD, join/approve/decline, private session log/notes/schedule, notifications), while the prototype has several social/reputation/retention features this repo doesn't yet have. Folded the gap into the backlog as items 15-25 above, in priority order, each with a one-line rationale and an explicit feasibility read — flagging freemium/payments (23) and native App Store distribution (24) as blocked on business decisions and credentials this session doesn't have, rather than coding gaps. No application code was changed this session — this was a planning/documentation update only.

Picked backlog item #7 (free-text/keyword campaign search) as the night's work: item #3 (deployment decision) is an explicit human decision, not a coding task; item #6c (email notifications) is explicitly blocked on SMTP credentials that don't exist; #7 was next in priority order and a clean, one-night scope.

Implementation: `lib/campaigns.ts`'s `listCampaigns()` gained a `q` option — when present, it's trimmed, lowercased, and matched with `LIKE` against `LOWER(title)`, `LOWER(description)`, and `LOWER(system)` (OR'd together), ANDed with the existing `system` exact-match filter and the cancelled-campaign exclusion. A new `escapeLikePattern()` helper escapes `%`, `_`, and the escape character itself before the value is wrapped in `%...%`, with `ESCAPE '\'` on each `LIKE` clause, so a keyword containing `%` or `_` is treated as a literal substring rather than a SQL wildcard — judgment call: worth the small extra complexity since campaign titles/descriptions are free text a DM could plausibly type either character into (e.g. "50% off", "session_zero"), and silently treating them as wildcards would make search results wrong in a hard-to-debug way. `GET /api/campaigns` and the `/campaigns` server component both thread a `q` query param through (page links preserve it across pagination). Chose AND (not OR) semantics between `q` and `system`: `system` remains a precise filter for a player who already knows the exact system string from browsing, while `q` is the fuzzy/keyword layer — combining them narrows rather than broadens, which matches how the two inputs read in the UI (a "Search" box plus a separate "System" box, not a single merged field). Added a "Search" text input to the `/campaigns` filter form, and adjusted the empty-state copy to distinguish "no campaigns match yet" (nothing posted) from "no campaigns match your search" (a filter/keyword excluded everything).

Testing: added two new unit tests to `__tests__/unit/campaigns.test.ts` — one covering matches against each of title/description/system, case-insensitivity, the AND-with-`system` combination (including a case that should miss), and a no-match case; one specifically covering `%`/`_` as literal characters. Both new tests use highly-distinctive keyword prefixes (`kwzx7...`) after the first attempt collided with an earlier test's fixture data in the same file (that file's tests share one SQLite DB across `it` blocks per Vitest's per-file module isolation, not per-test — a keyword search is far more collision-prone against leftover fixture data than the exact-match tests elsewhere in this file, which already used `Unique ...` prefixes for the same reason). 80 -> 82 tests, all green; build and lint both clean.

No email/SMTP work attempted (backlog #6c remains blocked, as documented). No changes made outside `lib/campaigns.ts`, `app/api/campaigns/route.ts`, `app/campaigns/page.tsx`, and `__tests__/unit/campaigns.test.ts`.

2026-09-06 (live session, backlog #8: player profiles)
Owner asked to run the next phase of development in a live chat session (not the nightly unattended task) right after fixing last night's push-permission blocker. Picked backlog item #8, next in priority order after #7 (item #3 is a human deployment decision; #6c is blocked on SMTP credentials that still don't exist).

Data model: a new `profiles` table (`user_id` PK/FK to `users`, `bio`, `preferred_systems`, `availability` all TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '', `updated_at`). Judgment call: stored `preferred_systems` as one free-text comma-separated string rather than a normalized child table — this app already has real per-system data via campaigns' own `system` field for matching/filtering purposes, so a player's "preferred systems" is closer to a personal-bio field than a structured taxonomy; a simple string keeps the UI to one text input and avoids over-building before there's a real need to query "find players who prefer X." `lib/profiles.ts` mirrors the `notification_preferences` pattern already in this codebase: `getProfile()` returns an implicit empty default when no row exists yet (no row is written until a user actually saves), `upsertProfile()` does a partial update (only overwrites fields actually passed in) with trimming and a length cap on each field (2000 chars bio, 300 chars each for preferred-systems and availability), and `splitPreferredSystems()` turns the stored string into a clean array for chip-style display. Also added `myCampaigns(userId)`, returning `{ dming, playing }` — `dming` is every campaign where the user is `dm_id`; `playing` is campaigns where the user has an `approved`-status membership (pending/declined/left are correctly excluded, including a membership that *was* approved and then left). Both queries use the existing `ORDER BY ... , rowid` tie-break convention from this file's tooling notes (#14) for same-millisecond timestamps.

Routes: `GET`/`PUT /api/profile` (auth required; GET also returns `dming`/`playing` so the profile page is a single round trip) and a public, unauthenticated `app/players/[id]/page.tsx` showing another user's bio/preferred-systems/availability read-only (404 via `notFound()` for an unknown id). `app/profile/page.tsx` is a client component following this repo's existing pattern (see `NotificationSettingsPage`/`NotificationsPage`) — fetch-on-mount, redirect to `/signin` on a 401 rather than rendering an empty/broken form. Linked from the NavBar (clicking your own display name now goes to `/profile`, replacing the old plain-text name) and from every DM/roster display name on the campaign browse list, campaign detail header, and `RosterPanel` (all now link to `/players/[id]`).

Judgment call on scope: kept the public `/players/[id]` view to bio/preferred-systems/availability only — did not also expose a stranger's own "my campaigns" DMing/playing list there. A campaign's DM is already public (shown right on the campaign itself), so linking to a DM's profile doesn't leak anything new, but showing a full list of every campaign a random player has joined felt like it exposes more about a stranger's activity than this feature needs to for a first pass; that's a separate, deliberate product decision to revisit later rather than default into it. Availability was included on the public view (not just the self-editable one) since surfacing it to other players is the actual point of a matchmaking app — the field exists for the person's own campaign-mates and interested DMs to see, not just for their own reference.

Testing: added `__tests__/unit/profiles.test.ts` (9 tests) covering the default-empty-profile case, save/re-read with trimming, partial updates, length-limit rejection on all three fields, per-user isolation, `splitPreferredSystems()`, and `myCampaigns()` including the pending/declined/left-exclusion case. Also ran an ad hoc manual smoke test against a real `next start` server on a spare port (not part of the committed test suite, just a live sanity check before committing): unauthenticated `GET /api/profile` → 401; signup → default empty profile; `PUT` → save; re-`GET` → values persisted; an oversized bio → 400; the public `/players/[id]` page rendered the saved bio/systems/availability and 404'd for an unknown id; and the campaign browse page's DM name rendered as a working `/players/<id>` link after posting a campaign. 82 → 91 tests, all green; build and lint both clean.

No changes to email/SMTP (backlog #6c, still blocked) or to the deployment decision (#3, still a human call).

2026-09-06 (nightly scheduled build)
Ran fully unattended per the nightly-build task's instructions: no clarifying questions, judgment calls documented here instead.
