Caught it immediately (before any build/test ran) by noticing the edit tool's own confirmation referenced the Windows-style path rather than the scratch clone. Recovered cleanly this time, unlike the 2026-09-02 incident: since `lib/notifications.ts` hadn't been touched in the mounted folder before this session, its pre-edit content was still identical to the scratch clone's `git show HEAD:lib/notifications.ts` — copied that known-good original back over the mounted file via the bash sandbox (`cp`, not the Edit tool) and confirmed with `diff` that the mounted checkout was byte-for-byte reverted to its last-committed state before continuing, then redid the same edit correctly in the scratch clone via a Python heredoc. Double-checked `lib/db.ts` and `lib/types.ts` for the same kind of accidental drift and confirmed both matched their last-committed content exactly (they'd only ever been touched via bash, never via Edit/Write) — no lasting effect on the mounted folder from this mistake, but flagging again since this is now the second and third time (2026-09-02, and twice in a row within this same slip) this exact tool-target confusion has happened; future sessions should treat "which path did the Edit/Write tool just confirm?" as a mandatory sanity check on the very first source-file edit of a session, not just a documented pitfall to remember.

Also hit a transient issue unrelated to the tool-path mistake: an early background `next start` server (launched with `nohup ... & disown` in one bash call, intending to curl it from a *later*, separate bash call) was unreachable by the time the next tool call ran — this sandbox's bash tool documents that "each bash call is independent (no cwd/env carryover)," which turns out to also mean backgrounded processes don't survive between calls, not just cwd/env state. Fixed by running the entire smoke test (start server, sleep, every curl call, kill server) inside a single bash invocation instead of splitting server-start and server-use across separate calls.

Testing: new `__tests__/unit/messages.test.ts` (11 tests) covering: sending a message and confirming it's trimmed; rejecting messaging yourself; rejecting a message to a nonexistent recipient; rejecting an empty/whitespace-only message; rejecting an over-length (>4000 char) message; a fresh message firing a `message_received` notification whose `related_user_id` is the sender's id; that notification being suppressed when the recipient has muted the type (confirming `notify()`'s new optional parameter doesn't bypass the existing preference-check choke point); a full thread reading back identically in chronological order regardless of which participant asks; the inbox ordering conversations most-recently-active-first with correct per-conversation unread counts (including the asymmetric case — the sender of the *last* message in a thread has 0 unread on their own side, the recipient has ≥1 until they read it); conversation-list isolation (a stranger with no messages sees an empty inbox); and marking a conversation read clearing only the read-direction's unread count while leaving the other participant's own unread count for their own unread reply untouched. 208 → 219 tests, all green; `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port 4501, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside one bash call and drove it with three independent cookie jars (Alice, Bob, Carl-the-stranger). Confirmed, in order: signed-out `GET /api/messages` → 401; a fresh signup's inbox is empty; Alice successfully messages Bob (201, trimmed body persisted); Alice messaging herself → 400; an empty/whitespace body → 400 (zod); messaging a nonexistent user id → 400 with "Recipient not found"; Bob's inbox shows the new conversation with `unreadCount: 1`; Bob opening the conversation returns the message and the correct `viewerId`/`otherUser`; Bob's inbox immediately after shows `unreadCount: 0` (confirming the mark-read-on-open side effect); Bob replies (201); Alice's notifications list now contains a `message_received` entry with `related_user_id` equal to Bob's id and message text "Bob sent you a message."; Carl (uninvolved) has an empty inbox; the rendered `/players/[id]` HTML for Bob (viewed as Alice) contains a "Message" link; and `GET /api/messages/<nonexistent-id>` → 404. Cleaned up the temp SQLite db, build log, and cookie jars afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). Next unblocked item in priority order is #32 (table group chat), which this session's own backlog text notes explicitly depends on #31's message data model existing first — it now does.

2026-09-07 (live session, backlog #32: table group chat)
Continuing the same-day chain of one-item-at-a-time increments. Picked backlog item #32, the next unblocked item in priority order — it explicitly depended on #31's messaging infrastructure (or at least the same underlying data model), which now exists; #3 (deployment decision) and #6c (email/SMTP) remain blocked on human decisions/missing credentials as documented throughout this file.

Design questions resolved before coding, per this item's own backlog text:
* **Data model.** A new `campaign_messages` table, deliberately reusing #31's `messages` table shape (id, sender_id, body, created_at) but scoped to `campaign_id` instead of a `recipient_id` — the backlog text itself flagged this as "almost certainly right." No `read` column on the message row this time, unlike `messages.read` — a group thread's "have I read this" state is naturally per-user-per-thread, not per-message-per-recipient, so it lives in a separate new table instead (`campaign_message_reads`: `campaign_id`, `user_id`, `last_read_at`, upserted). This both drives the per-user unread count and doubles as the input to the notification-throttling rule below.
* **Who can post/read.** Exactly `hasPrivateAccess` (DM + approved active members) — the boundary this backlog item's own text named explicitly, and the same one already gating party notes and the session log. A member who leaves loses read/write access to the chat immediately, same as the rest of the private side, but their own past messages stay visible in the thread's history for the remaining party (history isn't rewritten just because someone left) — verified with a dedicated test.
* **Notification strategy** (the open question this backlog item's own text explicitly flagged for judgment). Rejected "notify on every message" as clearly too noisy — this app's other fan-out notifications (`party_notes_updated`, `session_log_posted`) fire on events that happen rarely (an edit, a session recap), while an active table chat could see many messages in a row in one sitting; notifying on each one would make the notification inbox useless for anything else. Also rejected "no notification type at all, rely on the existing unread-badge/inbox pattern" — unlike party notes and the session log, which render their own visible content inline on a page the party already visits, a chat message has no other surface prompting a party member to go check the campaign page. Landed on a middle path: added `campaign_chat_message` to `NOTIFICATION_TYPES`/`NOTIFICATION_LABELS` (so it goes through the exact same `notify()`/mute/SSE-push machinery as everything else, no new plumbing), but a party member is only notified when a new message is the *first* unread one for them since their last visit — computed by checking `getUnreadCampaignMessageCount(campaignId, recipientId) === 0` immediately before inserting the new message. This means a burst of messages produces exactly one notification (not N), and reading the thread (which upserts `last_read_at` to now) re-arms the next notification. Verified directly: three messages sent back-to-back before the recipient checks in produce exactly one `campaign_chat_message` notification each for the DM and the other player; after the recipient reads the thread, the next message notifies them again.

Implementation: `lib/db.ts` gained the `campaign_messages` and `campaign_message_reads` tables (both brand-new tables, so no `ALTER TABLE` migration was needed — unlike #31's `related_user_id` column addition to the pre-existing `notifications` table). `lib/types.ts` gained `CampaignMessage`/`CampaignMessageWithSender` (the enriched, sender-name-attached shape client components actually consume, matching the existing client-safe-types convention used by `ConversationSummary`) and the new `campaign_chat_message` notification type/label. `lib/campaignMessages.ts` (new) exports `listCampaignMessages` (oldest-first, enriched with `senderName` via `getUserById`), `getUnreadCampaignMessageCount` (excludes the viewer's own messages), `markCampaignChatRead` (upsert), and `sendCampaignMessage` (validates `hasPrivateAccess`, non-blank, ≤4000 chars — same length cap as #31's `messages.body` for consistency — trims, inserts, marks the *sender* caught-up too so their own message never shows as unread to themselves, then fans out the throttled notification).

Routes: `app/api/campaigns/[id]/chat/route.ts` (new) — `GET` pre-checks `hasPrivateAccess` explicitly before calling `listCampaignMessages` (mirroring `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/notes`, since neither read helper does its own auth) and marks the viewer caught-up as a side effect of opening it, same mark-read-on-open convention as `GET /api/messages/[userId]`; `POST` validates the request body shape with zod (400 on empty/over-length) and then relies on `sendCampaignMessage`'s own internal `hasPrivateAccess` check, caught and mapped to 403 (mirroring `PUT /api/campaigns/[id]/notes` relying on `updateNotes`'s internal check the same way) — the resulting split (zod owns input-shape validation → 400, the lib's access check → 403) matches how this app already separates those two concerns elsewhere.

UI: `components/CampaignChatPanel.tsx` (new, self-fetching client component wrapped in `Section`, following `PartyNotesPanel`/`ConversationView`'s load-on-mount-plus-reload-after-send pattern) renders messages as left/right-aligned chat bubbles (right-aligned + shaded for the viewer's own messages, matching `ConversationView`'s exact styling, with the sender's display name shown above anyone else's bubble since — unlike a 1:1 thread — there's more than one possible "other" sender here) plus a composer. Added to `app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx` inside the existing `{access && (...)}` block, between `ScheduleForm` and `PartyNotesPanel` — so it's gated behind the exact same private-access boundary as everything else in that block, with zero new access-control code needed on the page itself.

Testing: new `__tests__/unit/campaignMessages.test.ts` (13 tests) covering: the DM and active members posting and reading the thread oldest-first with sender names attached; rejecting a post from a stranger, from a still-pending (not-yet-approved) requester, and from a member who has since left; a departed member's own past messages still appearing in the thread's history; rejecting an empty/whitespace-only message and an over-length one; the rest of the active party (not the sender) receiving a `campaign_chat_message` notification; that notification respecting a muted preference; the "only the first unread message notifies" rule (three messages in a row producing exactly one notification each for two different recipients); a fresh notification firing again after a recipient catches up (`markCampaignChatRead`) and a new message arrives; per-user unread counts excluding the viewer's own messages and resetting to zero after catching up; and per-campaign thread isolation (a message in one campaign not leaking into another campaign's `listCampaignMessages`). 219 → 232 tests, all green; `npx tsc --noEmit` (after `next build`, since a fresh `.next/types` directory is needed for `app/layout.tsx`'s generated `LayoutProps` type either way — confirmed this is a pre-existing, unrelated gap by checking it against `git stash`, not something this session's changes caused), `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port 4511, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside a single bash call (per this file's own tooling note about backgrounded processes not surviving across separate bash calls in this sandbox) and drove it with five independent cookie jars (DM, an approved member "Alice," an approved-then-left member "Leaver," a stranger, and a still-pending requester "Pending"). Confirmed, in order: signed-out `GET .../chat` → 401; a stranger's `GET`/`POST` → 403; a pending (not-yet-approved) requester's `GET` → 403; the DM posting a message → 201; a stranger's `POST` → 403 with the expected error message; an empty/whitespace body → 400 (zod); Alice's `GET` returns the DM's message with the correct `senderName` and marks her caught-up; Alice's notifications inbox contains exactly one `campaign_chat_message` notification after the DM's first post; after the DM posts two more messages in a row without Alice checking back, her notification count for that type is still only 2 total (not 3) — confirming the throttle; the Leaver leaving the campaign (`POST .../leave`) then getting 403 on both `GET` and `POST` to the chat afterward; and the DM's final `GET` showing all three messages in the correct oldest-first order. Rather than discard and redo from scratch, read every changed/new file in full against this repo's own conventions (the `DmControls`/`ScheduleForm` DM-only pattern, `lib/*.ts` + thin `app/api/*/route.ts` + self-fetching client component layering, `requireUser()`/`errorResponse()`, zod validation, `Section.tsx` wrapping, the `hasPrivateAccess`/hasPrivateAccess-adjacent access-boundary convention, and this file's own test-density style) before treating it as this session's work: it matched every one of those conventions closely enough that it reads as if built by an earlier increment of this same ongoing process, just not yet taken through the build/lint/test/commit/push/smoke-test/documentation steps this task requires. Verified rather than assumed: read `lib/initiativeTracker.ts` and `lib/npcNotes.ts` end to end, both test files end to end, both route trees, both components, and the exact diffs to `lib/access.ts`/`lib/db.ts`/`lib/types.ts`/`app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx` via `git diff`, then ran the full verification pipeline below myself rather than trusting it was already green.

Design as implemented (confirmed against the backlog line's own framing): an initiative tracker is a per-campaign ordered list of combatant entries (`initiative_entries` table: name, a numeric `initiative` value, a free-text `hp` field like "18/24" rather than a bare integer -- matching how loosely this app already treats similar "current status" text elsewhere, e.g. `characters.epilogue` -- and free-text `notes`). The running order (`order_index`) is maintained explicitly by the DM via move-up/move-down swaps with the adjacent entry, independent of each entry's own `initiative` value -- entries are never auto-sorted by initiative, and there's no drag-and-drop, matching the backlog line's own "basics" scope. NPC quick-notes (`npc_notes` table: name + free-text notes) use the exact name+notes-pair shape the backlog line itself called for, with no separate "quick notes" concept layered on top. Both tables persist as "current state" between requests/sessions -- like party notes, nothing resets automatically (this app has no discrete "session" entity to reset against); the DM clears the initiative tracker explicitly via a "Clear all" action (`DELETE /api/campaigns/[id]/initiative`, distinct from removing one entry at a time) and deletes NPCs one at a time.

Access-boundary judgment call (the real design decision this backlog line left open): both tools are DM-only, not shared with the rest of the party the way session log/party notes/schedule/chat are. Implemented as a new, narrower `isDm(userId, campaignId)` predicate in `lib/access.ts` (true only when `campaign.dm_id === userId`), kept alongside the existing wider `hasPrivateAccess()` (DM + approved active members) rather than folding a new mode into that function -- this keeps the "who can see this" boundary named, single-purpose, and independently testable, mirroring how `hasPrivateAccess` itself already works for the wider private-side boundary. Reasoning for DM-only (documented here since the backlog text didn't resolve it): initiative-tracker HP and NPC notes are exactly the kind of thing a DM needs hidden from players during actual play (a monster's real remaining HP, an NPC's secret motive or plot-twist notes) -- unlike party notes or the session log, which are explicitly a *shared* record the whole table owns together, these are the DM's own prep/running tools. Every route (`GET`/`POST /api/campaigns/[id]/initiative`, `PATCH`/`DELETE .../initiative/[entryId]`, `POST .../initiative/[entryId]/move`, and the mirrored `.../npcs` and `.../npcs/[noteId]` routes) checks `isDm()` before any read or write; both panels (`InitiativeTrackerPanel`, `NpcNotesPanel`) are only ever rendered on the campaign page inside the same `{isDm && ...}` gate already used for `DmControls`, right below it.

Testing: `__tests__/unit/initiativeTracker.test.ts` (15 tests) covers add (trimming, sequential `order_index`, default null/empty hp/notes), rejecting adds from an approved player and a stranger, rejecting a blank name, over-length name/hp/notes, and a non-finite (`NaN`/`Infinity`) initiative value, partial updates, rejecting updates from a non-DM and of an unknown entry id, removal with `order_index` re-tightened to stay contiguous, rejecting removal from a non-DM and of an unknown entry, move-up/move-down swap behavior including no-op at either end of the list, rejecting moves from a non-DM, clearing a campaign's entries while leaving another campaign's untouched, and rejecting a clear from a non-DM. `__tests__/unit/npcNotes.test.ts` (10 tests) covers the equivalent add/update/delete/reject-non-DM/reject-unknown-id/per-campaign-isolation matrix for NPCs. `__tests__/unit/access.test.ts` gained 3 new tests for `isDm()` itself (true only for the actual DM, false for a stranger and a null/signed-out user id, false for an unknown campaign id). 232 → 260 tests, all green. `npm run build`, `npm run lint`, and `npx tsc --noEmit` all pass clean.

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #34, phase 1, resource vault)
Picked backlog item #34 (resource vault: per-campaign uploads + community resource library) as the next unblocked item in priority order -- #3 (deployment decision) and #6c (email/SMTP) remain explicitly blocked on human/business decisions and missing credentials, and #34 was next in line after #33 (DM dashboard basics, done earlier tonight). Note on process: on starting this increment, the scratch clone's most recent commit (`bb0da86`, backlog #33) was already fully committed but hadn't yet been pushed to `origin/main` -- pushed it first (confirmed via `git fetch` that `origin/main` now points at `bb0da86`) before starting any new work, so this increment builds cleanly on top of it.

Scope, per this item's own backlog text and the task's own instructions: the backlog line names two phases -- "per-campaign uploads" and "a cross-community library browsable by system" -- and explicitly flags the second as "a larger second phase (needs the system-hub browsing surface in #36 below to make sense as a destination)." Backlog #36 (curated system-specific hubs) doesn't exist yet, so this increment is scoped to phase 1 only: per-campaign uploads visible to the campaign's active party, following the same disciplined phased-delivery approach already used for #20 (sub requests) and #27 (availability). The community-browsable library is deferred, not attempted.

Design questions resolved before coding:
* **Storage.** Reused the base64 `data:`-URL-in-SQLite pattern already established for character portraits (backlog #26, `lib/characters.ts`'s `MAX_PORTRAIT_DATA_URL_LENGTH`/`validatePortraitDataUrl`), including its "reject outright, never transform" validation style and its convention of capping the *encoded* data URL length (base64 inflates the original file size by ~4/3) rather than a raw byte count. New `campaign_resources` table (`id`, `campaign_id`, `uploader_id`, `name`, `description`, `mime_type`, `data_url`, `created_at`, `updated_at`) -- a brand-new table, so no `ALTER TABLE` migration was needed (same as `campaign_messages`/`campaign_message_reads` in backlog #32).
* **Allowed file types and size cap.** The task explicitly called for a broader allowlist than the portrait feature's images-only set: images (PNG/JPEG/WEBP/GIF, matching the portrait feature's own set), PDF, and plain text. Size cap: 4MB per *source* file (~5.33M encoded characters, rounded up to a clean 5,600,000-character budget on the encoded `data:` URL, mirroring how the portrait feature's own ~200KB/280,000-character numbers are round, justified figures rather than exact byte-math). Reasoning for 4MB, documented in `lib/campaignResources.ts`: a portrait is a single column on a character and only ever one per character, so a small cap made sense; a resource vault holds many rows per campaign, so the cap needed to stay conservative enough that a dozen resources at the cap doesn't meaningfully bloat the file-based (not in-memory) SQLite database, while still being generous enough to fit a real map/handout scan or a many-page homebrew PDF -- 4MB comfortably covers that without needing real object storage. No server-side compression/transformation exists, matching the portrait feature's own choice to reject oversized/wrong-type uploads outright with a clear error rather than transform them.
* **Who can view/upload.** `hasPrivateAccess` (DM + approved active members) -- the exact boundary the task instructions called for ("same `hasPrivateAccess` boundary as party notes/session log/chat -- a DM's homebrew notes for their table aren't meant to be public"), not the narrower DM-only `isDm()` boundary backlog #33 introduced for the initiative tracker/NPC notes. Judgment call, documented in `lib/campaignResources.ts`: any active party member can upload, not just the DM -- a player sharing a handout or character art is just as legitimate a use as a DM's own maps/notes, and nothing about this app's existing conventions singles out uploads as DM-only the way #33's prep tools are (those are explicitly hidden-from-players tools; a resource vault is closer in spirit to party notes/table chat, a shared space the whole table contributes to).
* **Who can edit/delete.** A narrower boundary than upload access: only the uploader themselves, or the campaign's DM (`requireUploaderOrDm()` in `lib/campaignResources.ts`) -- so one player can't rename or delete a resource someone else on the table contributed. The DM is included as a backstop so an unreachable or departed uploader's file isn't stuck forever, mirroring how a DM can already moderate other shared content on their own campaign (editing/deleting any session log entry, regardless of original author).
* **Editing scope.** `updateCampaignResource()` only ever touches `name`/`description` -- re-uploading a different file under an existing entry is out of scope for this phase (delete-and-re-add covers that case); this keeps the update path simple and avoids a second, more complex file-replacement validation path.
* **Notifications.** Fires a new `campaign_resource_uploaded` notification to the rest of the active party (DM + other approved members, excluding the uploader) on every upload -- not throttled the way `campaign_chat_message` is (backlog #32), because uploads are expected to be infrequent events more like `party_notes_updated`/`session_log_posted` than a fast-moving chat thread with realistic message bursts, so there's no equivalent noise problem to solve.

Implementation: `lib/db.ts` gained the `campaign_resources` table + an index on `campaign_id`. `lib/types.ts` gained `CampaignResource`/`CampaignResourceWithUploader` (the enriched, uploader-name-attached shape the panel actually consumes, matching the existing client-safe-types convention used by `CampaignMessageWithSender`) and the new `campaign_resource_uploaded` notification type/label. `lib/campaignResources.ts` (new) exports `listCampaignResources` (oldest-first, enriched with `uploaderName` via `getUserById` -- callers must check `hasPrivateAccess` themselves before calling this, same convention as `listCampaignMessages`/`getNotes`), `addCampaignResource` (checks `hasPrivateAccess` internally, validates name/description/data-URL shape and size, inserts, fans out the notification), `updateCampaignResource` and `deleteCampaignResource` (both check `requireUploaderOrDm`).

Routes: `app/api/campaigns/[id]/resources/route.ts` (`GET` pre-checks `hasPrivateAccess` explicitly, mirroring `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/chat`; `POST` validates body shape via zod -- 400 -- then relies on `addCampaignResource`'s own internal access/validation checks, caught and mapped to 403, mirroring `POST /api/campaigns/[id]/chat` relying on `sendCampaignMessage`'s internal check the same way) and `app/api/campaigns/[id]/resources/[resourceId]/route.ts` (`PATCH`/`DELETE`, both relying on the lib's own `requireUploaderOrDm` check mapped to 403, mirroring the NPC-notes route pair's reliance on `requireDm`).

UI: `components/ResourceVaultPanel.tsx` (new, self-fetching client component wrapped in `Section`, following `NpcNotesPanel`'s add/edit/delete-list pattern combined with `CharacterManager`'s client-side file-read-as-`FileReader`/`readAsDataURL` upload flow, including a matching client-side pre-check against the raw file's byte size before the base64 round trip, same reasoning as `CharacterManager`'s `MAX_PORTRAIT_FILE_BYTES`). Each resource shows an inline thumbnail for images, a document emoji for PDF/text, a download link (the `data:` URL as the anchor's `href`, using the `download` attribute so plain text/PDF don't just navigate the tab away), the uploader's name, and edit/delete controls gated client-side by `isDm || resource.uploader_id === viewerId` (the server independently enforces the same check, so this is only a UI affordance, not the real boundary). Added to `app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx` inside the existing `{access && (...)}` block, between `CampaignChatPanel` and `PartyNotesPanel` -- gated behind the exact same `hasPrivateAccess` boundary as everything else in that block, with zero new access-control code needed on the page itself.

Testing: new `__tests__/unit/campaignResources.test.ts` (23 tests) covering: the DM and an active member uploading, trimmed, listed oldest-first with the uploader's display name attached; an omitted description defaulting to empty; the stored `mime_type` correctly extracted for a PNG, a PDF, and a plain-text data URL; rejecting an upload from a stranger, a still-pending (not-yet-approved) requester, and a member who has since left; a departed member's own past upload staying visible in the vault's history (matching backlog #32's own "history isn't rewritten" precedent); rejecting a blank name, an over-length name/description, a data URL that isn't a recognized image/PDF/text shape (both a non-data-URL string and a well-formed-but-disallowed-type like `video/mp4`), a data URL over the max length, and an upload with no file at all; the uploader editing their own resource's name/description, the DM editing a resource someone else uploaded, and rejecting an edit from a third active member who is neither the uploader nor the DM; rejecting an edit or delete of an unknown resource id; an edit that omits a field leaving the existing value untouched; the uploader deleting their own resource and the DM deleting anyone's, while rejecting a delete from a third active member; per-campaign isolation of resource lists; the rest of the active party (not the uploader) receiving exactly one `campaign_resource_uploaded` notification each on an upload; and that notification respecting a muted preference. 260 -> 283 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside a single bash call (per this file's own tooling note that backgrounded processes don't reliably survive across separate bash calls in this sandbox -- hit exactly this on the first attempt tonight, a `next start` launched in one call was gone by the next; fixed by keeping the entire smoke test, start-to-cleanup, inside one bash invocation) and drove it with five independent cookie jars (DM, an approved member "P1," an approved-then-left member "Leaver," a stranger, and a still-pending requester "Pending"). Confirmed, in order: signed-out `GET` -> 401; stranger `GET` -> 403; pending requester `GET` -> 403; DM `GET` -> 200 with an empty list; P1 (approved, non-DM) uploading a valid PNG -> 201 with the correct `mime_type`; a stranger's `POST` -> 403; an oversized upload (~5.7MB encoded data URL, over the 5,600,000-char cap) -> 403 with the expected "too large" error message; a wrong-type upload (`video/mp4`) -> 403 with the expected "must be a PNG, JPEG, WEBP, or GIF image, a PDF, or a plain text file" error; P1's own `GET` showing the uploaded resource with `uploaderName: "P1"`; the Leaver leaving the campaign then getting 403 on `GET`; the stranger (neither the uploader nor the DM) getting 403 attempting to delete P1's resource -- the unit tests separately cover the more precise case of a *fellow active party member* who is neither uploader nor DM being rejected the same way; the DM successfully `PATCH`-renaming P1's resource -> 200; and the DM successfully `DELETE`-ing it -> 200, with the list confirmed empty afterward. Cleaned up the temp SQLite db, cookie jars, build log, and server process afterward (confirmed via `ps aux` that no leftover `next start` process remained on the spare port).

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). Next unblocked item in priority order is #35 (session RSVP + reminders).

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #35, session RSVP + reminders)
Continuing the owner's "keep completing backlog items back-to-back throughout the day" instruction. Picked #35 next: the next unblocked item in priority order (#3 is a human deployment decision; #6c is blocked on SMTP credentials that still don't exist), and its own backlog line already sketched both halves (a small per-member RSVP table, and an in-app reminder that doesn't need the still-blocked email/SMTP infrastructure).

Two design questions to resolve up front, per the task's own framing:

RSVP data model: a new `session_rsvps` table (`campaign_id`, `user_id`, `response` — `confirmed`/`declined`, `CHECK`-constrained; no row means no answer yet), `PRIMARY KEY (campaign_id, user_id)`. Deliberately has no `session_at`/date column of its own — an RSVP only ever answers the campaign's *current* `next_session_at`, so `lib/schedule.ts`'s `updateSchedule()` now calls a new `clearRsvpsForCampaign()` (in `lib/sessionRsvps.ts`) whenever `nextSessionAt !== campaign.next_session_at`, i.e. the date is actually changing (including being cleared back to unscheduled) — a no-op re-save of the identical value doesn't wipe anyone's answer. This mirrors the task's own framing exactly: "an RSVP for last week's date is meaningless once the date changes." Access is the same `hasPrivateAccess` boundary already gating party notes/schedule/table chat (DM + approved active members) — no reason to invent a narrower or wider boundary for this feature. `setRsvp()` also rejects outright if `next_session_at` is null (nothing to confirm or decline yet), which is also what keeps the reset-on-reschedule invariant simple: every row that can ever exist is always answering the campaign's current date, so clearing on any change is always correct and never loses a "still valid" answer.

Reminders — the harder question, exactly as the task predicted: this app has no background job runner or cron, so there's no process that can wake up at T-24h and fire a notification on its own. Rather than build one (out of scope — it would need real always-on infrastructure, the same category of gap as backlog #3's deployment decision), landed on a lazy check-and-fire pattern: `lib/sessionReminders.ts`'s `isReminderDue(nextSessionAt, now, thresholdHours = 24)` is a pure function (an injectable `now`, matching `lib/schedule.ts`'s `computeScheduleStatus()` convention, so tests never depend on real wall-clock time) that says whether a session is both still upcoming and within the threshold; `checkAndFireSessionReminder(campaignId, userId, now)` calls it, checks a new `session_reminders_sent` table (`campaign_id`, `user_id`, `session_at`, `sent_at`, keyed by the *exact* `next_session_at` value) to see if this specific session date has already had a reminder sent to this user, and if not, fires a `session_reminder` notification through the existing `NOTIFICATION_TYPES`/`notify()`/mute-preference machinery (no new plumbing needed there — muting works for free) and records the send. Judgment call on the reminder-sent table's key: rather than resetting/clearing it on reschedule the way `session_rsvps` is cleared, `session_reminders_sent` is simply keyed by the session date itself — a reschedule produces a new `next_session_at` value, which is automatically a new, never-yet-sent key, so a fresh reminder can fire for the new date without any explicit reset logic; old rows for a since-changed date are left in place as a harmless audit trail rather than actively cleaned up.

Wired the check into two real user-activity trigger points, per the task's own suggestion of "the notifications endpoint, or the campaign detail page itself" — chose both, since they cover different scope cheaply: `GET /api/notifications` (hit by `NavBar`'s existing 15s polling fallback and by the `/notifications` page) calls a new `checkAndFireSessionRemindersForUser(userId)`, which reuses `lib/profiles.ts`'s existing `myCampaigns()` query (dming + playing, deduplicated) to sweep *every* campaign the signed-in user has private access to, regardless of which page they're on. The campaign detail page's own server component additionally calls `checkAndFireSessionReminder(id, viewer.id)` directly for just that one campaign, when the viewer has private access — a narrower, immediate check that fires right when someone looks at the specific campaign whose session is coming up. Both are idempotent no-ops once a reminder's already been recorded for the current date, so calling both on the same page load (e.g. `/campaigns/[id]` also indirectly causing a later `/api/notifications` poll) never double-fires.

**Known limitation, called out explicitly rather than glossed over** (in `lib/sessionReminders.ts`'s own doc comment, in "What the app does today," and here): a reminder only actually fires when *someone* triggers the check by using the app. If nobody in a campaign's active party opens `/notifications`, lets `NavBar` poll, or visits the campaign's own page between the 24h threshold and the session itself, nobody gets reminded for that session at all — there's no process watching the clock in the background to guarantee it fires. This is an honest constraint of an app with no always-on worker, in the same spirit as this file's existing documented scope-cuts (the SSE reconnect behavior under #6e, and the "sessions run"/"sessions played" stats being a proxy rather than ground truth under #18) — not a bug, and not something a bigger one-night effort could realistically close without the kind of real hosting/infrastructure decision backlog #3 is already waiting on.

Other judgment calls:
* RSVP responses are a plain two-state `confirmed`/`declined` (no row = no answer), matching the backlog line's own "confirm/decline" wording exactly, rather than inventing a third "maybe" state the backlog text never asked for.
* The DM can RSVP too, not just players — `hasPrivateAccess` already includes the DM uniformly everywhere else (party notes, schedule, chat), and there's no clear reason a DM's own attendance confirmation is less meaningful than a player's; carving out a DM-only exception would have been an extra, unrequested special case.
* `SessionRsvpPanel` (new client component, self-fetching, following `CampaignChatPanel`'s exact pattern) only renders on the campaign page when `campaign.next_session_at` is set — no point showing RSVP controls for a campaign with nothing scheduled, and `setRsvp()` would reject the attempt anyway; the page-level conditional keeps that guard visible in the UI instead of only surfacing it as a runtime error.
* The reminder message text stays generic ("...is coming up soon -- don't forget to RSVP") rather than embedding a formatted date server-side, matching `schedule_updated`'s own existing "has changed" (no embedded date) convention — avoids locale/timezone-formatting decisions inside a notification string that's meant to be a nudge, not the source of truth (the campaign page itself, and now the RSVP panel, already show the actual date).

Testing: two new files. `__tests__/unit/sessionRsvps.test.ts` (12 tests) covers an active member confirming and a DM declining, changing a response and clearing it back to no answer, defaulting to no response for someone who hasn't RSVPed, rejecting a stranger/a still-pending requester/a member who has since left, rejecting an RSVP attempt before any session is scheduled, listing every active party member with their response (including a null for no answer yet) and each one's `isDm` flag, `clearRsvpsForCampaign` wiping only the target campaign's rows, a reschedule clearing every existing RSVP, clearing the schedule back to unscheduled also clearing RSVPs, and a no-op re-save of the identical scheduled date leaving existing RSVPs untouched. `__tests__/unit/sessionReminders.test.ts` (17 tests) covers `isReminderDue`'s pure boundary logic (no date, further out than the threshold, within the default 24h threshold, exactly at the threshold boundary, already past-due, a custom threshold, and an unparseable date string) all with an injected `now`; then `checkAndFireSessionReminder` firing exactly once for a session within the window, not duplicating on repeated checks for the same date, doing nothing when the session is too far out or unscheduled, doing nothing for a user with no private access or an unknown campaign id, firing again for a fresh date after a reschedule, and respecting a muted `session_reminder` preference; then `checkAndFireSessionRemindersForUser` sweeping multiple campaigns (a DMed one and a played-in one) independently and no-opping for a user with no campaigns at all. 283 → 312 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port 4501, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside a single bash call per this file's own tooling note that backgrounded processes don't reliably survive across separate bash calls in this sandbox (confirmed again tonight on the first attempt — a `nohup ... & disown`'d server from one call was already gone, `ps aux` showed no trace, by the next call; every subsequent step of this smoke test ran start-to-finish, including cleanup, inside one bash invocation). Signed up a DM, two players, and a stranger; created a campaign; had both players request and the DM approve. Confirmed, in order: `PUT .../rsvp` before any session is scheduled → 403 with the expected "no scheduled session" error; the DM scheduling a session 48h out → 200; `GET .../rsvp` showing all three active members with `response: null`; a player confirming and the DM declining, both persisting and visible to a third viewer's own `GET`; a stranger's `GET`/`PUT` → 403 on both; the DM rescheduling to a different date (72h out) → every existing RSVP reset to `null` on the next `GET`; then, to exercise the reminder path without waiting on real wall-clock time, wrote directly to the SQLite file with a small `better-sqlite3` script (server stopped at that point) setting the campaign's `next_session_at` to 10 hours out; restarted the server and confirmed a player's first `GET /api/notifications` produced exactly one `session_reminder` notification, a second `GET` produced no duplicate, a second player's first visit to the campaign's own detail page produced their own first (and separately-tracked) `session_reminder`, and that same player's second page visit produced no duplicate either — confirming both trigger points fire independently, exactly once, per user. Cleaned up the temp SQLite db, cookie jars, build/server logs, and server process afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). Next unblocked item in priority order is #36 (curated system-specific hubs).

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #36, curated system-specific hubs)
Picked backlog item #36 as the next unblocked item in priority order (#3 deployment decision and #6c email notifications remain explicit human/credential blockers; #23/#24 remain blocked on business/legal decisions).

Design question this item raises, resolved before writing any code: the backlog line asks for a *curated* list of systems, but `campaigns.system` has been deliberately free text since day one (and backlog #7 built a free-text keyword search directly against it) -- forcing it onto a closed enum now would silently break every existing campaign whose DM typed something that doesn't match a fixed list, and would regress #7's own search. Resolution: a curated hub is a browsing *layer* on top of the free text, never a reclassification of it. `campaigns.system` doesn't change at all; a curated system is just a name + description + a short list of case-insensitive substring aliases, and a hub page is a live query for campaigns whose `system` field happens to match one of those patterns. This mirrors the backlog line's own "+ your system" homebrew framing: a campaign that matches no curated entry isn't an error state, it's simply not featured on a curated hub, and stays fully reachable through the ordinary `/campaigns` browse+search this app already has.

Implementation:
* `lib/types.ts`: `CURATED_SYSTEMS` (an `as const` array of six slugs) + `CURATED_SYSTEM_INFO` (a `Record<slug, { name, description, aliases }>`), following the exact `DANGER_LEVELS`/`DANGER_LEVEL_LABELS` shape already established in this same file, extended with an `aliases` array per entry since a name alone is too narrow a substring match (e.g. a campaign titled "Pathfinder 2E one-shot" needs to match via "pf2e"/"pathfinder 2e" as well as the canonical name). Curated list: D&D 5e, Pathfinder 2e, Call of Cthulhu, Vampire: The Masquerade, Blades in the Dark, Powered by the Apocalypse (PbtA) -- the backlog line's own reference set ("5e, Pathfinder 2e, Call of Cthulhu, Vampire: TM, PbtA/Blades") with its combined "PbtA/Blades" entry split into two, since Powered by the Apocalypse and Blades in the Dark (Forged in the Dark) are genuinely different systems with different named communities, not one thing with two names.
* `lib/campaigns.ts`: `escapeLikePattern` (previously a private helper) is now exported so `lib/systems.ts` can reuse it directly rather than re-implementing the same `%`/`_`/backslash escaping. `listCampaigns()` gained a `systemAliases?: string[]` option -- when present, it builds one `LOWER(system) LIKE @sysAliasN ESCAPE '\'` clause per pattern, ORs them together, and ANDs that group into the query exactly the same way the existing `system` (exact match) and `q` (keyword) filters already are. This was the natural integration point: it reuses 100% of the existing pagination/sort/cancelled-exclusion machinery rather than writing a parallel query path in `lib/systems.ts`.
* `lib/systems.ts` (new): `isCuratedSystemSlug()`, `listCuratedSystems()` (all six, in `CURATED_SYSTEMS` order), `getCuratedSystem(slug)` (null for an unrecognized slug), `systemMatchPatterns(slug)` (resolves a slug to `[name, ...aliases]`, null for unknown), and `campaignsForSystem(slug, opts)` (the live query, delegating to `listCampaigns({ systemAliases: patterns })`, also null for an unknown slug). The null-vs-empty-result distinction is deliberate: `{ items: [], total: 0 }` means "a real curated system with nothing posted yet," while `null` means "not a curated system at all" -- the `/systems/[slug]` page uses exactly that distinction to decide between an empty-state message and a 404.
* `app/systems/page.tsx` (new): lists all six curated systems (name, description, a live campaign count via `campaignsForSystem(slug, { pageSize: 1 }).total`) plus an explicit "Other / homebrew" card linking to `/campaigns`.
* `app/systems/[slug]/page.tsx` (new): `notFound()` for an unrecognized slug; otherwise the system's name/description, a live paginated list of matching campaigns (same `PAGE_SIZE = 10`, same list-item markup as `/campaigns`' own list view -- title/DM/headcount/location line, description), a "Search all campaigns instead" link pre-filled with the system's canonical name, and the same "Other/homebrew -> /campaigns" fallback repeated at the bottom so a homebrew system is never a dead end even after landing on an unrelated hub.
* `components/NavBar.tsx`: added an unconditional "Systems" link (visible signed-in or signed-out, same as "Browse"/"Find a sub") between "Browse" and "Find a sub".
* `app/campaigns/page.tsx`: added a "Browse by system" link under the page's own `<h1>`, satisfying the backlog line's "link... from the campaign browse page's system filter" note without needing to touch the filter form itself.
* No new API routes and no zod schemas: this is a pure read/browse feature, server-rendered directly off `lib/systems.ts` (which itself calls straight into `lib/campaigns.ts`/`better-sqlite3`), matching the existing precedent of `/players/[id]` (also a server component with no API route behind it) rather than the CRUD routes' convention of a `route.ts` + zod body schema, which only applies to mutations.

Testing: new `__tests__/unit/systems.test.ts`, 12 tests -- slug recognition for every `CURATED_SYSTEMS` entry and rejection of an unknown one; `listCuratedSystems()` returning all six in declared order with non-empty name/description; `getCuratedSystem()`/`systemMatchPatterns()` both returning null for an unrecognized slug and the right data for a known one; `campaignsForSystem()` returning null for an unrecognized slug; matching a campaign via the curated canonical name (case-insensitive); matching via a curated alias substring embedded in a longer, realistic system string ("Homebrew D&D 5e West Marches"); confirming two curated hubs don't cross-match each other's campaigns (a "Pathfinder 2E" campaign appears under `pathfinder-2e` but not `dnd-5e`); confirming a campaign with a genuinely unmatched system ("Fate Core (homebrew hack)") is excluded from every one of the six curated hubs, not just the "obvious" ones; cancelled-campaign exclusion (matching `listCampaigns`' own default); and pagination/sort passthrough (`pageSize: 1` limiting `items` while `total` still reflects every match, newest-first ordering). 312 → 324 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port 4099, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside a single bash call, per this file's own tooling note that backgrounded processes and open network sockets don't reliably survive across separate bash calls in this sandbox (confirmed again tonight: a server started in one call was unreachable and had no trace in a later call's `pgrep`, consistent with each call running in its own network/PID namespace). Signed up a DM, then posted three campaigns: one with `system: "Homebrew D&D 5e West Marches"` (an alias-substring match), one with `system: "Call of Cthulhu"` (an exact curated-name match), and one with `system: "Custom Homebrew Space Opera System"` (intentionally unmatched by any curated entry). Confirmed `/systems` renders all six curated names (including "Dungeons & Dragons 5th Edition") plus the "Other / homebrew" card; `/systems/dnd-5e` and `/systems/call-of-cthulhu` each show "1 campaign matching this system" and list exactly the right campaign; the unmatched "Starlight Drifters" campaign appears on neither hub page; `/systems/not-a-real-system` returns a 404; and `/campaigns` renders the new "Browse by system" link. Cleaned up the temp SQLite db (including `-wal`/`-shm` files), cookie jar, and server process afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). Next unblocked item in priority order is #37 (lightweight community discussion boards).

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #37, lightweight community discussion boards)
Picked backlog item #37 as the next unblocked item in priority order (#3 deployment decision and #6c email notifications remain explicit human/credential blockers; #23/#24 remain blocked on business/legal decisions). #37's own backlog text explicitly named itself as "the concrete first slice of backlog #25 (clubs/curated community lists), which currently names an idea but not a shape," so this session also revisited #25 once #37 landed (see below).

Scope decisions made before writing any code, following the task's own framing:
* A fixed, curated set of boards, not user-created ones -- mirrors the `CURATED_SYSTEMS`/`CURATED_SYSTEM_INFO` `as const`-array-plus-label-map convention from backlog #36 exactly (`BOARD_TOPICS`/`BOARD_INFO` in `lib/types.ts`). Chose four boards: New Player Questions, Homebrew & House Rules, Looking for Group Advice, and Local Meetups & In-Person Games -- the last one a deliberate tie-in to this app's own in-person-first product direction (see the 2026-09-07 product-direction entry above), giving that stated goal an actual place in the product rather than only living in `location`/proximity-discovery backlog items.
* Flat threads and flat replies, no nesting -- matches the task's own "keep it simple" instruction and this app's existing preference for the simplest workable shape (e.g. campaign chat/session log are also flat).
* Browsing is public (no sign-in required for `/boards`, `/boards/[slug]`, `/boards/[slug]/[threadId]`), matching the existing openness of campaign browsing, the sub-request pool, and system hubs -- all ungated reads. Posting a thread or a reply requires sign-in, with the same "Sign in to..." prompt pattern already used by `JoinLeaveControls`/`ConversationView` for the identical read-public/write-gated boundary.
* "Light moderation," resolved explicitly rather than glossed over: for this first pass it means self-moderation only. A thread or reply's own author can delete it (a two-click confirm, via a new shared `DeletePostButton` component reused by both thread and reply delete actions), and deleting a thread cascades to delete its replies. There is no edit affordance (delete-and-repost covers revision, keeping the write surface intentionally small) and, more importantly, no admin/reporting system of any kind. Boards aren't campaign-scoped, so unlike campaign resources or session log entries (where the DM is always available as a backstop moderator for their own table), there's no equivalent role here -- a bad-faith post is only removable by its own author. This is called out explicitly as an intentional scope cut, not an oversight: real moderation tooling (reports, an admin role, rate limiting) is a distinctly bigger feature this pass does not attempt.
* No new notification type for replies. A thread author isn't notified when someone replies to their thread. Considered adding a `board_reply_posted` type (following the exact `notify()`/`NOTIFICATION_TYPES` pattern already used everywhere else), but notifications' two link-target columns (`campaign_id`, `related_user_id`) don't have a natural slot for "a reply landed on your thread" -- neither is a campaign or a user profile, it's a thread id, which would need a new nullable column plus matching render logic on the notifications inbox page (which today, worth noting, doesn't even render a link for `related_user_id`-based notifications like `message_received`). That felt like more schema surface than a narrow first pass justified; revisit if boards see enough real use that "no way to know you got a reply" becomes an actual complaint.

Implementation:
* `lib/types.ts`: `BOARD_TOPICS` (an `as const` array of four slugs) + `BOARD_INFO` (a `Record<slug, { name, description }>`), plus `BoardThread`/`BoardThreadWithAuthor`/`BoardReply`/`BoardReplyWithAuthor` following this file's existing client-safe-types convention (e.g. `CampaignMessageWithSender`).
* `lib/db.ts`: two new tables, `board_threads` (with a `CHECK (board_slug IN (...))` constraint mirroring the fixed curated set, matching how `danger_level`/`characters.status` are also CHECK-constrained to their own `as const` sets) and `board_replies` (flat, referencing `board_threads(id)`). Both are brand-new tables, so no migration block was needed (same as `messages`/`sub_requests` when those first landed).
* `lib/boards.ts` (new): `isBoardSlug()`/`listBoards()`/`getBoard()` mirror `lib/systems.ts`'s `isCuratedSystemSlug()`/`listCuratedSystems()`/`getCuratedSystem()` exactly, including the null-for-unrecognized-slug convention. `listThreads(slug, opts)` returns `{ items, total } | null` (null distinguishes "not a real board" from a real board with zero threads), newest-first, paginated, enriched with each thread's author name and reply count. `createThread()`/`createReply()` validate and trim (title ≤200 chars, thread body ≤10,000, reply body ≤5,000). `deleteThread()`/`deleteReply()` enforce the self-moderation-only boundary (`requireAuthor()`, thrown as `BoardError` -- mapped to a 403 at the route layer, matching every other author/ownership-check convention in this app).
* `app/api/boards/[slug]/threads/route.ts` (GET public list + POST create, sign-in required), `app/api/boards/[slug]/threads/[threadId]/route.ts` (DELETE only -- the thread's own detail is server-rendered directly, no GET mirror needed, same "no API route for a pure read" precedent as `/systems/[slug]`), `app/api/boards/[slug]/threads/[threadId]/replies/route.ts` (POST only), `app/api/boards/[slug]/threads/[threadId]/replies/[replyId]/route.ts` (DELETE only) -- all thin routes using `requireUser()`/`errorResponse()`/zod, following the exact shape of `app/api/campaigns/[id]/chat/route.ts`.
* `app/boards/page.tsx` (board index, mirrors `/systems`' index exactly, including the `pageSize: 1`-for-count-only trick), `app/boards/[slug]/page.tsx` (board's thread list + `NewThreadForm`), `app/boards/[slug]/[threadId]/page.tsx` (thread detail + replies + `ReplyForm`, all server-rendered directly off `lib/boards.ts`, with `DeletePostButton` shown only when the viewer is the relevant post's own author).
* `components/NewThreadForm.tsx`, `components/ReplyForm.tsx` (small client compose forms, following `CampaignChatPanel`'s send-form shape but simpler -- since the surrounding list is server-rendered, not client-fetched, these only need to `router.refresh()`/`router.push()` on success rather than managing their own list state), and `components/DeletePostButton.tsx` (a small reusable two-click-confirm delete action, following `JoinLeaveControls`' own confirm-step pattern for a destructive action).
* `components/NavBar.tsx`: added an unconditional "Boards" link (visible signed-in or signed-out, same as "Browse"/"Systems"/"Find a sub").
* Backlog #25 revisited: marked "substantially addressed" (not fully closed) in the backlog list below. #37 is the concrete discussion-board slice #25's own text called for, but #25 also gestures at persistent regional *groups* (a standing membership list for, say, "Austin tabletop meetup," distinct from a thread discussing meetups) -- that's a meaningfully separate feature (its own membership/roster concept) that #37 doesn't build. The "Local Meetups & In-Person Games" board gives that idea a place to be discussed, not a place to organize as an ongoing group.

Testing: new `__tests__/unit/boards.test.ts`, 21 tests -- slug recognition for every `BOARD_TOPICS` entry and rejection of an unknown one; `listBoards()` returning all four in declared order with non-empty name/description; `getBoard()` returning null for an unrecognized slug and the right data for a known one; `createThread()` trimming title/body; rejecting a thread on an unknown board; rejecting a blank/over-length title and a blank/over-length body; `listThreads()` returning newest-first, paginated, and isolated per board; `listThreads()` returning null for an unrecognized slug (distinct from a real board's `{ items, total }` shape, deliberately asserted as a shape check rather than an exact zero-count given this file's shared per-file SQLite db across tests); pagination correctness (`pageSize` limiting `items` while `total` still reflects every match); a thread listing enriched with the author's display name and reply count; `getThread()`/`getThreadWithAuthor()` returning null for an unknown id; a thread's own author deleting it and cascading to delete its replies; rejecting a thread delete from anyone but its author and for an unknown thread id; deleting one thread leaving another thread's/board's data untouched; posting a reply with its body trimmed and listing replies oldest-first; rejecting a reply on an unknown thread and a blank/over-length reply body; the thread's own author being allowed to reply too (no relationship gate); a reply's own author deleting it without affecting the thread or other replies; rejecting a reply delete from anyone but its own author -- explicitly including a check that even the thread's *own author* can't delete someone else's reply, confirming self-moderation really has no backstop; rejecting a delete of an unknown reply id; and per-thread isolation of replies. 324 → 345 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port 4571, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside a single bash call, per this file's own tooling note that backgrounded processes don't reliably survive across separate bash calls in this sandbox. Confirmed, in order: `/boards` renders all four curated board names while signed out; `/boards/new-player-questions` renders (200) while signed out; `POST /api/boards/new-player-questions/threads` while signed out → 401; `GET /api/boards/not-a-real-board/threads` → 404. Signed up two users (Alice, Bob). Alice posted a thread ("How do I roll initiative?") → 201, and it rendered correctly on both the board's thread list and the thread's own detail page while still signed out. Bob (not the thread's author) attempting `DELETE` on Alice's thread → 403 with the expected "only the person who posted this thread" message. Bob replied to Alice's thread → 201, and the reply rendered on the thread page. Alice (the thread's author, but not the reply's) attempting to delete Bob's reply → 403 -- confirming self-moderation has no DM-style backstop even for the thread's own owner. Bob deleted his own reply → 200, and the thread page correctly reverted to "No replies yet." Alice deleted her own thread → 200, and the board's thread list correctly reverted to "No threads yet." Cleaned up the temp SQLite db, cookie jars, and server process afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). #25 (clubs/regional groups) is now substantially addressed by #37 rather than fully closed -- see the backlog list above for the remaining gap (persistent regional groups vs. discussion boards). No further unblocked backlog items were started this session; #38 (follow + activity feed), #39 (attendance-based reliability, blocked on real RSVP data existing first -- which it now does per #35), #40 (new-player onboarding path), and #41 (real proximity-based discovery, blocked on a geocoding/maps API key) remain the next candidates in priority order.

2026-09-07 (live session, continued): implemented backlog item #38 (follow + activity feed).

The design question this item raises head-on: the backlog line's own text says "surfacing their public session-log posts and character milestones" — but session log entries are NOT public. `lib/access.ts`'s `hasPrivateAccess()` gates the session log (plus party notes, table chat) to a campaign's DM and approved/active members, and this boundary already has explicit regression coverage at both the unit layer and the HTTP-integration layer (rendered-HTML content checks across every access role). Building a feed that literally surfaced "session-log posts" for a non-member follower would mean either (a) silently bypassing `hasPrivateAccess` for feed purposes — a real privacy regression dressed up as a feature, or (b) restricting the feed to only followers who also happen to be active members of the relevant campaign, which defeats the entire point of a *cross-campaign* activity feed and doesn't match how a "follow" relationship works anywhere else in this app. Neither is acceptable, so the feed's scope is deliberately narrower than a literal reading of the backlog text: it surfaces only events that are already fully public today, independent of any campaign membership — a character's existence/status on its owner's `/players/[id]` page, and a campaign's accepting-requests/rating state on its own public detail page. This is documented at three levels so it can't be silently "fixed" into a privacy hole later: a comment on `feed_events` in `lib/db.ts`, a comment at the top of `lib/feed.ts`, and this entry.

Two design questions the backlog item's own text raised, both resolved explicitly:

1. **Who can follow whom.** The backlog line says "players/DMs you've actually played with," which reads like it wants a shared-campaign-membership gate. Rejected that reading in favor of the same open-relationship convention already established by backlog #31 (direct messaging): any signed-in user can follow any other signed-in user, no prerequisite relationship. Reasoning: (a) this app has no comparable gate anywhere else — campaign browsing, the sub-request pool, messaging, and board posting are all ungated the same way; inventing one just for follows would be a new, unexplained inconsistency; (b) a real "played together" gate would need to walk shared campaign membership *history*, including past `'left'` memberships, not just current ones — real added complexity; (c) it would buy no actual privacy benefit, since everything a follow can surface is already fully public on the followed user's own profile regardless of who follows them. The backlog phrase is read as describing the expected *use case* (you naturally follow people from your own tables), not a hard technical requirement.

2. **What counts as a feed-worthy event.** Four event types, each hooked at the exact moment it happens rather than derived after the fact by querying mutable rows (a character's `updated_at` bumps on every edit, not just a status change, so it can't double as a reliable "this is when it happened" timestamp — unlike an immutable per-row table like `session_log_entries`, which the existing stats/chronicle code already reads directly):
   - `character_created` — hooked into `lib/characters.ts`'s `createCharacter`, fires unconditionally (a new character is immediately public on its owner's profile).
   - `character_status_changed` — hooked into `updateCharacter`, fires only when status actually transitions into `retired` or `fallen` from something else (not on every edit, and not on a transition back to `active`, which isn't the milestone the character-legacy framing is about).
   - `campaign_became_full` — hooked into `lib/memberships.ts`'s `approveRequest`, fires only on the real open→full transition (checked against `campaign.accepting_requests` as read *before* the update in that same call, so re-approving into an already-full, manually-reopened campaign doesn't re-fire it).
   - `campaign_first_rated` — hooked into `lib/campaignRatings.ts`'s `rateCampaign`, fires only the very first time a campaign receives *any* public rating (checked via the campaign's rating count before the upsert), not on every subsequent rating, to avoid noise.

   All four are written through a single `recordEvent()` choke point in `lib/feed.ts`, mirroring how `lib/notifications.ts`'s `notify()` is the one place every notification fan-out goes through.

Data model: `follows` (follower_id, followed_id, unique pair) and `feed_events` (actor_id, type, optional campaign_id/character_id, a precomputed message, created_at). `listFeed(viewerId, { page, pageSize })` joins `feed_events` to `follows` on `followed_id = actor_id` filtered to the viewer's own `follower_id`, ordered newest-first with the same `rowid` tiebreak convention used everywhere else in this file (same-millisecond ties). One integrity wrinkle surfaced immediately by the test suite: `feed_events.character_id REFERENCES characters(id)`, but unlike campaigns (only ever soft-cancelled, never deleted), characters *can* be genuinely deleted via `deleteCharacter` — the existing "deletes a character" unit test started failing with a `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` the moment `createCharacter` began writing a referencing feed event. Fixed by having `deleteCharacter` null out any referencing `feed_events.character_id` before deleting the character row, rather than deleting the event too — the event's message already has the character's name baked in as plain text at write time, so it stays meaningful without the row it once pointed to.

`/players/[id]` gained a Follow/Unfollow toggle (`components/FollowButton.tsx`, simple two-state toggle rather than `DeletePostButton`'s two-click confirm — unfollowing isn't destructive and `unfollow()` is idempotent) plus follower/following counts. `/feed` is a new server-rendered, paginated page (following `/boards/[slug]`'s convention — a `page` searchParam and numbered links — rather than the client-fetch pattern used by `/messages`/`/notifications`, since this is a read-only browse view with no client-side mutation of its own list). `app/api/follows/[userId]/route.ts` is the thin POST (follow)/DELETE (unfollow) mutation surface, following the exact `requireUser()`/`errorResponse()` pattern as every other action route.

339 → 355 unit tests (plus the existing 6 HTTP integration tests unchanged: 345 → 361 total), all green (`__tests__/unit/feed.test.ts`, 16 new tests — see the "Testing" section above for the full list, including the explicit privacy-boundary regression test that posts a real session log entry in a campaign the viewer has no membership in and asserts zero feed events / an empty feed). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port 3919, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside a single bash call (backgrounded processes don't survive across separate bash calls in this sandbox, per this file's own tooling note). Signed up three users (Bob, Carol, Alice). Confirmed, in order: Alice attempting to follow herself → 400 ("You can't follow yourself."); Alice following Bob → 201; Alice following Bob again → 400 ("You're already following them."); a signed-out follow attempt → 401. Bob created a character ("Thoradin Steelfist") and Carol created an unrelated character ("NotFollowedCharacter") neither Alice follows Carol nor has any relationship to her. Alice's `/feed` rendered Bob's "created a new character" event for Thoradin Steelfist and did NOT render Carol's NotFollowedCharacter anywhere on the page. A signed-out request to `/feed` rendered the "Sign in" prompt instead of erroring. Alice unfollowed Bob → 200, and Bob's event immediately disappeared from Alice's feed (replaced by the "not following anyone yet" empty state). Alice's view of Bob's `/players/[id]` page correctly showed the button reverted to "Follow" (not "Following") and rendered the follower count. Cleaned up the temp SQLite db, cookie jars, and server process afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call).
