2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #39, attendance-based reliability signal)
Continuing the owner's "keep completing backlog items back-to-back throughout the day" instruction. Picked #39 next: the next unblocked item in priority order per the previous entry's own note (#3 and #6c remain the hard blockers).

Before writing any code, re-read #39's own backlog text against #35's actual implementation, since the backlog line asserts a dependency ("depends on #35 producing real attendance data") that doesn't hold up: #35's `session_rsvps` table records *intent* to attend the campaign's current `next_session_at` -- confirmed or declined -- not whether anyone actually attended. Worse, it isn't even durable history: `lib/schedule.ts`'s `updateSchedule()` deletes every RSVP row for a campaign the moment `next_session_at` changes to anything else (including back to unscheduled), which is the correct behavior for what RSVP is *for* (an answer for a date that's no longer scheduled is meaningless), but it also means there is no persisted RSVP trail surviving past a single scheduled date to mine for a historical "who showed up" stat once a session has actually happened and the DM moves on to scheduling the next one. This is the same category of problem backlog #18 (personal stats) already hit and solved by cutting scope rather than fabricating data: that item dropped "hours at the table" entirely because no honest data source existed for session duration, rather than inventing a fake number. Reading #39 literally and deriving "reliability" from RSVP data would have meant the same mistake in a different shape -- presenting intent-to-attend as if it were attendance, or reading every session as `null` once its RSVPs got wiped.

Of the two honest paths the task laid out -- (a) a real "mark attendance" affordance tied to session log entries, or (b) a more modestly-labeled proxy computed from RSVP data alone -- (b) turned out not to really be available at all once the RSVP-durability issue above is accounted for: there's no persisted RSVP history left to compute *any* honest proxy from after a session passes. So (a) was the only path that produces real data, and it wasn't actually much more scope than a well-labeled proxy would have been: session log entries are already the one durable, immutable per-session record this app has (used by backlog #18's own "sessions run/played" proxy for exactly this reason), so attendance just needed to hang a small table off that.

Design: a new `session_log_attendance` table (`entry_id`, `user_id`, `attended`, `created_at`; composite primary key on `(entry_id, user_id)`). `lib/attendance.ts` is the new business logic, following this repo's established conventions closely: `attendanceCandidates(campaignId)` returns the active party minus the DM (a DM is presumed present for every entry they themselves post -- they wrote the recap -- so this signal is deliberately scoped to players only, matching the sub-request pool's own "reliability rating requested" framing, which is about players/subs showing up, not the table's own DM); `listEntryAttendance(entryId)` is a no-auth-of-its-own read helper (matching `lib/npcNotes.ts`/`lib/sessionRsvps.ts`'s convention of leaving the access check at the route) returning every current candidate enriched with the DM's recorded value or `null` if nobody has recorded it yet; `setEntryAttendance(entryId, dmId, records)` does a full-replace (mirrors `lib/availability.ts`'s `setAvailabilitySlots` -- the UI always submits the complete current checklist rather than individual toggles), checks `dmId` against the entry's campaign DM itself (matching `lib/sessionLog.ts`'s own authorship check), and rejects any submitted userId that isn't a current candidate rather than silently accepting it; `getAttendanceStats(userId)` is the aggregate `{ recorded, attended, rate }` read, with `rate: null` (not `0`) when `recorded` is `0` -- the same unrated-not-zero convention `lib/ratings.ts`'s `getUserRatingSummary` already established, so a user nobody has ever marked attendance for reads as "no data yet," not as an unreliable 0%.

Wiring: `POST /api/campaigns/[id]/log` (session log entry creation) gained an optional `attendance` array in the same request body -- "when the DM posts a session log entry, they mark which of the currently-active party actually showed up," submitted together rather than as a separate follow-up call. The route validates every submitted userId against `attendanceCandidates()` *before* calling `createEntry()`, so a bad id 400s cleanly rather than leaving a content-only entry behind after a partial failure. A new `app/api/campaigns/[id]/log/[entryId]/attendance/route.ts` (GET + PUT, both DM-only) lets the DM view or fix up an entry's attendance after the fact, for the case where they didn't mark it at creation time or need to correct it. `SessionLogPanel` gained: an optional "Record who attended this session" checklist under the new-entry composer (defaults every current party member to checked -- the DM unchecks whoever missed it, rather than starting from a blank slate that would require checking everyone by hand every time), and a DM-only "Attendance" toggle per existing entry that fetches the current record and lets it be edited/saved. The campaign detail page passes its already-queried `approvedMembers` list straight through as `partyMembers` -- no new query needed, since that list already excludes the DM by construction (it only ever joins the `memberships` table).

Judgment call on where the signal is shown: per-entry attendance ("who missed this specific session") stays DM-only to view, even though the rest of the session log/party notes/chat is visible to the whole active party -- naming a specific missed session is more pointed/potentially awkward than this app wants to surface widely, whereas the aggregate rate is a much softer, already-normalized signal (like a star rating) that's fine to show publicly. So `getAttendanceStats()` is surfaced on `/players/[id]`, right next to the existing "As DM"/"As player" reputation lines, labeled "Attendance (as player)" with an explicit "DM-recorded attendance, not RSVP responses" caption so it's never confused with backlog #35's RSVP feature -- this satisfies the original prototype's own "sub reliability rating requested: 4+*" mocked use case without having to also wire it into the sub-request pool's volunteer list in this pass (a reasonable follow-up, not built here, since a curious requester can already visit a volunteer's public profile).

Known limitation, called out explicitly rather than glossed over (in `lib/attendance.ts`'s own doc comments, in "What the app does today," and here): attendance-marking candidates are always computed from the campaign's *current* active party, not from a point-in-time snapshot of who was actually an approved member back when a given entry was first logged. This app's memberships table only tracks current status (pending/approved/declined/left), not an interval history of who was approved between which dates, so there is no way to reconstruct "who was really on the roster that day" for an old entry -- the same category of gap as backlog #19's campaign chronicle only counting *currently*-linked characters rather than a full historical roster. In practice this only matters when the DM edits attendance on an old entry well after the roster has changed (e.g. marking a brand-new member as having attended a session logged before they ever joined); marking attendance at the moment an entry is first posted -- the common path this pass optimizes for -- reflects the real party at the time, since the roster hasn't had a chance to drift yet.

369 unit tests (355 → 369; the existing 6 HTTP integration tests unchanged: 361 → 375 total), all green (`__tests__/unit/attendance.test.ts`, 14 new tests -- see the "Testing" section above for the full list). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean; confirmed via `git stash` that the one pre-existing `LayoutProps` TypeScript error in `app/layout.tsx` is present on `main` before this change too, so it's unrelated to this pass and was left alone rather than papered over.

Manual smoke test: built and started a real production server (`next start`, spare port, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`) inside a single bash call (backgrounded processes don't survive across separate bash calls in this sandbox, confirmed again this session -- a first attempt that started the server in one call and issued requests in a follow-up call found the server process simply gone, consistent with this file's existing tooling note). Signed up a DM and two players (Alice, Bob), approved both into a campaign. DM posted a session log entry with `attendance: [{Alice, attended:true}, {Bob, attended:false}]` in the same request as the content -- 201, entry created. Confirmed the DM's own `GET .../log/:entryId/attendance` shows Alice `attended:true`/Bob `attended:false`; confirmed a non-DM party member (Alice) gets 403 on that same GET. Confirmed Alice's public `/players/[id]` page renders "Attendance (as player)" "100% (1/1 logged session)" and Bob's renders "0% (0/1 logged session)" (verified in the actual server-rendered HTML text nodes, working around React's `<!-- -->` hydration-boundary comments between adjacent JSX expressions, which broke a naive first grep attempt). Used the DM-only PUT to flip Bob's attendance to `true` and confirmed his rendered rate updated to 100% (1/1). Posted a second entry with no `attendance` field at all and confirmed Alice's rate stayed at 100% (1/1) rather than silently dropping (proving an unmarked entry doesn't count against anyone). Confirmed submitting attendance for a userId with no relationship to the campaign (a freshly signed-up stranger) 400s at entry-creation time. Confirmed a brand-new signed-up user with zero attendance history renders "No attendance data yet" rather than "0%". Cleaned up the temp SQLite db, cookie jars, and server processes afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). The next unblocked candidates in priority order: #40 (new-player onboarding path) and #41 (real proximity-based discovery, still blocked on a geocoding/maps API key).

2026-09-07 (live session, continued): backlog item #40 (new-player onboarding path)
Picked up the next unblocked backlog item after #39 (attendance-based reliability signal), skipping the still-blocked items ahead of it in numeric position where applicable (#3 deployment decision, #6c email notifications, #23/#24 freemium/native-app, #41 still blocked on a geocoding/maps API key). #40's own text asked for two things: a "new to tabletop" profile flag, and a DM-settable "new-player friendly" campaign flag with a browse filter, explicitly modeled on the existing `danger_level`/`location` convention.

Data model: `profiles.new_to_tabletop INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` and `campaigns.new_player_friendly INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, both added to the `CREATE TABLE` statements in `lib/db.ts` plus the matching `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` migration guards at the end of the file for databases created before this session (the same pattern already used for `danger_level`/`location` themselves). `lib/types.ts` documents both fields on the `Campaign`/`Profile` interfaces, explicit about the distinction between the two: a player's self-flag ("I'm new") vs. a DM's flag on their own table ("this table is welcoming to someone new") -- two different flags on two different entities, not one shared concept, since a new-to-tabletop player joining a campaign doesn't make that campaign new-player-friendly and vice versa.

`lib/profiles.ts`'s `upsertProfile()` gained an optional `newToTabletop` boolean (undefined = leave unchanged, matching every other field in that function), stored as 0/1. `lib/campaigns.ts`'s `createCampaign()` gained an optional `newPlayerFriendly` boolean (defaults false) and `updateCampaign()` gained the same option in its `Partial<{...}>` updates object (undefined = leave unchanged) -- both following the exact shape of the existing `location`/`dangerLevel` handling in those same functions, right down to reusing the same INSERT/UPDATE statement style. `listCampaigns()` gained a plain `newPlayerFriendly?: boolean` filter option: when true, ANDs a `new_player_friendly = 1` clause onto the existing WHERE chain (system/q/location/systemAliases/includeCancelled), exactly like every other opt-in filter already there -- no LIKE/escaping needed since this is a straight boolean equality check, not a substring match.

API surface: `app/api/profile/route.ts`'s PUT body schema gained `newToTabletop: z.boolean().optional()` (the route already spreads `...profileInput` straight into `upsertProfile()`, so no other route code needed to change). `app/api/campaigns/route.ts`'s GET handler parses `?newPlayerFriendly=true` off the query string and its POST body schema gained the same optional boolean; `app/api/campaigns/[id]/route.ts`'s PATCH body schema gained it too, again relying on the existing "schema fields flow straight into the lib function" pattern rather than new plumbing.

UI: `/campaigns/new` and `DmControls`'s edit form both gained a plain checkbox next to (after) the existing Location field/danger-level select, submitted alongside everything else already in those forms. The campaign detail page header (`app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx`) renders a badge -- identical pill styling to the existing `danger_level` badge -- right after the danger-level badge when `new_player_friendly` is set. `/campaigns` browse list cards and `DiscoverDeck`'s swipe cards both gained the same badge (the backlog text explicitly asked for "browse cards," and rather than only covering the list view, both presentations of a campaign got it, matching how `danger_level` already appears on the Discover card even though it doesn't appear on the plain list card today -- this item's badge appears on both, closing that small existing gap for the new field at least). The `/campaigns` filter form gained a "New-player friendly only" checkbox (a bare HTML checkbox in a `method="get"` form -- unchecked omits the field from the query string entirely, which is exactly the "no filter" state, so no extra JS was needed), and every existing query-preserving `Link` on that page (the List/Discover view toggle, and the numbered pagination links) had `newPlayerFriendly` added to their `query` objects alongside `system`/`q`/`location`/`sort`, so the filter survives switching views or paging exactly like every other filter already does.

Judgment call flagged explicitly in the task brief: "consider whether a new-to-tabletop user should see anything different by default." Landed on a small opt-in nudge rather than either extreme (building nothing, or silently pre-applying the filter): `/campaigns` now checks, server-side, whether the signed-in viewer's own profile has `new_to_tabletop` set and the filter isn't already active, and if so shows one line -- "New to tabletop? Try campaigns flagged new-player-friendly." -- linking straight to the filtered view with every other current filter preserved. Silently pre-applying the filter was rejected because a new-to-tabletop user browsing for something specific (a particular system, a friend's game) shouldn't have their own filters overridden on every visit; this keeps the default view identical for everyone while still surfacing the option where it's actually relevant. A "New to tabletop" badge was also added next to the display name on `/players/[id]` (mirroring the campaign-side badge's placement convention) since the flag is otherwise invisible anywhere else in the app once set.

Testing: 375 -> 381 tests. `__tests__/unit/campaigns.test.ts` gained four tests (defaulting to false and settable true on create; settable and clearable via update; left untouched when omitted from an update; the browse-list filter excluding a non-flagged campaign while an unfiltered query still returns both). `__tests__/unit/profiles.test.ts` gained two tests (set/cleared independently of every other profile field via `upsertProfile`; left untouched when omitted from an update) plus updated the existing "saves and re-reads, trims whitespace" and "brand-new user defaults" tests to also assert on the new field, matching how `location` was folded into those same tests when it shipped. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db (same "everything in one bash call" constraint noted in the #39 entry above -- backgrounded server processes don't survive between separate tool calls in this sandbox): signed up a DM and a player, set the player's `new_to_tabletop` flag via `PUT /api/profile` and confirmed it round-tripped on re-GET; had the DM create one campaign with `newPlayerFriendly: true` and a second without it, confirmed `GET /api/campaigns?system=...` (no filter) returned both while `?newPlayerFriendly=true` returned only the flagged one; confirmed the flagged campaign's detail page HTML rendered the "New-player friendly" badge while the unflagged one's didn't; confirmed the browse page's filter checkbox is present in the server-rendered form; created eleven new-player-friendly campaigns under one system to force pagination (page size 10) and confirmed the page-1 -> page-2 link correctly carried `newPlayerFriendly=true` alongside `system`/`sort`, with page 2 correctly showing the eleventh (oldest) matching campaign; and confirmed `PATCH /api/campaigns/[id]` toggling an existing campaign's flag immediately changed whether its detail page rendered the badge. Server, temp SQLite db, and response/cookie-jar scratch files all cleaned up afterward.

2026-09-07 (live session, continued)
Picked backlog item #41 (real proximity-based, in-person-first discovery) as the next unblocked item after #40. Before writing any code, checked this environment for a maps/geocoding API key (env vars) and confirmed none exists -- so, per this task's own instructions, split the item into a phase 1 that doesn't need one and an explicitly-deferred phase 2 that does, following the exact phased-delivery discipline already established for #20, #27, and #34 in this backlog (a `~~struck-through item~~` **Phase 1 done \<date\>** annotation, with phase 2 named as a specific, still-blocked follow-up rather than silently dropped).

Design decisions, made before touching code:
* **Boolean vs. enum for the campaign side.** The task brief offered either a plain `is_in_person` boolean or a three-value `"in_person" | "remote" | "hybrid"` enum. Went with the enum: a table that meets in person but keeps a remote seat open for someone who can't travel that week is a real, common shape this app shouldn't have to misrepresent as strictly one or the other, and the enum still supports every filter/ranking need a boolean would (in-person vs. everything-else), it just also captures the honest middle case. Modeled as a nullable, CHECK-constrained `TEXT` column (`campaigns.session_format`) -- the exact same shape as `danger_level`, right down to null meaning "the DM hasn't said" rather than defaulting to a value that implies an answer.
* **A distinct, smaller enum for the profile side, not the same `SessionFormat` type.** `profiles.session_format_preference` uses its own `"in_person" | "remote" | "either"` enum rather than reusing `SessionFormat`, because a *preference* isn't shaped like a table's actual format -- "hybrid" describes what a table offers, not what a person wants (nobody "prefers hybrid" the way a table "is hybrid"). Scoped deliberately narrow: this field is purely informational for this phase, shown on `/players/[id]` next to the existing location line, and is explicitly NOT wired into any ranking or matching logic -- the task brief's own "use your judgment on scope... keep it to plain fields, not a new subsystem" line was the guardrail here. A future phase could use it (e.g. to bias a signed-in viewer's own ranking toward their stated preference), but that's a real, separate design question about personalized vs. global ranking this pass didn't need to answer.
* **Ranking approach: an unconditional sort, not an opt-in filter toggle.** The task brief allowed either "a stable sort that puts in-person first within whatever the existing sort order already is, or a filter toggle defaulting to in-person." Chose the sort: a filter toggle defaulting to "in-person only" would need an explicit "show remote too" escape hatch to satisfy the brief's own "don't remove the ability to find remote games" requirement, whereas a stable re-sort satisfies that requirement for free (remote campaigns are still right there, just lower) with no second control needed. Implemented as a single `CASE session_format WHEN 'in_person' THEN 0 WHEN 'hybrid' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END ASC` clause prefixed onto `listCampaigns()`'s existing `ORDER BY`, made unconditional (not behind a new option) specifically so every existing call site -- `/campaigns`' list view, its Discover deck, and `lib/systems.ts`'s curated system hubs (which forward straight through to `listCampaigns()`) -- picks up the new default with zero call-site changes, matching the brief's own framing of this as "the new default everywhere."
* **Why this doesn't break the pre-existing sort/pagination tests.** Every campaign fixture in the existing test suite (and every campaign created before this column existed, via the `ALTER TABLE` migration) has `session_format = NULL`, which the `CASE` expression buckets into the same rank as explicitly-`remote` campaigns (rank 2) -- not promoted, since only an explicit `in_person` flag earns the boost (so the ranking can't be gamed by simply not answering). When every row in a query ties at the same rank, the `CASE` prefix is a no-op and the original secondary sort (`created_at`/`title`) alone determines order, exactly as before. Verified this holds, rather than assuming it: added an explicit regression test (`"preserves the existing newest/oldest/title sort order when no campaign has a session_format set"`) alongside the new ranking test itself.
* **Also added a real, opt-in `sessionFormat` exact-match filter** (`listCampaigns()`, the browse form, and the `GET /api/campaigns` route), mirroring `newPlayerFriendly`'s shape -- not strictly required by the brief (ranking alone already doesn't exclude remote games), but it directly answers the brief's explicit instruction not to regress the ability to *deliberately* find only remote games, which a viewer might genuinely want (e.g. someone who is only available for remote play).

Implementation followed every existing convention exactly: `lib/types.ts` gained `SESSION_FORMATS`/`SESSION_FORMAT_LABELS`/`SessionFormat` and `SESSION_FORMAT_PREFERENCES`/`SESSION_FORMAT_PREFERENCE_LABELS`/`SessionFormatPreference` (the same `as const` array + label-map shape as `DANGER_LEVELS`); `lib/db.ts` gained the two new columns on `campaigns`/`profiles` in the `CREATE TABLE` statements plus the matching `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` migration guards (SQLite can't add a `CHECK` constraint via `ALTER TABLE`, so migrated databases are left unconstrained at the schema level the same way `danger_level` already is -- validation still happens in `lib/campaigns.ts`/`lib/profiles.ts` before any write); `lib/campaigns.ts`'s `createCampaign`/`updateCampaign` gained `sessionFormat` following the exact undefined-leaves-unchanged/null-clears/value-sets three-state convention already established for `dangerLevel`; `lib/profiles.ts`'s `upsertProfile` gained the symmetric `sessionFormatPreference`; both `app/api/campaigns/route.ts`/`app/api/campaigns/[id]/route.ts` and `app/api/profile/route.ts` gained matching zod validation (`z.enum(SESSION_FORMATS)`/`z.enum(SESSION_FORMAT_PREFERENCES)`, `.nullable().optional()` on the update paths); `app/campaigns/new/page.tsx` and `DmControls.tsx`'s edit form gained a "Format" `<select>` mirroring the existing "Danger level" select; `app/profile/page.tsx` gained an "In-person or remote?" select; the campaign detail page header, `/campaigns` browse cards, and `DiscoverDeck.tsx` all gained a Format badge (same rounded-pill style as the existing danger-level/new-player-friendly badges); `app/players/[id]/page.tsx` gained a read-only "In-person or remote?" line, same placement convention as the existing Location line.

**A real process mistake this session, corrected before it caused any damage:** partway through, several edits were made against the mounted host folder (`C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo`, reachable from the Read/Write/Edit tools) instead of the bash-sandbox scratch clone (`~/build/session-zero`) the setup instructions require for all real development work. Caught this by noticing `npx tsc --noEmit`/`vitest` in the scratch clone weren't reflecting the edits at all. Recovery: confirmed via `diff` that the mounted folder's already-stale-`git`-but-current-working-tree state (a known, documented condition from this same setup's own notes -- prior sessions rsync files into that folder without ever committing there, so its `git log` sits many commits behind while its actual file contents are current) held exactly the intended diff and nothing else for every file touched, then `cp`'d just those specific files from the mounted folder into the scratch clone over `mcp__workspace__bash`, verified with a second `diff` pass and a clean `git status --short` in the scratch clone (only the intended files listed as modified), and continued all further edits (test files, this progress file) directly through the sandbox from that point on. No stray/unrelated content crossed over -- three other files the mounted folder's `git status` also flagged as "modified" (relative to its stale HEAD) turned out to be byte-identical to the scratch clone's already-current versions, confirming they were pre-existing drift unrelated to this session's own edits, not something to worry about.

Testing: 381 -> 392 tests. `__tests__/unit/campaigns.test.ts` gained eight tests (session_format defaulting to null and settable on create; rejecting an unrecognized format on create; set/changed/cleared via update; rejecting an unrecognized format on update; left untouched when omitted from an update; the `sessionFormat` exact-match filter excluding other formats while an unfiltered query still returns every one of them; the default ranking re-ordering four campaigns -- in-person, hybrid, unset, remote, created in a deliberately-scrambled order -- into rank order while `total` still reflects all four; and the existing newest/title sort order verified unchanged when no campaign in the fixture set has a `session_format`). `__tests__/unit/profiles.test.ts` gained three tests (`session_format_preference` set/changed/cleared independently of other fields; left untouched when omitted from an update; rejecting an unrecognized value) plus the existing "brand-new user defaults" test now also asserts the new field defaults to null. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Server, temp SQLite db, and log/cookie scratch files all cleaned up afterward.

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #29, sub requests carry a specific date/time and location)
This increment was specifically dispatched to pick up backlog #29, an older, still-open, unblocked item that today's earlier chain of agents skipped over while working through the newer owner-approved #31-42 batch in numeric order (#30 is being picked up separately, in its own increment). #29 and #30 both predate #31-42 and were never blocked -- they were simply passed over, not deferred for a real reason -- so this entry exists to close that gap explicitly rather than let an older item silently rot at the bottom of the backlog.

Before writing any code, read #29's own backlog text closely (it already prescribes the shape: a nullable `needed_at TEXT` column mirroring `campaigns.next_session_at`, reusing `lib/schedule.ts`'s UTC-safe formatting/display logic, plus a location field), `lib/subRequests.ts`, and `lib/schedule.ts` in full. The backlog line raises one real open question and explicitly leaves it for this increment to resolve: "worth deciding whether location is even meaningful per-request... or whether it should just default from the campaign's own location field with an optional override." Resolved it the second way, not arbitrarily -- most campaigns already have their own `location` field shown right on the campaign detail page, so a sub request re-typing that same value every time would be pure duplication; a per-request field only earns its keep as an *override* for the genuine edge case (a one-off session at a different table/venue than usual). Modeled as `sub_requests.location TEXT`, nullable, where null means "use the campaign's location" -- the requester never has to type anything unless their specific case actually differs from the norm.

Implementation, following every existing convention exactly rather than inventing new patterns:
* `lib/db.ts`: `sub_requests`' `CREATE TABLE` gained `needed_at TEXT` and `location TEXT` (both nullable, no CHECK constraint needed since neither is an enum), plus the matching `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` migration guards at the end of the file, reusing the `subRequestColumns` snapshot already captured earlier in the file (for `character_id`'s own migration guard) rather than re-querying `PRAGMA table_info` a second time -- the same reuse pattern `campaignColumns`/`profileColumns`/`characterColumns` already establish for their own multiple migration checks.
* `lib/types.ts`: `SubRequest` gained `needed_at: string | null` and `location: string | null`, each with a doc comment explaining the override semantics. `SubRequestSummary` gained `campaignLocation: string` (always the campaign's own location field, so a caller never needs a second lookup to render the fallback) and `neededAtStatus: ScheduleStatus` (computed server-side, not left to the client).
* `lib/subRequests.ts`: `createSubRequest()` gained two new optional positional parameters, `neededAt` and `location`, after the existing `characterId` -- matching that function's own existing convention of trailing optional params rather than switching the whole signature to an options object mid-function. `neededAt` is validated exactly the way `lib/schedule.ts`'s `updateSchedule()` validates `next_session_at` (parse with `new Date()`, reject `NaN` as `SubRequestError("Invalid date.")`); `location` is trimmed and capped at a new `MAX_LOCATION = 200`, matching `lib/profiles.ts`'s own `MAX_LOCATION` constant and reasoning (campaigns.ts itself has no length cap on `location` at all, so profiles.ts was the closer precedent to follow for a bounded free-text location field). A blank/whitespace-only location trims down to `null`, not an empty string, so "no override" stays a single, unambiguous value. `withContext()` (the shared enrichment step behind both the campaign-page listing and the app-wide pool) now also sets `campaignLocation: campaign?.location ?? ""` and `neededAtStatus: computeScheduleStatus(row.needed_at)`, imported directly from `lib/schedule.ts` -- confirmed no circular import risk first (`schedule.ts`'s own import graph doesn't reach back into `subRequests.ts` anywhere).
* API surface: `app/api/campaigns/[id]/subs/route.ts`'s POST body schema gained `neededAt: z.string().trim().min(1).optional()` and `location: z.string().trim().max(200).optional()` -- format/business validation still happens in `createSubRequest()` itself, matching this route's existing division of labor (the schema just shapes the JSON, the lib function is the source of truth for what's actually valid).
* UI: `SubRequestPanel.tsx` (the campaign-page panel) gained a `campaignLocation` prop (threaded from `app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx` as `campaign.location`) plus two new inputs on the post form -- a `datetime-local` input for "Needed for" and a text input for "Location override," placeholder showing the campaign's own location so it's clear what happens if it's left blank. Each listed request now shows "Needed for: <formatted date>" (with a red "(past due)" tag when `neededAtStatus` is `"past-due"`, reusing the server-computed status rather than recomputing it client-side) and "Location: <override or campaignLocation>" (labeled "(campaign default)" when no override was given), both rendered distinctly from -- and above -- the free-text note, directly answering the backlog line's own "no way to show this distinctly from the note text" complaint. `/subs` (the app-wide pool, a server component) got the identical two lines added to its existing per-request card.

Testing: `__tests__/unit/subRequests.test.ts` gained 7 new tests in a new `describe` block: defaulting both `needed_at`/`location` to null when omitted; accepting a valid `neededAt`+location override and having both land on the created row; rejecting an invalid `neededAt`; rejecting an over-length location override; trimming a blank location override down to null rather than storing whitespace; `campaignLocation` in listings always reflecting the campaign's own location regardless of whether that specific request has an override (checked both with and without an override on the same campaign); and `neededAtStatus` correctly classifying a null/future/past `needed_at` as unscheduled/upcoming/past-due respectively, reusing real `computeScheduleStatus()` output rather than re-implementing the comparison in the test. 392 -> 399 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db (entire server-start + curl sequence run inside single `mcp__workspace__bash` calls, per this sandbox's own well-documented constraint that backgrounded processes don't survive between separate tool calls -- confirmed this the hard way again this session before switching to the one-call-per-server-lifetime pattern already established in prior entries): signed up a DM and a player, created a campaign with `location: "Austin, TX"`, had the player join and the DM approve; posted one sub request as the player with both a `neededAt` (one week out) and a location override ("Bob's garage instead of usual spot"), and a second as the DM with neither; confirmed `GET /api/subs` (the app-wide pool) and `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/subs` (the campaign listing) both returned identical, correct data for both requests, including `neededAtStatus: "upcoming"` for the first and `"unscheduled"` for the second, and `campaignLocation: "Austin, TX"` on both regardless of the first request's own override; confirmed posting a sub request with an unparseable `neededAt` ("not-a-real-date") 400'd with `{"error":"Invalid date."}`; and confirmed the actual server-rendered `/subs` HTML contained "Needed for: 9/14/2026, 5:21:01 PM" and "Location: Bob's garage instead of usual spot" for the override request, and "Location: Austin, TX (campaign default)" for the other -- proving the UI, not just the API, renders both fields correctly and distinctly from the note. Server process, temp SQLite db, and cookie-jar scratch files all cleaned up afterward.

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #30, campaign starting level + tone tags)
This increment was specifically dispatched to pick up backlog #30, the other older, still-open, unblocked item (alongside #29) that today's earlier chain of agents skipped over while working through the newer owner-approved #31-42 batch in numeric order. #29 and #30 both predate #31-42 and were never blocked -- they were simply passed over -- and #29 was already closed in a separate increment earlier today; this one closes the gap for #30.

Before writing any code, read #30's own backlog text closely. It explicitly poses two open questions and asks the implementer to resolve them before touching schema: (1) whether `starting_level` should be free text or a small integer, and (2) whether genre/tone/style should be a single closed enum or a multi-select tag list, "worth deciding... since that shapes both the schema and the campaign browse filter UI." Resolved (1) in favor of free text, following the exact nullable-text-column convention `sub_requests.location` established under backlog #29 earlier today -- systems vary too much in how they express level/rank ("Level 3" vs. "Tier 2" vs. a narrative milestone like "just past the prologue") for a rigid integer to be honest across every system this app supports, and the backlog line's own text already leaned this direction. Resolved (2) in favor of a multi-select tag list, not a single closed enum -- the backlog line's own example ("a campaign can plausibly be both 'heavy battle' and 'horror'") is a direct argument against a single enum, since a real table often is more than one tone at once. The vocabulary itself still stays closed and curated (not free text), so the browse filter can offer real checkboxes and match reliably rather than needing fuzzy string matching against arbitrary DM input.

Implementation, following every existing convention exactly rather than inventing new patterns:
* `lib/types.ts`: `Campaign.starting_level: string | null` (nullable, following the exact three-state convention `danger_level`/`session_format` already use for DM-set optional fields) and `Campaign.tone_tags: CampaignToneTag[]` (always an array, defaulting to empty rather than null so callers never need a null-check before iterating it). New `CAMPAIGN_TONE_TAGS` `as const` array (horror, heavy-combat, roleplay-focused, political-intrigue, comedic, mystery-investigation, exploration, one-shot-friendly) plus `CampaignToneTag` type and `CAMPAIGN_TONE_TAG_LABELS` display-label map, matching the exact `DANGER_LEVELS`/`SESSION_FORMATS` shape.
* `lib/db.ts`: `campaigns`' `CREATE TABLE` gained `starting_level TEXT` (nullable, no CHECK constraint -- it's free text, not an enum) and `tone_tags TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'` (JSON-encoded array), plus the matching `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` migration guards for both, reusing the existing `campaignColumns` snapshot already captured earlier in the file (same pattern as every other campaign-column migration).
* `lib/campaigns.ts`: this is where the real design work landed, since `tone_tags` is the first JSON-array column on the `campaigns` table itself (as opposed to a separate ratings-style table) -- every raw `SELECT * FROM campaigns` read now has to parse it. Added a `CampaignRow` interface (the raw DB shape, `tone_tags` typed as the raw JSON string) and an exported `rowToCampaign()` that parses it into a real array (falling back to `[]` on malformed JSON, matching `lib/ratings.ts`/`lib/campaignRatings.ts`'s own defensive fallback) -- `getCampaign()` and `listCampaigns()` both route every row through it now instead of casting the raw row directly to `Campaign`. `createCampaign()`/`updateCampaign()` gained `startingLevel`/`toneTags` inputs with the same validation shape `campaignRatings.ts`'s `rateCampaign()` already established for its own tags: reject an unrecognized tag, reject more than `MAX_TONE_TAGS` (5, deliberately below the full 8-tag vocabulary so a DM has to pick the tags that actually describe the table rather than checking every box), full-replace semantics on update (a provided array always replaces the whole list, matching how `rateCampaign`/`rateCampaignParticipant` treat their own tags -- no partial add/remove). `listCampaigns()` gained a `toneTags` any-of filter option: rather than adding a `json_each`/JSON1 dependency this codebase doesn't otherwise use, matched via a `LIKE '%"tag"%'` substring against the JSON text for each selected tag, OR'd together -- safe against false substring matches because every match is anchored by the JSON string's own quote characters and the curated vocabulary has no tag that's a substring of another (verified: horror/heavy-combat/roleplay-focused/political-intrigue/comedic/mystery-investigation/exploration/one-shot-friendly share no substring relationships). An unrecognized tag passed to the filter is silently dropped rather than erroring, matching `app/api/campaigns/route.ts`'s existing `parseSessionFormat` precedent for an invalid GET filter value.
* `lib/profiles.ts`: `myCampaigns()` also reads raw `campaigns` rows directly (for the profile page's DMing/playing lists) -- exported `rowToCampaign`/`CampaignRow` from `lib/campaigns.ts` specifically so this second call site could parse `tone_tags` the same way instead of duplicating the logic.
* API surface: `app/api/campaigns/route.ts`'s POST body schema gained `startingLevel: z.string().trim().max(100).optional()` and `toneTags: z.array(z.enum(CAMPAIGN_TONE_TAGS)).max(5).optional()`; its GET handler reads repeated `?toneTags=` query params via `searchParams.getAll()`. `app/api/campaigns/[id]/route.ts`'s PATCH schema mirrors this with the same three-state (`undefined`/`null`/value) nullable shape `dangerLevel`/`sessionFormat` already use for `startingLevel`.
* UI: `/campaigns/new` and `DmControls`'s edit form both gained a "Starting level" text input and a "Tone / style" checkbox group (using `CAMPAIGN_TONE_TAG_LABELS` for display text). The campaign detail header, `/campaigns`' browse list cards, `DiscoverDeck.tsx`'s cards, and `/systems/[slug]`'s curated hub cards all gained a "Starting level: <value>" badge and one badge per tone tag -- the same badge styling already used for `session_format`/`new_player_friendly` on each of those surfaces (session_format/new_player_friendly don't render on the system hub cards today, but backlog #30's own instruction to show these fields "anywhere danger_level/session_format already render" plus the system hub cards being explicitly named meant adding them there regardless). `/campaigns`' filter form gained a "Tone / style (any of)" checkbox group, threaded through the same query-param/pagination-link-preservation convention as every other filter on that page (including the view-toggle links and the new-player-friendly nudge link, which all needed `toneTags` added to their preserved query objects).

Testing: `__tests__/unit/campaigns.test.ts` gained 13 new tests: `starting_level` defaulting to null and settable/trimmed on create; rejecting an over-length value on create and separately on update; set/changed/cleared via update including a blank-string update clearing it back to null; left untouched when omitted from an update; `tone_tags` defaulting to an empty array and settable on create; rejecting an unrecognized tag on both create and update; rejecting more than the max allowed tag count; set/changed/cleared via update; left untouched when omitted from an update; round-tripping correctly through the JSON-in-TEXT storage on a fresh `getCampaign()` read (guards against a regression where `rowToCampaign` stops being called somewhere); the any-of browse filter matching on a single tag and on a pair of tags (including a campaign that only matches via the union of the two, proving it's genuinely any-of and not all-of); and an unrecognized tag in the browse filter being silently ignored (returning the unfiltered count) rather than erroring the whole page. 399 -> 412 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db (entire server-start + curl sequence run inside single `mcp__workspace__bash` calls, per this sandbox's own constraint that background processes don't persist across separate tool calls): signed up a DM and created three campaigns -- one "Level 3" with tags `["horror", "heavy-combat"]`, one "Tier 2" with tags `["comedic", "one-shot-friendly"]`, and one with neither field set -- and confirmed all three round-tripped correctly through the create API response. Confirmed `GET /api/campaigns?toneTags=horror` returns only the first; confirmed `?toneTags=horror&toneTags=comedic` returns both tagged campaigns (the any-of union); confirmed `?toneTags=not-a-real-tag` returns the full unfiltered set rather than erroring or returning zero. Confirmed the rendered `/campaigns` HTML contains "Starting level: Level 3"/"Starting level: Tier 2" badges and one badge per tone tag ("Horror", "Heavy combat", "Comedic", "One-shot friendly"), and that `/campaigns?toneTags=horror` renders only the horror-tagged campaign's card. Confirmed the campaign detail page renders the same badges. Confirmed `PATCH /api/campaigns/[id]` changing both `startingLevel` and `toneTags` on an existing campaign round-tripped on a subsequent `GET`. Confirmed posting an invalid tag ("not-a-real-tag") on create is rejected with a 400 and a clear zod message. Server, database, and temp response files cleaned up afterward.

Also caught and corrected a process mistake mid-session, worth flagging explicitly since it's the exact pitfall this task's own setup instructions warned about: the first pass of all `lib`/`app`/`components`/test-file edits for this item was made with the Edit tool against the host-mounted `C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo` path rather than the `~/build/session-zero` scratch clone, because Edit/Write/Read only ever operate on that host path. Caught it when a fresh `vitest run` against the scratch clone showed the original 27 `campaigns.test.ts` tests, not the new count -- confirming the scratch clone hadn't actually changed. Recovered by verifying the host repo was otherwise byte-identical to the scratch clone at commit `e8af75a` (diffed a handful of untouched files first), then copying each of the 13 touched files from the host path into the scratch clone via `cp` (both paths are reachable from the same `mcp__workspace__bash` sandbox), and re-running the full `tsc`/`build`/`lint`/`test` gate against the scratch clone from that point forward -- the host-path copies were never committed from or treated as authoritative; the scratch clone (and its own git history) is the only thing that matters for the commit/push in this same increment.

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #43, board reply notifications -- self-identified, numbered backlog exhausted)

The numbered backlog (#1-42) is now fully done, phased, or genuinely blocked on a human/business/credential decision (#3 deployment, #6c email/SMTP, #23/#24 freemium/native-app, #41 phase 2 needs a geocoding API key). Per this task's own "backlog runs dry" fallback instructions, this increment picked its own next item rather than inventing one out of thin air, by first checking the last several session-log entries for an explicitly-flagged, deliberately-scoped-out gap -- and found one immediately: backlog #37's own session log entry (lightweight community discussion boards) says outright, "No new notification type was added for new replies... a thread author currently has to revisit their own thread to see new replies," and names the exact reason it was cut: notifications' two existing link-target columns (`campaign_id`, `related_user_id`) have no natural slot for a thread id. That's a legitimate, well-scoped, high-value gap that undercuts backlog #37's whole premise -- a discussion board where you can't find out someone replied to you is a lot less useful -- so it was picked over inventing a new feature from scratch, following the task's own explicit worked example of this exact scenario.

Design decisions made before writing any code:
* A third link-target column, `notifications.related_thread_id` (nullable, `REFERENCES board_threads(id)`), following the exact precedent `related_user_id` set when backlog #31 (direct messaging) needed a non-campaign link target. The board slug itself is deliberately NOT also stored on the notification row -- it's looked up from the thread at read time (`GET /api/notifications` calls `lib/boards.ts`'s `getThread()`), mirroring `lib/boards.ts`'s own enrich-on-read convention for `authorName`/`replyCount` rather than duplicating denormalized data that could drift. The tradeoff, accepted explicitly: a notification for a since-deleted thread can no longer show a link (it just shows plain text), but the message itself already names the thread by title at creation time, so nothing meaningful is lost.
* Notification batching: `lib/campaignMessages.ts`'s `campaign_chat_message` notifies only on the first unread message since a recipient's last visit, specifically because a live table chat can see many messages in quick succession. A discussion board thread doesn't have that problem -- reply volume is forum-shaped, not chat-shaped -- so the closer precedent is `lib/messages.ts`'s `message_received`, which notifies on every message with no batching and no extra reads-tracking table. Chose that simpler model: the thread's author is notified on every reply from someone else, full stop. No new "last visited a thread" table was needed as a result, keeping this a genuinely narrow pass.
* Only the thread's original author is notified, not every other participant/replier in the thread. This is deliberately scoped to the concrete gap backlog #37 named ("a thread author... to see new replies"), not a broader "subscribe to a thread and get notified on all activity" model -- that would be a bigger, separate feature (more like a mailing-list digest) with its own design questions (do you get notified about your own replies to a thread you're subscribed to? do you unsubscribe?) that this pass doesn't attempt.
* No self-notification: replying to your own thread doesn't notify you. Matches the existing `session_log_kudos` self-kudos exclusion and `campaign_chat_message`'s sender exclusion -- consistent with every other fan-out notification in this app already excluding the actor from their own notification.
* Deleting a thread nulls out any notification's `related_thread_id` rather than leaving a dangling FK reference or deleting the notification itself -- copied directly from `lib/characters.ts`'s `deleteCharacter()`, which does the identical thing to `feed_events.character_id` on character delete, for the identical reason (the notification's own message text already has the relevant name baked in as plain text, so it stays meaningful once the referenced row is gone). Foreign keys are enforced in this app (`db.pragma("foreign_keys = ON")` in `lib/db.ts`), so skipping this step would have made `deleteThread()` throw a constraint-violation error the moment any reply on a thread had ever notified its author -- caught by writing the "notify then delete" test before assuming this could be skipped.

Implementation:
* `lib/types.ts`: added `"board_reply"` to `NOTIFICATION_TYPES` and a matching `NOTIFICATION_LABELS` entry (both are fully data-driven -- `lib/notificationPreferences.ts`'s mute/unmute logic and `/settings/notifications`'s toggle list picked the new type up automatically with no further changes). Added `related_thread_id: string | null` to the `Notification` interface with a doc comment explaining the enrich-at-read-time convention.
* `lib/db.ts`: added `related_thread_id TEXT REFERENCES board_threads(id)` to the `notifications` `CREATE TABLE` statement (with a comment), plus the standard `PRAGMA table_info` migration guard at the bottom (reusing the existing `notificationColumns` snapshot taken when `related_user_id` was migrated in) so an existing database picks up the new column via `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` -- without a `REFERENCES` clause on the migrated path, matching how `related_user_id`'s own migration is also constraint-free (SQLite's `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` can't add a new FK constraint to an existing table).
* `lib/notifications.ts`: `notify()` gained a sixth, optional `relatedThreadId: string | null = null` parameter (following the exact pattern `relatedUserId` set as the fifth optional parameter for #31), threaded through into the inserted row.
* `lib/boards.ts`: `createReply()` now calls `notify()` after inserting the reply, targeting the thread's author (skipped when the replier IS the author), with a message like `"Bob replied to your thread \"How does initiative work?\" on New Player Questions."` and `relatedThreadId` set to the thread's id. `deleteThread()` now runs `UPDATE notifications SET related_thread_id = NULL WHERE related_thread_id = ?` before deleting the thread's replies and the thread itself.
* `app/api/notifications/route.ts`: `GET` now maps each returned notification through a small enrichment step, looking up `getThread(n.related_thread_id)?.board_slug` when `related_thread_id` is set, attaching it as a computed `boardSlug` field (not persisted, purely a response-shape addition).
* `app/notifications/page.tsx`: added a `NotificationRow` client-side type (`Notification & { boardSlug: BoardSlug | null }`) and a second conditional link, `View thread` -> `/boards/<boardSlug>/<related_thread_id>`, rendered alongside the existing `View campaign` link whenever both fields are present.

Testing: extended `__tests__/unit/boards.test.ts` with a new `describe("board_reply notifications (backlog #43)")` block, 5 tests -- notifying the thread's author when someone else replies (checking both the notification's `related_thread_id` and that its message names the replier and the thread title), no notification on a self-reply, a fresh notification firing again on a second reply from someone else rather than being batched, a muted `board_reply` preference being respected, and deleting the thread nulling out `related_thread_id` on the existing notification while its message text still names the thread. Extended `__tests__/unit/notifications.test.ts` with 1 test covering `notify()`'s new `relatedThreadId` parameter directly (defaulting to null when omitted, correctly set when a real thread id is passed -- using an actual `createThread()`-created thread rather than a fabricated id, since `related_thread_id` is a real enforced FK and an arbitrary string throws a `SqliteError: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`, caught by running the test suite once already believing this test was done). 412 -> 418 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manual smoke test against a real `next start` server (spare port 4321, fresh `SQLITE_DB_PATH`), entire signup/post/reply/notification/mute/delete sequence run inside a single `mcp__workspace__bash` call per this sandbox's own constraint that backgrounded processes don't survive across separate tool calls: signed up Alice and Bob; Alice posted a thread on `new-player-questions`; Bob replied and Alice's `GET /api/notifications` showed exactly one `board_reply` notification with the correct `related_thread_id`, message text (naming Bob and the thread title), and an enriched `boardSlug` of `"new-player-questions"`; Alice replied to her own thread and confirmed the `board_reply` count stayed at 1 (no self-notification); Alice muted `board_reply` via `PUT /api/notifications/preferences`, Bob replied again, and the count correctly stayed at 1; Alice re-enabled the preference, Bob replied once more, and the count correctly went to 2 (confirming un-muting resumes delivery and each of Bob's replies notifies independently, not batched); Alice deleted the thread via `DELETE /api/boards/new-player-questions/threads/<id>` and confirmed both existing `board_reply` notifications now show `related_thread_id: null` and `boardSlug: null` while their `message` text still reads "...replied to your thread \"How does initiative work?\"..." -- proving the FK-null-out worked and the message stayed meaningful; confirmed `/notifications` still renders 200 for Alice; confirmed `GET /api/notifications/preferences` lists the new `board_reply` type with its label. Server, SQLite db (including `-wal`/`-shm`), and cookie jars cleaned up afterward.

Also caught and corrected the exact process mistake this task's own setup instructions warned about (and a prior session's log entry above also hit): the first full pass of every `lib`/`app`/test-file edit for this item was made with the Edit tool, which only ever operates on the host-mounted `C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo` path, not the `~/build/session-zero` scratch clone the bash tool builds/tests against. Caught it immediately -- the very first `npx tsc --noEmit`/`vitest run` against the scratch clone came back completely clean with the original 412-test count, which was the tell (a change that should have added tests and touched multiple files couldn't possibly leave the count unchanged). Recovered by re-applying every change as a Python string-replacement script run through `mcp__workspace__bash` directly against the scratch clone (never touching the host path again for anything but read-only reference lookups), then re-running the full `tsc`/`build`/`lint`/`test` gate from that point forward. The host-mounted copies of these files are now stale/ahead of the scratch clone's git history in a few places but were never committed from and are overwritten wholesale by this session's own step-11 targeted rsync sync at the end, so no inconsistency survives past this entry.

2026-09-07 (live session, continued: backlog #44, dedicated unread-message badge in the nav -- self-identified, numbered backlog exhausted at #43)

The numbered backlog (#1-43) is fully done, phased, or genuinely blocked on a human/business/credential decision (#3 deployment, #6c email/SMTP, #23/#24 freemium/native-app, #41 phase 2 needs a geocoding API key, #42 flagged-only). Per this task's own "backlog runs dry" fallback instructions, this increment picked its own next item again -- this time by working through the task's own supplied candidate list rather than a fresh session-log-flagged gap (backlog #43 already used up the one session-log-flagged gap this file had on hand). The first candidate on that list was the one worth investigating first: "does the unread-badge/notification system correctly cover all of these consistently (e.g., does `/messages` have its own unread indicator in the nav the way `/notifications` does)?"

Before writing any code, verified this was a real, still-open gap rather than something already covered: read `components/NavBar.tsx` in full and confirmed it renders exactly one unread badge, on the "Notifications" link, driven by `lib/notifications.ts`'s `getUnreadCount()` (a single aggregate over the whole `notifications` table, pushed live via `lib/notificationEvents.ts` + `/api/notifications/stream`, falling back to 15s polling). The "Messages" link right next to it is a plain, unbadged `<Link>`. Confirmed `message_received` (#31), `campaign_chat_message` (#32), and `board_reply` (#43) are all real, registered `NOTIFICATION_TYPES` that do feed into that one aggregate count -- so a new DM already nudges the Notifications badge today, just not distinguishably from a join-request or a schedule change, and never on the "Messages" link itself. Separately confirmed `lib/messages.ts`'s `listConversations()` already computes a real, correct per-conversation `unreadCount` (backed by `messages.read`, a completely different table/mechanism from `notifications`) -- it's just never summed or surfaced anywhere near the nav. This matched the candidate's own framing exactly: a real, user-facing inconsistency (four different "you have something new" mechanisms -- table chat's `getUnreadCampaignMessageCount()` isn't even surfaced anywhere, not that this pass touches it -- with only one of them reaching the nav), not a nonexistent problem invented to have something to build.

Design decisions made before writing any code:
* Scope: fix specifically the "/messages has no indicator" gap the candidate named, not a broader unification of every unread mechanism in the app. `lib/campaignMessages.ts`'s `getUnreadCampaignMessageCount()` (per-campaign table chat) stays exactly as it is today -- used only internally to gate the first-unread-since-visit `campaign_chat_message` notification, with no UI surface of its own even on the campaign page itself. Surfacing that one too would be a reasonable follow-up but is a separate, campaign-page-scoped design question (where would it even render? a per-campaign-card badge on some list of "my campaigns"? there's no such list today outside `/profile`) that this pass doesn't attempt, matching how #34's cross-community resource library and #41 phase 2 are explicitly deferred elsewhere in this file rather than silently expanded into.
* A second counter, not a filtered read of the first: rather than deriving a "Messages" badge number by filtering the existing aggregate `getUnreadCount()` down to `type = 'message_received'` rows, the badge is driven by the messages table directly (`getTotalUnreadMessageCount()`, a plain `COUNT(*) WHERE recipient_id = ? AND read = 0`). This was the more honest choice: `markConversationRead()` (opening a conversation) already independently clears `messages.read` without touching the `notifications` table at all (documented in that function's own pre-existing doc comment -- "reading a conversation doesn't retroactively mark its notifications read"), so a filtered-notifications approach would show a stale, too-high Messages count the moment someone reads a conversation without also visiting `/notifications`. The two counters can legitimately disagree (a muted `message_received` preference is another case: the notifications-table count would permanently undercount messages for that user), and that's fine -- they're answering two different questions ("how many things are waiting in your general inbox" vs. "how many actual messages haven't been read"), not one fact duplicated in two places.
* A dedicated second SSE stream + pub/sub module, `lib/messageEvents.ts` + `GET /api/messages/stream`, rather than piggybacking a second event type onto the existing `/api/notifications/stream` connection. Mirrors `lib/notificationEvents.ts` file-for-file (same `EventEmitter`-per-process shape, same `user:<id>` channel-naming, same doc-comment explaining why a single in-process emitter is fine for this app's one-process `next start` deployment and where a future horizontally-scaled version would swap in a shared broker) rather than inventing a new abstraction. Considered folding this into the existing stream (one connection, two event types) to save a second `EventSource`/heartbeat/reconnect cycle per signed-in user, but two independent single-purpose modules matched this codebase's own established preference for a dedicated table/module per concern over a shared polymorphic one more closely (the same reasoning `campaign_ratings` got its own table instead of relaxing `ratings.ratee_id`'s FK target, per backlog #28's own session log entry) -- and it keeps `notificationEvents.ts` itself completely untouched, at zero risk of regressing the existing, already-tested notifications badge.
* NavBar wiring mirrors the existing notifications effect exactly, one level down: a second `useEffect` declaring its own `pollOnce`/`startPolling` functions *inside* the effect body (not at component scope), specifically to avoid the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` ESLint gotcha this file's own "Tooling notes" section already documents hitting once before ("`NavBar`'s polling effect avoids the whole issue by declaring its loader function inside the effect body itself"). The polling fallback reuses the existing `GET /api/messages` endpoint (summing `conversations[].unreadCount` client-side) rather than adding a new lightweight polling-only endpoint -- `/api/messages` already returns exactly the data needed and this app's per-user conversation volume is small (the same "stays cheap" reasoning `listConversations()`'s own doc comment already gives for doing its own dedup/ordering in JS over a SQL window function), so a second endpoint purely to shave a few unused fields off the response wasn't worth the extra surface area.

Implementation:
* `lib/messages.ts`: new `getTotalUnreadMessageCount(userId)` (exported). `sendMessage()` now calls `publishUnreadMessageCount(recipientId, getTotalUnreadMessageCount(recipientId))` after inserting and notifying. `markConversationRead()` now calls `publishUnreadMessageCount(userId, getTotalUnreadMessageCount(userId))` after the read-marking `UPDATE`.
* `lib/messageEvents.ts` (new): `publishUnreadMessageCount()`/`subscribeToUnreadMessageCount()`, mirroring `lib/notificationEvents.ts`'s `publishUnreadCount()`/`subscribeToUnreadCount()` shape exactly.
* `app/api/messages/stream/route.ts` (new): `GET`, `requireUser()`-gated, sends an immediate `{ unreadCount }` snapshot via `getTotalUnreadMessageCount()` then forwards every subsequent `subscribeToUnreadMessageCount()` publish as an `unread` SSE event, with the same 20s heartbeat/abort-cleanup shape as `/api/notifications/stream`.
* `components/NavBar.tsx`: new `unreadMessageCount` state and a second `useEffect` (SSE-with-polling-fallback, mirroring the existing notifications effect) driving it. The "Messages" `<Link>` gained `className="relative"` and the identical red-circle badge markup the "Notifications" link already uses, now shown conditionally when `unreadMessageCount > 0`.

Testing: `__tests__/unit/messages.test.ts` gained a `getTotalUnreadMessageCount (backlog #44)` describe block, 5 tests -- zero for a brand-new user with no messages at all; summing unread messages across multiple conversations rather than just the most recent one; a viewer's own outgoing messages never counting toward their own unread total; the count dropping to zero for one conversation's messages once read via `markConversationRead()` while another conversation's unread messages stay counted; and per-user isolation (a stranger with no messages sees 0). New `__tests__/unit/messageEvents.test.ts`, 4 tests mirroring `notificationEvents.test.ts`'s own structure -- the pub/sub publishing an unread-count update to the recipient's channel on each `sendMessage()` call (`[1, 2]` across two sends); publishing nothing at all to the sender's own channel; publishing an update on `markConversationRead()`; and only notifying the matching user's own subscribers, not a bystander's. `__tests__/integration/http.test.ts` gained a 7th test (alongside the existing 6e notifications-SSE test it mirrors one level down): a signed-out request to `/api/messages/stream` 401s; a signed-in connection receives an immediate `"unreadCount":0` snapshot; and a real `POST /api/messages/[userId]` from another user pushes a live `unread` event with a nonzero count over the wire before the reader connection is cancelled. 418 -> 428 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

**2026-09-07 (live session, continued): backlog #45 (table group chat's own unread-count UI) session log.** The numbered backlog (#1-44) was fully done/phased/blocked at the start of this increment, so this session followed the task's own "backlog runs dry" fallback -- and this time didn't even need to invent a candidate list: backlog #44's own session log entry (just above) explicitly named the gap -- "surfacing table chat's own unread count somewhere" -- as the natural next thing to pick up. Verified it before writing any code by reading `lib/campaignMessages.ts`, `components/CampaignChatPanel.tsx`, and `components/NavBar.tsx`: table chat already had a real per-campaign unread count (`getUnreadCampaignMessageCount()`) and already fired a `campaign_chat_message` notification on the first unread message since a visit, but nothing anywhere read that count back out for display -- no nav badge, no per-campaign indicator, nothing on the campaign page itself. Confirmed genuinely open, so built it.

Design, mirroring the #43/#44 pattern one level down: `getTotalUnreadCampaignMessageCountForUser()` (new, `lib/campaignMessages.ts`) sums `getUnreadCampaignMessageCount()` across every campaign a user DMs or actively plays in, reusing `lib/profiles.ts`'s `myCampaigns()` -- the same "campaigns I have access to" set `lib/sessionReminders.ts`'s `checkAndFireSessionRemindersForUser()` already reuses, rather than re-deriving it. A sibling `getUnreadCampaignMessageCountsForUser()` returns the same thing broken out per campaign id (omitting zero-count campaigns), for list-level display. A third small pub/sub, `lib/campaignChatEvents.ts`, mirrors `lib/messageEvents.ts`/`lib/notificationEvents.ts` exactly (single in-process `EventEmitter`, `setMaxListeners(0)`, a `user:<id>` channel per user) -- kept as its own module for the same reason `messageEvents.ts` stayed separate from `notificationEvents.ts`: this is a third independently-drifting counter over yet another table pairing (`campaign_messages`/`campaign_message_reads`), and opening one campaign's chat clears only that campaign's contribution to it without touching the notifications or direct-message tables at all. `sendCampaignMessage()` now publishes every other active party member's fresh total after inserting a message (not just the ones who get the `campaign_chat_message` notification under the first-unread-since-visit rule -- the badge should reflect every new message, the notification-suppression logic is a separate concern that happens to overlap); `markCampaignChatRead()` publishes the reader's own fresh total, exactly mirroring `markConversationRead()`'s publish-on-read call in `lib/messages.ts`.

A new `GET /api/campaigns/chat/stream` SSE route (`app/api/campaigns/chat/stream/route.ts`) mirrors `/api/messages/stream` line-for-line (immediate snapshot on connect, 20s heartbeat, `request.signal`-driven cleanup). It lives under `app/api/campaigns/chat/` as a sibling of the dynamic `app/api/campaigns/[id]/` segment rather than inside it, since this is an aggregate across every campaign the signed-in user has access to, not scoped to one campaign id -- confirmed Next.js resolves the literal `chat` path segment ahead of the `[id]` dynamic one (no real campaign id could ever collide with the literal string "chat" anyway, since ids are UUIDs).

Placement was the real judgment call this item raised, since -- unlike Messages and Notifications, which each have their own top-level NavBar link to carry a badge -- there's no "my tables" nav link for table chat to hang one on, and table chat itself is scoped per-campaign, not a single top-level surface. Considered and rejected adding a new top-level nav link (more surface than a one-item pass justified, and this app's nav is already fairly long) in favor of three complementary, smaller pieces:
1. **NavBar aggregate badge** rides the existing signed-in-user link (display name -> `/profile`), on the reasoning that `/profile`'s "My campaigns" list is already the natural place to see *which* table has new chat, so the badge on the link that leads there is a reasonable, discoverable choice even though it's nominally an "account" link rather than a dedicated "chat" one.
2. **Per-campaign badges on `/profile`'s "My campaigns" list** (both "Running (as DM)" and "Playing in" sections), sourced from a new `unreadCampaignChatCounts` map added to `GET /api/profile`'s existing response (reusing that endpoint rather than adding a new one, the same "reuse an existing listing endpoint" convention `GET /api/messages`'s and `GET /api/notifications?pageSize=1`'s own polling-fallback reuse already established). This is deliberately the *only* place a per-campaign count can sit still and stay accurate to read, since:
3. **A one-time `initialUnreadCount` snapshot on the campaign detail page itself**, next to `CampaignChatPanel`'s "Table chat" heading -- computed server-side in `app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx` (a server component) *before* the client panel's own `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/chat` fires, because that GET route already calls `markCampaignChatRead()` as a side effect (an existing, pre-#45 convention this item didn't change). A live badge inside the campaign page would therefore always read zero by the time it could render anything -- the honest signal here is a one-time "here's what you're about to catch up on" snapshot, not a counter that claims to be live where it structurally can't be.

The host-mounted copy is overwritten wholesale by this session's own step-11 targeted rsync sync at the end regardless, so no inconsistency survives past this entry -- but noting it here since it's now the third time in a row this exact trap has caught an agent on this task, which may be worth a sharper warning or a tooling fix upstream rather than relying on each session to individually notice and self-correct.

**2026-09-07 (live session, continued): backlog #46 (calendar export, self-identified) session log.** The numbered backlog (#1-45) was fully done, phased, or genuinely blocked on a human/business/credential decision (#3 deployment, #6c email/SMTP, #23/#24 freemium/native, #41 phase 2 geocoding), so this increment followed the task's own "backlog runs dry" fallback and picked the calendar-export seed the task's own prompt suggested, after confirming it was still genuinely open: grepped for any existing `.ics`/`calendar`/`VCALENDAR` reference anywhere in the repo and found none, and confirmed `next_session_at` (backlog #35) plus the RSVP data that shipped alongside it had no export path of any kind out of this app.

Before writing any code, resolved three open design questions:
* **Private or public?** `app/campaigns/[id]/page.tsx` only renders `ScheduleForm` inside `{access && (...)}` where `access = hasPrivateAccess(...)`, and `lib/access.ts`'s own doc comment names "session log, party notes, schedule" as this app's private trio -- so the new export route follows that intended boundary (`hasPrivateAccess`, 403 for a non-member) even though `GET /api/campaigns/[id]` itself already leaks the raw `next_session_at` field to any caller regardless of access (a pre-existing gap, left alone -- not this item's job to fix).
* **How to represent RSVP status in the export?** Rather than a second export or a query param, the single VEVENT's DESCRIPTION includes the downloading viewer's own current RSVP response, read fresh via the existing `getViewerRsvp()` -- confirmed/declined/hasn't-answered-yet -- so RSVP status becomes context on the one event that matters instead of a parallel feature.
* **Session length for DTEND?** This app has no per-campaign duration field anywhere (same gap #18's stats dashboard already flagged for "hours at the table"), so a fixed `DEFAULT_SESSION_DURATION_MINUTES = 180` (3 hours) is used rather than omitting DTEND, which would make most calendar apps render the event as an all-day block.

Implementation:
* `lib/calendarExport.ts` (new): `escapeIcsText()` (RFC 5545 TEXT escaping -- backslash escaped first so it doesn't double-escape the semicolon/comma/newline escapes that follow), `foldIcsLine()` (75-octet line folding with a single leading-space continuation, per spec), `formatIcsUtc()` (ISO instant -> `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ`), and `buildSessionIcs()` -- the only exported function that assembles a full document, pure and DB-free by design so it's directly unit-testable without touching SQLite. Its UID is keyed on `(campaignId, exact session instant)` -- stable across repeated exports of the same still-scheduled session (so a calendar app that dedupes by UID updates rather than duplicates), but a genuine reschedule produces a new UID rather than colliding with whatever an importer already holds for the old date.
* `app/api/campaigns/[id]/calendar/route.ts` (new): `GET`, `requireUser()` + `hasPrivateAccess()` gated (403), 404 when the campaign doesn't exist or has no `next_session_at` set, otherwise calls `buildSessionIcs()` and returns `new NextResponse(ics, { headers: { "Content-Type": "text/calendar; charset=utf-8", "Content-Disposition": 'attachment; filename="..."' } })` -- the first non-JSON, non-SSE API response this codebase has shipped. The filename is derived from the campaign title (non-alphanumeric runs collapsed to a single hyphen, lowercased) falling back to `"session.ics"` for a title that strips to nothing.
* `components/ScheduleForm.tsx`: a small "Add to calendar (.ics)" anchor added next to the existing status/date line, shown whenever `nextSessionAt` is set -- no new top-level nav surface or dedicated page, matching how RSVP/chat/notes all already live inline on the campaign detail page.

Testing: new `__tests__/unit/calendarExport.test.ts`, 18 tests, all pure (no DB): `escapeIcsText`'s four escape cases including the backslash-escaped-first ordering; `foldIcsLine` leaving a short line untouched and, for a long one, confirming the original content round-trips exactly from the folded pieces (not just "it looks folded"); `formatIcsUtc`'s exact format and sub-second stripping; and `buildSessionIcs` covering a well-formed CRLF-terminated `VCALENDAR`/`VEVENT` wrapper, DTSTART/DTEND exactly `DEFAULT_SESSION_DURATION_MINUTES` apart, SUMMARY/DESCRIPTION content, all three RSVP-status description cases, LOCATION using the campaign's explicit location when set, falling back to "Online/Remote" only when unset **and** `session_format === "remote"`, and omitted entirely otherwise, a stable UID across two exports of the identical session and a different UID after a reschedule, and correct comma-escaping inside a campaign title. 439 -> 457 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (confirmed `/api/campaigns/[id]/calendar` appears in the route manifest), and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port (4178) with a fresh SQLite db, the entire signup/campaign-create/join/approve/schedule/RSVP/download sequence run inside a single `mcp__workspace__bash` call per this sandbox's own backgrounded-process constraint (using a `mktemp -d` workdir after an earlier attempt collided with a stale, permission-denied `/tmp/campaign.json` left over from an unrelated earlier process on this shared sandbox -- switching to a private temp directory fixed it cleanly). Confirmed: `GET .../calendar` 404s with "No upcoming session is scheduled." before a date is set; 403s for a signed-in user who hasn't joined; 401s when signed out; and 200s with `Content-Type: text/calendar; charset=utf-8` and `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="curse-of-strahd-redux.ics"` once scheduled. The downloaded body correctly folded a long UID line across two physical lines with a leading-space continuation, showed "Your RSVP: Confirmed" for the player who'd RSVPed and "You haven't RSVP'd yet." for the DM who hadn't, and correctly escaped the comma in both "Curse of Strahd, Redux" (title) and "Austin, TX" (location) as `\,`. Confirmed the rendered campaign-detail HTML included a working "Add to calendar (.ics)" link pointing at the right campaign id. Server process and temp workdir (db, cookie jars, response bodies) fully cleaned up afterward; confirmed no orphaned `next start` process or listening port remained.
