2026-09-08 (live session, continued)
Picked backlog item #47 (self-identified) as the night's work: the numbered backlog (#1-46) was fully done/phased/blocked, so per the task's own fallback instructions this session worked through the seed candidate list the task prompt itself suggested -- campaign templates/duplicate campaign, sub-request digest/sort improvements, board search, a printable/exportable character sheet, and a site-admin/moderation role. Before committing to one, grepped the codebase for "duplicate"/"template" and for board search to confirm the first and third candidates were both still genuinely unbuilt, then weighed the options: the admin/moderation role is a real, previously-flagged gap (backlog #37's own session log entry) but is a much bigger lift -- a genuine new role concept threaded through the auth model -- than fits cleanly in one increment; duplicate campaign needed no new schema, no new role, and reuses existing `createCampaign`/`updateCampaign` validation wholesale, making it the cleanest fully-buildable-in-one-pass option while still being a real, concrete DM quality-of-life win (a DM running recurring one-shots or a west-marches table currently has to re-type every field from scratch each time).

Implementation:
* `lib/campaigns.ts`: new `duplicateCampaign(id, dmId)`. Looks up the source campaign, throws `CampaignError` if it doesn't exist or the caller isn't its DM, then calls `createCampaign()` with the source's re-usable setup fields (title suffixed "(Copy)", description, system, capacity, location, `newPlayerFriendly`, `sessionFormat`, `startingLevel`, `toneTags`). Danger level needed a second step: `createCampaign()` has never accepted a `dangerLevel` parameter at all (it's only ever been settable after creation, via `updateCampaign` -- matching the fact that `/campaigns/new`'s own form has no danger-level field either), so `duplicateCampaign()` follows up with `updateCampaign(copy.id, dmId, { dangerLevel: source.danger_level })` when the source has one set, rather than changing `createCampaign()`'s long-standing signature for this one caller.
* `app/api/campaigns/[id]/duplicate/route.ts` (new): `POST`, `requireUser()`-gated, calls `duplicateCampaign()` and returns the new campaign with a 201, `errorResponse(err)` on failure (400 for "not found" or "not the DM," matching every other DM-only mutation route's status-code convention in this codebase).
* `components/DmControls.tsx`: a new "Duplicate campaign" button alongside Edit/Cancel/Reopen (DM-only, same visibility as the rest of this panel), a `duplicate()` handler that POSTs to the new route and `router.push()`s straight to the new campaign's own detail page on success.

Judgment call on scope, following this app's own established "private side" boundary: the roster, schedule (`next_session_at`), session log, party notes, table chat, resource vault, initiative tracker, NPC notes, sub requests, and ratings are all deliberately NOT copied. These all describe one specific run of a table (who's actually playing, what actually happened, what's actually scheduled) rather than the reusable setup a DM would want to relaunch — carrying any of them over would either be meaningless for a brand-new table (an empty session log makes sense; a copied one full of "session 4" recaps for players who were never in "session 4" of the copy would not) or actively wrong (a copied roster would imply the same real people are already approved into a campaign they've never requested to join). The duplicate therefore starts exactly like any other freshly created campaign: open for requests, empty roster, nothing scheduled — the DM relaunches it the same way they would any new campaign.

Testing: `__tests__/unit/campaigns.test.ts` gained a new `duplicateCampaign (backlog #47)` describe block, 6 tests -- the full setup-field copy landing correctly on a brand-new campaign id owned by the same DM; a set danger level carrying over via the post-creation update path; danger level staying unset on the copy when the original never had one; the copy starting open/uncancelled/unscheduled with an empty roster even when the original campaign has an approved member and other live state, while the original's own state stays untouched by duplicating it; rejecting duplication by anyone other than the campaign's own DM; and rejecting an unknown campaign id. Caught two test-writing mistakes against this codebase's own real enum values and function signatures before they became false failures: `DANGER_LEVELS` doesn't include a plain `"high"` (the real values are `low-lethality`/`moderate`/`high-lethality`/`deadly-osr`), and `approveRequest()`'s real signature is `(membershipId, dmId)`, not `(campaignId, dmId, membershipId)` -- both fixed by checking `lib/types.ts` and `lib/memberships.ts` directly rather than guessing from nearby code. 457 -> 463 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (confirmed `/api/campaigns/[id]/duplicate` appears in the route manifest), and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Server processes and temp db/response files cleaned up afterward.

2026-09-08 (live session, continued)
Picked backlog item #48 (self-identified) as this increment's work: the numbered backlog (#1-47) was fully done/phased/blocked, so per the task's own fallback instructions this session considered the candidate list the task prompt itself offered (sub-request sort/hide, board search, character-sheet export, a site-admin/moderation role) plus one already flagged twice in this file's own session log -- backlog #37's own entry explicitly called out "no admin/reporting system of any kind" as an intentional-but-open scope cut, and #47's own session log repeated that same observation while explaining why it passed on tackling it that night ("a much bigger lift -- a new role concept in the auth model -- than fits a single clean increment"). Re-scoped it deliberately narrow before starting, per the task's own explicit guidance to prefer a smaller complete slice over a half-built broad one: a plain boolean flag, not a roles table; an env-var grant mechanism, not a UI; and moderation power limited to boards only, matching the same boundary #37 itself already drew.

Implementation:
* `lib/db.ts`: `users.is_admin INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (new column on the existing `CREATE TABLE`, plus the standard `ALTER TABLE` migration guard for pre-existing databases). A new `board_reports` table (`id`, `reporter_id`, `thread_id`, `reply_id`, `reason`, `created_at`) with a `CHECK` constraint enforcing exactly one of `thread_id`/`reply_id` per row, and two partial `UNIQUE` indexes (`(reporter_id, thread_id) WHERE thread_id IS NOT NULL` and the reply-id equivalent) enforcing "once per reporter per target" at the schema level, not just in application code.
* `lib/auth.ts`: a new `isAdminEmail()` helper compares a candidate email against `process.env.ADMIN_EMAIL`, normalized the same way (`trim().toLowerCase()`) as every other email comparison in this file. `signUp()` now sets `is_admin` on the inserted row based on this check -- grant-at-creation only, not retroactive if `ADMIN_EMAIL` is set later against an already-existing account (a deliberate simplification; re-granting later would need its own admin-management UI, which is exactly the bigger feature this pass is avoiding).
* `lib/access.ts`: `isSiteAdmin(userId)` -- a plain `SELECT is_admin FROM users WHERE id = ?`, sitting alongside `isDm()`/`hasPrivateAccess()` as this file's third access predicate, but explicitly campaign-independent (no `campaignId` parameter) since it's a site-wide flag, not a per-table role.
* `lib/boards.ts`: `requireAuthor()` (backlog #37) became `requireAuthorOrAdmin()` -- passes if the caller is the post's own author *or* `isSiteAdmin(userId)` is true, mirroring `lib/campaignResources.ts`'s existing `requireUploaderOrDm()` pattern (the lib function itself does the fine-grained authorization check by calling another lib predicate, rather than trusting a boolean the route layer would otherwise have to pass down and could get wrong). `deleteThread()`/`deleteReply()` needed no new parameters as a result -- the existing `DELETE` routes automatically gained admin-delete-any-post power for free. Both delete functions also now clean up any `board_reports` rows referencing the content being deleted (`foreign_keys = ON` in `lib/db.ts` means the delete would otherwise fail with a raw SQLite FK-constraint error) -- deleted outright, not nulled like the existing `notifications.related_thread_id` cleanup, since once an admin has removed a post there's nothing left for a report to point at. New `reportThread()`/`reportReply()` validate an optional reason (trimmed, capped at 500 chars) and reject a second report from the same reporter on the same target (`BoardError`, mirroring `lib/follows.ts`'s "you're already following them" duplicate-relationship convention rather than upserting, since a report has no mutable content worth updating). `hasReportedThread()`/`hasReportedReply()` back the thread page's per-viewer "already reported" state, the same pattern as sub-requests' `viewerHasVolunteered`. `listReportedContent()` aggregates `board_reports` by target, joins back to the live thread/reply row (skipping -- defensively, not expected in practice -- a report whose target was somehow deleted without its reports being cleaned up), and sorts by report count descending with most-recently-reported as the tie-break.
* Two new routes, `POST /api/boards/[slug]/threads/[threadId]/report` and the reply equivalent one level down: `requireUser()`-gated (any signed-in user, not just active party members -- boards have no relationship gate for reading/posting either, see #37's own reasoning), zod-validated reason, calling the new lib functions.
* `app/admin/boards/page.tsx` (new): a server component, not an API-backed client page -- reads `listReportedContent()` directly, the same "server component does the DB read" convention `app/boards/[slug]/[threadId]/page.tsx` already uses. Gated inline: a non-admin (including signed-out) sees a plain "Admin access required." message rather than a 404 or redirect, matching this app's existing preference for rendered-HTML-content boundary checks over hard navigation gates (see the HTTP integration suite's own privacy-boundary test). Each queue row reuses the existing `DeletePostButton` component pointed at the same `DELETE` routes campaign #37 already shipped -- no new delete route needed, since `requireAuthorOrAdmin()` already covers the admin case.
* `components/ReportPostButton.tsx` (new): mirrors `DeletePostButton`'s click-to-expand shape one level down in destructiveness -- reporting isn't itself destructive (only an admin's own subsequent delete is), so it's a plain expand-to-reason-field-then-submit rather than a two-step "yes really" confirm. Wired into `app/boards/[slug]/[threadId]/page.tsx` next to both the thread and each reply, shown to any signed-in viewer who isn't that post's own author, alongside `alreadyReported` computed server-side per post via `hasReportedThread()`/`hasReportedReply()`.
* `components/NavBar.tsx` / `app/layout.tsx`: `NavBar`'s `user` prop gained an optional `isAdmin` flag (computed in the server-component `layout.tsx` via `isSiteAdmin()`, alongside the existing `displayName`), rendering a new "Admin" link only when true.

Judgment call on the reporting system itself: the task's framing offered "just the `is_admin` flag + admin-can-delete-any-board-post" as an acceptable smaller fallback if the full scope (flag + reporting + admin view) proved too much for one clean pass. After scoping it out, reporting turned out to be a small, self-contained addition (one table, two small lib functions, two thin routes, one button component) rather than a second major subsystem, and skipping it would have left the admin power real but undiscoverable -- an admin with delete authority but no queue of what to review isn't a very useful admin. Built the full scope as a result, but kept it deliberately thin: no report categories/severity, no report-dismissal action (a report simply stops appearing once its content is deleted -- there's no "reviewed, keeping it" path that clears a report without deleting the post), and no moderation history/audit log. Those are real gaps for a production moderation tool but are the same category of "bigger, separate feature" #37's own session log already named as out of scope.

Judgment call on `ADMIN_EMAIL` granting at signup only, not on every request: an alternative design would check `ADMIN_EMAIL` live on every `isSiteAdmin()` call instead of persisting `is_admin` to the row, which would let changing the env var retroactively affect an existing account without a migration. Rejected in favor of a persisted flag because (a) it matches this app's own established pattern of persisting a fact at the moment it's true rather than re-deriving it from external config on every read (e.g. `campaigns.new_player_friendly` isn't recomputed from some external rule each time either), and (b) a live env-var check would mean losing admin status the moment `ADMIN_EMAIL` is unset or changed, which is a worse failure mode for an already-signed-up admin than "you'd need a new account or a manual DB edit to become one in the first place."

**Explicitly not solved end-to-end today, the same category of documented limitation as the lazy session-reminder mechanism from backlog #35:** `ADMIN_EMAIL` is unset in this sandbox, so running the test suite or a smoke test here does not make anyone a real admin against the app's actual persistent data -- only the unit test that sets the env var directly (see below) and the manual smoke test's own temporary server process (which had it set explicitly for that one run) ever exercised the grant path. The real owner, `bill.biller@gmail.com` per this project's own context, would need `ADMIN_EMAIL` set in whatever hosting environment backlog #3 eventually settles on for this to take effect for a real account.

Testing: see the Testing section above for the full breakdown (13 new tests: 4 in `auth.test.ts`'s new `ADMIN_EMAIL (backlog #48)` block, 9 in `boards.test.ts`'s new `site-admin moderation (backlog #48)` block). 463 -> 476 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (confirmed `/admin/boards`, `/api/boards/[slug]/threads/[threadId]/report`, and `/api/boards/[slug]/threads/[threadId]/replies/[replyId]/report` all in the route manifest), and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db, `ADMIN_EMAIL=bill.biller@gmail.com` set in the server's own environment before starting it, run inside a single `mcp__workspace__bash` call per this sandbox's own backgrounded-process constraint (confirmed again this session: a server backgrounded in one bash call does not survive into a later, separate call -- the whole signup/report/delete sequence had to run in one shot). Signed up with that exact email and confirmed the NavBar's "Admin" link and the `/admin/boards` page ("Reported board content") both rendered for that account; signed up a second user (Alice) and confirmed she saw neither the nav link nor the page content ("Admin access required." instead). Alice posted a thread; a third user (Bob) reported it (`201`) and a second report attempt from Bob correctly 400'd ("You've already reported this."); the admin's `/admin/boards` queue correctly showed the thread with "1 report" and a working delete button. Confirmed self-moderation still works unchanged: Alice successfully deleted her own thread (`200`). Alice then posted a second thread; Bob (a non-admin, non-author) attempting to delete it correctly 403'd ("Only the person who posted this thread, or a site admin, can delete it."); the admin account then successfully deleted that same thread (`200`), and a follow-up `GET` on the thread's own page confirmed a `404`. Server process and temp db/cookie-jar files cleaned up afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). The numbered backlog (#1-47) plus this session's own #48 are now all done, phased, or blocked on a decision outside this session's authority.

2026-09-08 (live session, continued): picked backlog item #49 (character sheet export/print view, self-identified) as this increment's work -- the numbered backlog is exhausted at #48, so this again followed the task's own "backlog runs dry" fallback, from the task's own seed candidate list (sub-request sort/hide, board search, character-sheet export, a UI/accessibility pass). Chose character-sheet export over the others: sub-request sort/hide and board search are both real but smaller UX polish on already-working list views, an accessibility pass is valuable but produces no single demoable feature and this session had no jsdom/Testing Library setup to verify fixes against beyond manual smoke-testing anyway, while character export was flagged explicitly as a candidate back in backlog #47's own rationale and had been passed over twice already (#47 picked duplicate-campaign, #48 picked admin/moderation) -- and a character (backlog #15/19/26) now genuinely carries enough data (name, class/ancestry/role, bio, backstory, status, epilogue, portrait) that having no way to get it out of the app was a real, user-facing gap, not a nice-to-have.

Investigated the existing character surface first: `lib/characters.ts`'s `getCharacter()`/`listCharactersForUser()`/`listCharactersForCampaign()`, the `Character` type in `lib/types.ts`, and `components/CharacterSummary.tsx` -- a single presentational component already shared by `/profile` (editable, via `CharacterManager`), `/players/[id]` (read-only), and the campaign detail page's roster-adjacent characters list, explicitly documented as shared "so none of the three views can drift apart." Confirmed there is no existing per-character detail page or route, and confirmed (by reading `app/players/[id]/page.tsx`) that every field on a character is already rendered to any visitor, signed in or not -- meaning a character carries no privacy boundary of its own to respect, unlike party notes/session log/table chat.

Design, following the `lib/calendarExport.ts` / `GET /api/campaigns/[id]/calendar` precedent from backlog #46 one-for-one: `lib/characterExport.ts`'s `buildCharacterSheetText()` is a pure, DB-free function (character fields + resolved owner name + resolved campaign title in, plain text out) so the API route stays a thin DB-reading wrapper around it, matching this app's own `lib/*.ts` vs. `app/api/*/route.ts` split. `GET /api/characters/[id]/export` has no `requireUser()` call at all -- a deliberate departure from most other routes in this app, justified explicitly in the route's own comment, since gating a download of already-fully-public data would be strictly more restrictive than the page that already shows the same data to anyone. Returns `text/plain` with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and a slugified filename, the same non-JSON-response shape `.ics` export introduced.

`app/characters/[id]/page.tsx` is a new Server Component: `notFound()` for an unknown id, otherwise resolves the owner (`getUserById`), the linked campaign if any (`getCampaign`), and the current temp-pilot if any (`getUserById` again), then renders a small print:hidden action bar (a back-link to the owner's `/players/[id]`, a `PrintButton` client component calling `window.print()`, and the download link) above the reused `CharacterSummary`. Two small follow-on fixes once this existed: `CharacterSummary` itself gained the "View / print character sheet" link (so it's discoverable from every existing place a character renders, not just a new page nobody would find), which needed a `hideSheetLink` prop (so the sheet page itself doesn't render a link back to itself -- the same "omit when redundant here" pattern `linkedCampaign`'s own doc comment already established for the campaign page) and its own `print:hidden` (it's app navigation, not character content, so it has no business appearing in a printed sheet even on a page where it's shown). The root `app/layout.tsx` also gained a `print:hidden` wrapper around `NavBar` and `print:max-w-none print:p-0` on `<main>`, so printing a character sheet doesn't also print the nav bar and page chrome around it -- Tailwind v4's `print:` variant needed no config changes.

Testing: new `__tests__/unit/characterExport.test.ts` (10 tests) covering `buildCharacterSheetText()` directly -- see the Testing section above for the full list. No new tests needed in `characters.test.ts`/`access.test.ts` since no access-checking or DB-write logic was added; the export path is pure formatting over data those files already cover. 476 -> 486 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (confirmed `/characters/[id]` and `/api/characters/[id]/export` in the route manifest), and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

2026-09-08 (live session, continued): backlog item #50, self-service account data export (self-identified), is DONE. The numbered backlog (#1-49) is fully done/phased/blocked, so this increment again followed the task's own "backlog runs dry" fallback -- this time from the seed list the task's own prompt suggested (sub-request sort/hide, board search, an accessibility pass, campaign filter-UI cleanup, data export/account deletion), picking "data export/account deletion" since it was explicitly called out as "a real gap for a production app with real user data, even if low-drama," and this session has arguably the freshest whole-app view of anyone who's worked on this repo today.

Before writing any code, read `lib/db.ts` in full to survey every table this app has (roughly 25 at this point) and confirm which ones trace back to a single user account. That survey settled the scope question up front: `campaigns.dm_id` is `NOT NULL`, and a DM's campaign can have an active party depending on it (roster, schedule, table chat, resources, sub requests all hang off it) -- there's no way to hard-delete a user's account without deciding what happens to every campaign they DM (cancel it? transfer it to someone else? leave it orphaned with a dead `dm_id`?), and that's a real product decision, not a mechanical delete. Rather than either skip the whole item or make that call unilaterally, the scope was narrowed to just the export half, with an explicit, visible note on the settings page itself explaining why deletion isn't offered yet and pointing to contacting the site owner -- the same "flag it, don't silently decide it" treatment this session's own instructions apply to items #23/#24 (payment/App Store) and #6c (email/SMTP).

Design: `lib/accountExport.ts`'s `getAccountExport(userId)` is the one new piece of business logic, deliberately shaped like `lib/stats.ts`'s `getUserStats()` (a DB-touching aggregator with no access-control logic of its own) rather than like `lib/calendarExport.ts`/`lib/characterExport.ts` (pure, DB-free formatters) -- unlike those two exports, which format one already-fetched record, this one *is* the fetch, across roughly twenty tables. It deliberately takes no viewer/access parameter beyond `userId` itself: every other `lib/*.ts` function that reads another user's data takes some access-check input, but this one is designed so it can only ever be called with the requesting user's own id (`app/api/account/export/route.ts` passes `requireUser()`'s own `auth.user.id` and nothing else), so there's no access boundary to get wrong.

Field-by-field sourcing, in the order they were worked through against `lib/db.ts`: account (id/displayName/email/isAdmin/createdAt -- explicitly selected columns, never `SELECT *`, so `password_hash` can never end up in the export the same way `lib/auth.ts`'s `getUserById()` already guards against it leaking anywhere else), profile (`getProfile()`), weekly availability (`getAvailabilitySlots()`), notification preferences (`getPreferences()`), characters (`listCharactersForUser()`), campaigns DMed (raw query + `rowToCampaign()`, reused directly from `lib/campaigns.ts` rather than re-implementing the `tone_tags` JSON-parse-on-read step), every membership row across every status -- not just approved, unlike `lib/profiles.ts`'s `myCampaigns()`, since a full data export should include a request that was declined too -- enriched with the campaign's own title via a join so the export is readable without cross-referencing ids by hand, sub requests posted, sub volunteering, sub placements (queried from both the volunteer side and the owner side, since the character owner is always the request's own requester per `lib/subRequests.ts`'s own validation, so there's no separate "placements as owner" table to query), direct messages sent and received, campaign chat messages sent, ratings given and received in both directions plus campaign ratings given (all three needed a small local `parseTags()` helper duplicating the JSON-parse-on-read pattern `lib/ratings.ts`/`lib/campaignRatings.ts` each already have privately as an unexported `rowToRating()` -- duplicated rather than exported, since pulling a private helper out of two otherwise-unrelated files for one call site each wasn't worth the coupling), session log entries authored, session log kudos given, notifications, following and followers (each enriched with the other user's display name via a join), board threads/replies authored and board reports filed, public feed events where the user is the actor (included for completeness even though they're already fully public elsewhere, per `feed_events`' own documented privacy boundary), session RSVPs, DM-recorded attendance rows *about* this user (data the app holds about them, even though a DM authored it), campaign resources uploaded (including the actual `data_url` file content, not just metadata -- a real data export should hand back files the user actually uploaded, not merely describe them), and DM-only prep tools (initiative entries, NPC notes) for campaigns the user DMs, since those are the user's own authored content even though no one else ever sees them. `stats: getUserStats(userId)` closes it out, reusing the existing dashboard stats wholesale rather than re-deriving anything.

`GET /api/account/export` (`app/api/account/export/route.ts`) is a thin route: `requireUser()`, call `getAccountExport()`, `JSON.stringify(data, null, 2)`, and return it as a real file download (`Content-Type: application/json`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="session-zero-data-export-<date>.json"`) -- the third non-JSON-API-response route in this codebase after backlog #46's `.ics` export and #49's plain-text character sheet, though this one is a bit different in kind: the *content* is JSON, but it's still meant to be saved as a file and read by a human, not consumed by this app's own client code, so the download-attachment treatment still applies.

UI: a new `/settings/account` page ("Your data") is a server component (mirroring `app/admin/boards/page.tsx`'s own inline-gate-with-rendered-content convention for a settings-style page that needs sign-in, rather than a client-side redirect) with a "Download my data (.json)" link (a plain anchor to the GET route, no confirmation needed since it has no side effect -- the same reasoning backlog #46's calendar-export link already established) and a second card explaining, in plain language, why account deletion isn't offered yet. It's cross-linked with the existing `/settings/notifications` page via a small two-item nav at the top of each (a lightweight, minimal version of what backlog item #10's own longstanding note suggested -- "should `/settings/notifications` become a tab within a broader `/settings` section" -- without attempting a full settings-section restructure, which felt like more scope than this one item justified). `/profile` also gained a plain "Download your data" link right under its own heading, since that's the account-management surface a user is already most likely to be on.

Judgment call, stated explicitly both in code comments and on the page itself: account deletion is NOT built, even partially, alongside the export. It would have been easy to add a "delete my account" button that just deletes the `users` row -- but with `campaigns.dm_id NOT NULL` and no cascade behavior decided for a DM's own active campaigns, that button would either throw a foreign-key error the moment it was clicked (if campaigns exist) or silently leave dangling references (if the FK constraint were loosened to allow it), neither of which is a real feature. This mirrors how backlog #23 (payment processing) and #24 (App Store distribution) are both explicitly flagged rather than attempted -- a real business/design decision outside this session's authority, not a coding gap.

**Pitfall hit and caught mid-session, worth flagging for future sessions since the task's own instructions call this out as "hit repeatedly today":** the first pass at every file in this item (the `lib/types.ts` addition, `lib/accountExport.ts`, the API route, both settings pages, the `/profile` edit, and the initial test file) was written using the Edit/Write tools, which operate on the *host-mounted* path (`C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo`), not the scratch clone inside the `mcp__workspace__bash` sandbox (`~/build/session-zero`) that `npm test`/`npx tsc`/`npm run build` actually run against. This wasn't caught by the very-first-edit `npx tsc --noEmit` re-check the setup instructions call for, because that check ran clean -- but only because it was silently checking the *old, unmodified* scratch clone (the new files simply didn't exist there yet, so there was nothing to fail on). The actual tell was `npx vitest run accountExport.test.ts` reporting "No test files found" despite the file clearly existing (confirmed via `cat` against the host path) -- at which point `ls lib/accountExport.ts` inside the scratch clone came back empty, confirming the mismatch. Recovery: every file was rewritten from scratch directly in the scratch clone via `mcp__workspace__bash` heredocs/`python3`, matching this session's own required workflow; nothing from the mis-targeted Edit/Write attempts was reused except as a reference for what to retype. Lesson for next time, beyond just re-reading the pitfall warning: the very-first-edit sanity check needs to verify the edit actually *landed* in the scratch clone (e.g. `grep` for a distinctive new string, not just "does tsc still pass"), since a no-op edit against the wrong filesystem passes every check trivially.

Testing: see the Testing section above for the full breakdown (17 new tests in a new `accountExport.test.ts`). 486 -> 503 tests, all green. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (confirmed `/api/account/export` and `/settings/account` in the route manifest), and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db, entire sequence run inside one `mcp__workspace__bash` call per this sandbox's own backgrounded-process constraint (confirmed the hard way again this session: a server started with `&` in one bash call is gone by the next call, since each `mcp__workspace__bash` invocation is an independent shell with no process carryover): confirmed a signed-out `GET /api/account/export` 401s; signed up a DM and a player; had the DM create a campaign and the player join, get approved, and send the DM a direct message; downloaded the player's own export and confirmed the response headers (`Content-Type: application/json; charset=utf-8`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="session-zero-data-export-2026-09-08.json"`) and body (their own `approved` membership correctly enriched with the campaign's title, the direct message they sent, and `stats.campaignsAsPlayer: 1`) were all correct, plus a `password_hash` string-search across the entire serialized export coming back empty; downloaded the DM's own export separately and confirmed the campaign appeared under `campaignsAsDm` and the received message appeared under `directMessagesReceived`; and confirmed `/settings/account` renders "Sign in required." when signed out and the full download link plus the account-deletion explanation when signed in. Server process and temp db/cookie/response files were all cleaned up afterward.

No changes to email/SMTP (#6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (#3, still a human call). The numbered backlog (#1-49) plus this session's own #50 are now all done, phased, or blocked on a decision outside this session's authority -- the next increment should again pick from this task's own remaining seed candidates (sub-request sort/hide, board search, an accessibility pass, campaign filter-UI cleanup) or a freshly self-identified gap, per this same fallback process.


2026-09-08 (live session, continued): backlog item #51 (sub-request pool sort/filter, self-identified) is DONE. The numbered backlog (#1-50) was fully done/phased/blocked, so this increment picked from this task's own remaining seed candidates -- specifically the one named directly in the prompt's own seed list: "with `needed_at` now structured (#29), does `/subs` sort/filter well as the pool grows (e.g. hide past-due by default, sort by soonest-needed)?" Answer, confirmed by reading the code before writing any: no. `listOpenSubRequests()` and `GET /api/subs` had no sort or filter parameters at all -- every open request across every campaign, regardless of campaign, was returned in a single fixed `ORDER BY created_at DESC` (newest posted first), and `neededAtStatus` (unscheduled/upcoming/past-due, already computed per-row since backlog #29) was purely decorative -- displayed on each card but never used to order or hide anything. Design: `listOpenSubRequests(viewerId, options?)` gained a `{ sort?: "soonest" | "newest"; includePastDue?: boolean }` options bag. `sort` defaults to `"soonest"` -- a SQL `ORDER BY (needed_at IS NULL) ASC, needed_at ASC, created_at DESC, rowid DESC` -- so a request with a specific date sorts by how soon that date is, and a request with no date at all (no urgency signal to sort by) sorts after every dated one, itself newest-posted-first. `includePastDue` defaults to `false`, excluding any request whose `needed_at` has already passed via a `WHERE needed_at IS NULL OR needed_at >= ?` clause -- deliberately careful to never call an *unscheduled* request past-due, matching `computeScheduleStatus()`'s own three-way classification rather than collapsing it to a boolean. Judgment call on where the new default lives: the lib function's own default is the improved one (soonest-first, past-due hidden), not just the page/route's default -- so any future direct caller of `listOpenSubRequests(viewerId)` with no options gets the more useful behavior automatically, rather than the old "newest, everything included" order lurking as a trap for a caller who doesn't know to ask for the new behavior explicitly. This meant updating one pre-existing test (`subRequests.test.ts`'s `neededAtStatus`-classification test) to pass `{ includePastDue: true }` explicitly, since it was relying on seeing a past-due row by default -- a reasonable adjustment since that test's actual point (verifying the three-way status classification) is orthogonal to the pool's new default filtering, not something the new feature broke. `GET /api/subs` gained `?sort=` and `?includePastDue=` query params, parsed with the same plain-parse-with-silent-fallback convention `GET /api/campaigns` already established (an unrecognized `sort` value is ignored rather than triggering a 400 -- list ordering is a display nicety, not a correctness gate worth failing a whole request over). `/subs` gained a `<form method="get">` filter bar (a sort `<select>` plus a "Show past-due requests" checkbox) directly above the list, matching `/campaigns`' own filter-form convention exactly, plus a link in the empty-state message ("Past-due requests are hidden by default -- show them") so an empty-looking pool with hidden past-due requests doesn't read as "nothing has ever been posted here." Scope cut: the campaign-page panel (`SubRequestPanel.tsx`, backed by `listSubRequestsForCampaign()`) was deliberately left untouched -- a single campaign's own sub-request list is bounded by that one campaign's own history, nowhere near large enough to need sort/filter controls the way the pool spanning every campaign on the site does. The seed question's own "as the pool grows" framing pointed specifically at the app-wide pool, not the per-campaign view. **Mid-session process incident, the exact pitfall flagged at the top of this task's own instructions (and already documented once below, in backlog #50's own log entry): the first four `Edit` tool calls for this item's code (`lib/subRequests.ts`, `app/api/subs/route.ts`, `app/subs/page.tsx`, and the test file) landed on the mounted host path (`C:\Users\billb\Session-zero\repo\...`) rather than the scratch clone the sandbox's `mcp__workspace__bash` tool actually runs `tsc`/`vitest`/`next build` against.** Caught immediately, before wasting any time debugging a phantom test failure: rather than trusting that `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vitest run` coming back clean meant the edits had landed (they didn't fail -- they just ran against the *unedited* scratch-clone files, which were also internally consistent and therefore passed trivially), a `grep` for a distinctive new string (`"backlog #51"`) against the scratch clone's copies of those same files came back empty, immediately proving the mismatch. Recovery: read the final edited content back from the host path (already correct, since `Edit`/`Read` against a mounted path do work, just not on the filesystem the test tooling uses) and rewrote each file from scratch directly into the scratch clone via `mcp__workspace__bash` heredocs (quoted delimiters, since the content itself contains backticks and `${}` template-literal syntax that would otherwise be shell-expanded). After the rewrite, `tsc`, the targeted test file, the full suite, `next build`, and `next lint` were all re-run from a clean state to confirm the *actual* edits were in place and everything was still green -- see the Testing section update above for the final counts. This is now the second session in a row to hit this exact mistake even with the warning present at the top of the task instructions both times -- worth flagging plainly for whoever reads this file next: the instructions' own suggested mitigation ("re-run tests right after your very first edit to catch this early") is necessary but not sufficient, because a no-op edit against the wrong filesystem still leaves the scratch clone internally consistent, so `tsc`/tests/build all still pass -- the only reliable tripwire is grepping the scratch clone itself for a string that only exists in the edit you just made. 503 -> 507 tests, all green (`__tests__/unit/subRequests.test.ts` gained a new `describe("sub-request pool sort/filter (backlog #51)")` block, 4 tests: past-due hidden by default but included with `includePastDue: true`; an unscheduled request never classified as past-due or excluded by the default filter; the default soonest-needed-first order correctly placing a sooner-dated request before a later-dated one before an undated one; and `sort: "newest"` reverting to newest-posted-first, ignoring `needed_at` entirely). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (no route-manifest changes expected or found -- `/subs` and `/api/subs` already existed, only their internals changed), and `npm run lint` all pass clean. Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db, the entire sequence run inside one `mcp__workspace__bash` call per this sandbox's own well-established backgrounded-process constraint (a background server started with `&` in one bash call does not survive into the next call -- confirmed the hard way again early in this smoke test, when an initial attempt with only a 1-second startup wait came back with every subsequent curl failing to connect; fixed by polling with a proper up-to-40-second retry loop before proceeding): signed up a DM, created a campaign, and posted four sub requests via the real HTTP API -- one needed 2 hours ago (past-due), one needed in 30 minutes, one needed in 5 hours, and one with no date given at all. Confirmed the default `GET /api/subs` returned exactly the 30-minute, 5-hour, and undated requests in that order (soonest dated first, undated last), with the past-due one excluded entirely. Confirmed `?includePastDue=true` returned all four, with the past-due request correctly sorted first among the dated ones (it's chronologically the soonest `needed_at`, regardless of having already passed). Confirmed `?sort=newest` returned the undated/5-hour/30-minute requests in reverse-creation order (still excluding the past-due one by default). Confirmed the rendered `/subs` page HTML included the sort `<select>` ("Soonest needed"/"Newest posted" options) and the "Show past-due requests" checkbox, showed exactly the three non-past-due notes by default, and showed the past-due note only when `?includePastDue=true` was appended to the URL. Server process and every temp db/cookie/response file were cleaned up afterward. No changes to email/SMTP (backlog #6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (backlog #3, still a human call). The numbered backlog (#1-50) plus this session's own #51 are now all done, phased, or blocked on a decision outside this session's authority -- the next increment should again pick from this task's own remaining seed candidates (board search across the four boards, a real accessibility pass, campaign filter-UI cleanup/saved-search convenience) or a freshly self-identified gap, per this same fallback process.

2026-09-08 (live session, continued): backlog item #52 (cross-board search, self-identified) is DONE. The numbered backlog (#1-51) was fully done/phased/blocked, so this increment picked from this task's own remaining seed candidates -- specifically the first one the prompt named directly: "as the four boards (#37) accumulate threads, is there a way to search across them?" Answer, confirmed by reading `lib/boards.ts` and every `app/boards/**`/`app/api/boards/**` file before writing anything: no -- `/boards` lists the four curated boards with a thread count each, `/boards/[slug]` lists that one board's own threads newest-first paginated, and that's the entire browsing surface. There was no query across boards, no query across a board's own thread bodies beyond scrolling pages by hand, and no way at all to search inside a reply (a reply has no page of its own -- it only exists inside its parent thread's page). Design: `searchBoards(q, opts?)` in `lib/boards.ts` mirrors `lib/campaigns.ts`'s `listCampaigns()` `q` keyword-search convention as closely as possible rather than inventing a new shape -- case-insensitive substring match via `LOWER(...) LIKE ... ESCAPE '\'`, the query string run through the same exported `escapeLikePattern()` `campaigns.ts` already uses (imported directly, no circular dependency since `campaigns.ts` doesn't import from `boards.ts`), and the identical `{ items, total }` page/pageSize pagination shape every other list function in this app already returns. The one real addition: the WHERE clause also matches via a correlated `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM board_replies br WHERE br.thread_id = bt.id AND LOWER(br.body) LIKE @q ESCAPE '\')` subquery, so a query that only appears inside a reply -- not the thread's own title or body -- still surfaces that thread, since the reply itself has nowhere else to point a searcher. Each result carries a new `matchedInThread: boolean` field (computed in JS after the query returns, by checking whether the query string is actually present in the row's own title/body -- simpler than threading a second SQL CASE expression through the query) so the UI can tell a searcher *why* a thread with no visible match in its own text still showed up: "Matched in a reply on this thread, not the original post." A new `GET /api/boards/search` route (public, no `requireUser()` -- matches every other board-browsing route's ungated-read convention, and an empty `?q=` isn't a 400, it's just zero results, the same "degrade gracefully rather than error on a display-only filter" shape `GET /api/subs`'s `?sort=` already established) and a new `/boards/search` page (its own `<form method="get">` search box plus a paginated result list, following `/boards/[slug]`'s own pagination-link pattern exactly) serve it. `/boards`' index page gained a "Search boards" link in its header. Judgment call on scope: `searchBoards()` and the API route both accept an optional `boardSlug` to scope a search to one board (harmless to add now, and a natural fit for a future per-board search box), but no UI control for it exists on `/boards/search` in this pass -- the seed's own framing was specifically about searching *across* boards, which a single global search box already covers directly; scoping to one board is a smaller, different feature (closer to `/boards/[slug]`'s own existing thread list with a filter) that this pass didn't try to also solve. An empty or whitespace-only query deliberately returns `{ items: [], total: 0 }` rather than every thread on every board -- `searchBoards("")` short-circuits before touching the database at all -- since this is meant to be a search, not a second "browse everything" view that duplicates what `/boards` and each board's own page already do. 507 -> 516 tests, all green (`__tests__/unit/boards.test.ts` gained a new `describe("board search (backlog #52)")` block, 9 tests -- see the Testing section above for the full list). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (confirmed `/api/boards/search` and `/boards/search` in the route manifest), and `npm run lint` all pass clean. Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with a fresh SQLite db, the entire sequence run inside one `mcp__workspace__bash` call per this sandbox's own well-established backgrounded-process constraint (a background server does not survive past the end of the bash call that started it): signed up a user and posted three threads via the real HTTP API, one on each of three different boards -- one with a distinctive keyword ("Zorblatt") only in its own title, one with a distinctive keyword ("Quixolane") only in its own body, and one with no distinctive keyword anywhere in its own text but a reply posted afterward containing one ("Wibbleforge"). Confirmed a search for "zorblatt" and a search for "Quixolane" each returned exactly the right single thread with `matchedInThread: true`; confirmed a search for "wibbleforge" returned the third thread (its own title/body have no match) with `matchedInThread: false`; confirmed an empty `?q=` and a query matching nothing at all both returned `{ items: [], total: 0 }`; confirmed `?boardSlug=` scoped to the wrong board correctly excluded an otherwise-matching thread; and confirmed the rendered `/boards/search` HTML showed the matched thread's title, board name, and (for the reply-only case) the "Matched in a reply on this thread, not the original post." note, and that `/boards` itself now renders a working "Search boards" link. Server process and every temp db/cookie file were cleaned up afterward. No changes to email/SMTP (backlog #6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (backlog #3, still a human call). The numbered backlog (#1-51) plus this session's own #52 are now all done, phased, or blocked on a decision outside this session's authority -- the next increment should again pick from this task's own remaining seed candidates (a real accessibility pass, campaign filter-UI cleanup, an "in-person near you" homepage surface) or a freshly self-identified gap, per this same fallback process.


2026-09-08 (live session, continued): backlog item #53 (accessibility pass)
The numbered backlog (#1-52) was fully done/phased/blocked, so this increment worked through this task's
own list of repeatedly-offered-but-never-picked seed candidates and picked the accessibility pass -- flagged
in the task's own framing as overdue precisely because it kept appearing as an option across several prior
increments without ever being chosen. Scope, per the task's own instructions: a fix-what's-there pass across
existing markup/JSX/classes, not a new-feature or restyling pass.

Audit method: read every component under `components/` and `app/` (54 `.tsx` files), cross-referencing
against the two conventions the task pointed at as "already correct" (the star-rating `aria-label`s in
`RatingsPanel.tsx`/`CampaignRatingPanel.tsx`, and the availability grid's `aria-pressed`/`aria-label`s in
`AvailabilityGrid.tsx`) -- and found the first of those two reference examples was itself incomplete (see
below), which reinforced doing a systematic pass rather than trusting spot-checks.

Findings and fixes:
* **No `focus-visible` styling existed anywhere in the app** (confirmed via a full-codebase grep for
  `focus-visible`/`outline-none`, zero hits either way -- so the app was relying entirely on each browser's
  own default focus outline, present but not deliberately styled or guaranteed consistent). Since this app
  has no shared Button/Input component (every button/link/input is styled ad hoc inline across dozens of
  files), hand-adding a Tailwind `focus-visible:` class file-by-file would be a much larger and more
  error-prone change than the fix warrants. Added one global rule to `app/globals.css` instead: a
  `:focus-visible` selector (not plain `:focus`, so a mouse click never shows the ring, only real
  keyboard/assistive-tech navigation) covering `a`, `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea`, and `[tabindex]`,
  with a light/dark-mode-aware outline color. One file, reaches every current and future interactive element.
* **Star-rating buttons** (`RatingsPanel.tsx`, `CampaignRatingPanel.tsx`): the `role="radiogroup"` container
  had `aria-label="Stars"`, but each individual star `<button>`'s own accessible name was just the glyph
  "★" -- a screen reader user had no way to tell which of the five identical buttons was "3 stars" vs.
  "5 stars". Added a per-button `aria-label` (`` `${n} star${n === 1 ? "" : "s"}` ``) to both files.
* **Session-log kudos button** (`SessionLogPanel.tsx`): had `aria-pressed` and a `title` attribute but no
  `aria-label`; since the button's own text content ("👏 {count}") already supplies an accessible name, the
  `title` was never actually surfaced to assistive tech. Added an `aria-label` stating the action and count
  explicitly ("Give kudos (3 given)" / "Remove kudos (3 given)").
* **Initiative tracker move buttons** (`InitiativeTrackerPanel.tsx`): already had `aria-label`s ("Move up" /
  "Move down") but they were generic, giving no way to tell which combatant a screen-reader user tabbing
  through a list of several would be reordering. Made them per-entry: `` `Move ${entry.name} up` ``.
* **Discover swipe deck** (`DiscoverDeck.tsx`): audited specifically for keyboard operability per the task's
  own framing, since it's the most visually distinctive part of the app and the most plausible place for a
  mouse-only interaction to have crept in. Found it already fully keyboard-operable -- "Pass" and "Start
  over" are real `<button>` elements, "View & join" is a real `<Link>`, and there is no drag/touch-only
  gesture underneath the "swipe" framing (confirmed via a grep for `onTouchStart`/`onPointerDown`/`onDrag`
  across the codebase, zero hits). No changes needed beyond the new global focus ring.
* **Unlabeled form fields**: a systematic pass over all 22 files containing `<input>`/`<textarea>`/`<select>`
  found roughly two dozen fields that were either placeholder-only or had no text at all, with no real
  `<label>` or `aria-label` -- adjacent visible text (e.g. "Or upload a picture" next to a file input in
  `CharacterManager.tsx`) doesn't programmatically associate with a control the way a `<label>` or
  `aria-label` does, exactly the gap the task called out. Fixed with an `aria-label` matching each field's
  placeholder/context in: `CampaignChatPanel.tsx` (table-chat message), `ConversationView.tsx` (DM message),
  `NewThreadForm.tsx` (thread title + body), `ReplyForm.tsx` (reply body), `ReportPostButton.tsx` (report
  reason), `PartyNotesPanel.tsx` (notes textarea, which had no placeholder at all), `ResourceVaultPanel.tsx`
  (both its add-form and inline edit-form name/description fields), `NpcNotesPanel.tsx` (same, add-form and
  edit-form), `SubRequestPanel.tsx` (the note, guardrails, and volunteer-message fields),
  `SubVolunteerAction.tsx` (volunteer message), `InitiativeTrackerPanel.tsx`'s inline edit-mode fields
  (name/initiative/HP/notes), `SessionLogPanel.tsx`'s new-entry and edit-entry textareas, and
  `CharacterManager.tsx`'s portrait-upload file input. Every other form audited (campaign create/edit,
  sign-in, `/profile`, the browse-page filter form, character-creation fields) was already correctly using
  real `<label>` wrapping (label text + input as DOM children, which associates implicitly) and needed no
  changes -- confirmed by reading each flagged file's actual surrounding markup rather than trusting a
  proximity heuristic, which produced false positives on a first-pass grep.
* **Notification-settings checkboxes** (`app/settings/notifications/page.tsx`): each row's checkbox had an
  accessible name of just "On"/"Muted" (that text was inside the `<label>`; the actual notification-type name
  lived in a separate sibling `<span>`, outside the label). A screen reader tabbing through the list of
  notification types had no way to tell which checkbox controlled which type. Added `aria-label={row.label}`.
* **NavBar unread-count badges** (`components/NavBar.tsx`): the three live badges (messages, notifications,
  table chat) render as a bare number inside a `<span>`, with only the parent link's own text ("Messages",
  "Notifications") for context. Added a `` `${count} unread` `` `aria-label` to each badge span.
* **Heading hierarchy / landmark structure**: grepped every `<h1>`-`<h4>` across the whole codebase and
  reviewed `/campaigns`, a campaign detail page, `/profile`, `/players/[id]`, `/boards`, and several other
  pages. Found a consistently sane structure already in place -- exactly one `<h1>` per page (a couple of
  files have two `<h1>` literals, but always in mutually-exclusive early-return branches, e.g. `/feed`'s
  signed-out vs. signed-in render, so only one ever reaches the DOM), `<h2>` siblings for each feature panel
  on the campaign detail page (`Section`-wrapped components each own their own `<h2>`), and `<h3>` used only
  for genuine sub-groupings nested under a `<h2>` (e.g. `/profile`'s "Running (as DM)" / "Playing in" under
  its own "My campaigns" `<h2>`). No skipped levels, no changes needed.
* Also confirmed there are no non-semantic clickable elements anywhere (grepped for `onClick` on `<div>`,
  `<span>`, `<li>`, `<p>`) -- every click handler in this app is already on a real `<button>` or `<Link>`, so
  there's no keyboard-trap risk of that kind to begin with.

516 tests, unchanged -- this is a presentational/markup-only pass with no new library-level logic, the same
convention backlog #21's own UI-only Discover-deck pass already established. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run
build`, and `npm run lint` all pass clean.

Manually smoke-tested against a real `next start` server on a spare port with fresh SQLite dbs across several
`mcp__workspace__bash` calls (each self-contained, per this sandbox's own well-established constraint that a
backgrounded server process does not survive past the end of the bash call that started it): confirmed via
actual rendered HTML that the new `aria-label`s render correctly and *dynamically* -- e.g. after a player gave
kudos to a DM's session-log entry, the DM's own view of that entry showed `aria-label="Give kudos (1 given)"`
with `aria-pressed="false"`, while the player who'd actually given it saw `aria-label="Remove kudos (1
given)"` with `aria-pressed="true"` on the exact same entry -- along with the table-chat message box, NPC
quick-notes, resource-vault, and portrait-upload labels all present verbatim in the served HTML. Some of the
newer labels (initiative-tracker inline edit fields, notification-settings checkboxes, the live NavBar badges,
the rating-stars editing view) sit behind this app's existing client-side fetch-then-render pattern for those
components and can't be observed from a plain `curl` fetch of the initial SSR shell -- a pre-existing
architectural fact about how this app's client components already work, not something this pass introduced or
could route around without adding a browser-automation dependency this project doesn't have. Those were
instead verified by confirming each edit was a unique, successfully-applied string replacement (a Python
patch script asserted exactly one match per replacement before writing) plus the clean `tsc`/build/lint pass.

Also hit and fixed an environment issue unrelated to any code change while getting to a clean starting
baseline: roughly 4GB of leftover per-test SQLite databases (`data/test-*.db*`, `.gitignore`d, apparently never
cleaned up across several recent live-session increments) had filled the sandbox's `/sessions` disk to 100%
used, causing `ENOSPC` write failures partway through the very first `npm test` run of this session -- nothing
to do with this session's own changes. Deleted them (`rm data/test-*.db*`), which freed about 4.1GB and let
the full suite run clean; re-deleted the newly-created ones after each subsequent test/smoke run to avoid
leaving the same mess for the next session. Disk sat at 55% used afterward, comfortably clear.

No changes to email/SMTP (backlog #6c, still blocked) or the deployment decision (backlog #3, still a human
call). The numbered backlog (#1-52) plus this session's own #53 are now all done, phased, or blocked on a
decision outside this session's authority -- the next increment should self-identify a fresh gap, since every
seed candidate this task's own prompt has offered across recent increments (board search, sub-request
sort/filter, an accessibility pass) has now been picked up.

Picked backlog item #54 (home page introduction) -- the lowest-numbered queued, unblocked item after #1-53 closed out and #55-59 were added to the backlog as owner-reviewed but not-yet-built. (Noted for the owner: backlog #34's phase 2 -- a cross-community resource library -- technically lost its stated blocker once #36 shipped, but has never been re-flagged as a candidate by a later session; worth a human look if strict numeric priority matters more than this file's own stated queue order. Not picked tonight since #54 was the owner's own most recently added, explicitly-scoped request.)

Added a "What is Session Zero?" section to the landing page (`app/page.tsx`), shown only when there's no signed-in user -- a short paragraph on the matchmaking + campaign-tracking model, a three-item "Find a table / Run your own / Keep it going" grid (styled consistently with the existing `/systems` card convention: `rounded border border-black/10 p-4`), and a closing line making the in-person-first framing explicit ("a tool for organizing in-person-first play, not a place to play the game itself"), directly answering the backlog line's two asks (what the site does, and the in-person-first community goal behind it). Judgment call: gated the whole section on `!user` rather than showing it unconditionally -- the backlog line's own phrasing ("for a first-time or signed-out visitor") reads as scoped to people who don't already know the site, and a signed-in user returning to `/` gets no value from re-reading an explainer above their usual Browse-campaigns shortcut. No new schema, no new route, no new library function -- pure JSX/copy, the same kind of presentational-only change backlog #53's accessibility pass and #21's Discover deck already established a "no new tests needed" convention for.
